
# A1 — orchestrator API layer  (findings kept: 23, refuted: 0, bloat_est: 1100)

The API layer works, but it has two production bugs that matter more than its size. First, synchronous model and REPL calls run directly on the uvicorn event loop in production paths. These include the cheap-first call in /chat, the legacy /chat/stream path that production actually serves, and the non-streaming and REPL modes of /v1. An AST scan found 64 blocking calls inside async handlers. With 6 workers, one generation freezes /health, dashboard SSE and client-disconnect detection for every request on that worker. Second, /chat shares one LLMPrimitives instance across concurrent requests and calls reset_counters() and sets cache_prompt on it per request, which corrupts the per-request token counts that AutoPilot's eval_tower reads. Request-path performance suffers from a 107 KB stack_priors.yaml re-parsed with pure-Python yaml.safe_load (285 ms per parse measured) at least twice per /chat. Each dashboard snapshot tick, per connected client and on the event loop, parses the whole day's progress JSONL four times (one result is thrown away), runs ps three times and re-parses contention_matrix.yaml 2-8 times. Structurally the costs are the 1006-line openai_chat_completions, which implements every mode twice and pastes its SSE error mapping four times (Retry-After and error body already differ between streaming and non-streaming), two full /chat/stream implementations that are 87% identical (production runs the 'legacy' one), the 8.2k-line dashboard.py with 300-750-line payload builders, and 13 scripts.* modules imported by the API, including private functions of an analysis CLI. Roughly 1,100 LOC can be removed or merged: about 370 from the duplicate stream path, about 300 from the openai_compat dedupe, about 200 of proven-dead code, about 150 of copied gate and bridge helpers, and about 60 of DI providers that only tests use.

## A1-01 [sev 5] [performance/bug] [effort M] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=20
**Blocking LLM/REPL calls run directly on the uvicorn event loop in production request paths**
files: O:src/api/routes/chat.py:562, O:src/api/routes/chat.py:1495, O:src/api/routes/chat.py:1526, O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:2033, O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:2051, O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:2102, O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:1741, O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:1552
evidence: An AST scan (scratchpad a1_async_block.py, which walks async bodies and does not count to_thread args) found 64 blocking calls inside async defs in src/api. Production-active examples: (1) `_try_cheap_first` calls `primitives.llm_call(...)` synchronously at chat.py:562; cheap-first defaults to on (src/config/__init__.py:277,600 `try_cheap_first_enabled=True`). (2) The legacy /chat/stream `generate()` calls `primitives.llm_call` (chat.py:1495) and `repl.execute` (1526). It is the path production serves because the launcher forces `unified_streaming: False` (orchestrator_stack.py:2365). (3) Non-streaming /v1/chat/completions runs `_run_client_tool_completion` (2033), `primitives.llm_call` (2051, 2102) and `_execute_repl_turn` (2122) inline. Streaming REPL mode calls llm_call inline at 1741. When `can_stream_model_text` is False, client and direct mode also call inline (1552, 1654). (4) `_run_two_stage_summarization` calls llm_call(n_tokens=4096) at chat_summarization.py:289. The same file offloads `_execute_direct/_execute_react/_execute_delegated` with asyncio.to_thread (chat.py:1054,1069,1246). proactive_stage.py:268 states the rule outright: 'Keep model I/O off the event loop in production runtime paths'. ruff ASYNC confirms the direct cases: 23 hits (ASYNC221 subprocess at dashboard.py:2755,3135; 9x ASYNC230 open). Cost: uvicorn runs 6 workers (orchestrator_stack.py:2631), so each in-flight generation freezes 1/6 of the API. /health, dashboard SSE frames and /chat/reward on that worker stall for the whole generation. The `_watch_disconnect` task (chat.py:256) cannot run, so client cancellation is not seen until the call returns.
proposal: Route every model or REPL call in async code through a thread. Non-streaming: `await asyncio.to_thread(primitives.llm_call, ...)`, `await asyncio.to_thread(_execute_repl_turn, repl, code)`, `await asyncio.to_thread(_run_client_tool_completion, ...)`. Streaming: reuse `_iter_thread_call_chunks` (openai_compat.py:85) for whole calls too, so chat.py:1495/1526 and stream_adapter.py:216/250 become `async for kind, value in bridge(...)`. Wrap `_route_request`, `_plan_review_gate` (which calls ArchitectReviewService.review_plan, an LLM call, whenever plan_review is on) and `_init_primitives` in to_thread at chat.py:809-875. Add a ruff `ASYNC` selection to `make gates` and keep the AST scanner as a test that fails on new blocking names inside async defs.
CORRECTED: Same fix, prioritized by traffic. (1) Move `_route_request` (embedder HTTP + FAISS), `_init_primitives` and the cheap-first llm_call in /chat into asyncio.to_thread, which copies contextvars so request_context still applies. (2) In /v1, offload every non-streaming call and the REPL turns. (3) Fix the AdmissionController docstring. Make the AST scanner skip the `_should_inline_plan_call_for_test` branch and lifespan so its gate is 0 false positives.
verify-note: Checked each site first-hand. chat.py:562: `primitives.llm_call(...)` runs inline inside `async def _try_cheap_first` (401). Cheap-first defaults on (config/__init__.py:600 `_env_bool(...CHAT_TRY_CHEAP_FIRST_ENABLED, True)`), and grepping all three repos finds no override. openai_compat.py has the same pattern: 2033 `_run_client_tool_completion`, 2051/2102 `llm_call` and 2122 `_execute_repl_turn` inline in the non-streaming path, and 1552/1654/1741 inline in streaming. Also inline: stream_adapter.py:216, and chat_summarization.py:289, which sits inside `async def _run_two_stage_summarization` (151) and is awaited from repl_executor.py:536. `ruff check src/api --select ASYNC` gives exactly 23 hits, including ASYNC221 at dashboard.py:2755 and 3135 and nine ASYNC230. The finder's scanner gives TOTAL 64 on re-run, but about 3 are not production (proactive_stage.py:264 is the pytest-only branch, plus 2 in lifespan). Workers default to 6 (orchestrator_stack.py:2631). Two things the finder did not name make this worse. First, `_route_request` runs on the loop (chat.py:809) and calls HybridRouter.route → retriever.retrieve_for_routing → TaskEmbedder.embed_task_ir. That is a synchronous httpx call (orchestration/repl_memory/embedder.py:193-214, 10 s client timeout) on every real-mode /chat, and memrl is default_prod=True. Second, AdmissionController uses a blocking threading.Semaphore (acquire with timeout), and its docstring (admission.py:119-122) claims inference runs via asyncio.to_thread, which these paths contradict. Caveat: the /chat/stream example has no live client except the deprecated gradio_ui (see A1-06). The hot cases are /v1 and /chat routing plus cheap-first.
verification: New test tests/unit/test_api_event_loop_responsiveness.py: inject fake primitives whose llm_call does time.sleep(0.5). Using httpx.AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(app)), start POST /v1/chat/completions (stream=false, x_disable_repl) and assert a concurrent GET /health completes in under 100 ms. Repeat for /chat with cheap-first enabled. Existing goldens must stay byte-identical: pytest -p no:cacheprovider tests/unit/test_openai_compat_default_golden.py tests/unit/test_v1_escalation_off_golden.py tests/unit/test_openai_compat_backend_failure_status.py tests/unit/test_chat_routes.py (runnable once fact (a) conftest fix lands). Re-run the AST scanner and expect 0 hits outside lifespan.

## A1-02 [sev 5] [best_practice/bug] [effort M] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=30
**Shared LLMPrimitives reused across concurrent /chat requests: reset_counters() and cache_prompt writes corrupt in-flight requests**
files: O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/routing.py:526, O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/routing.py:552, O:src/llm_primitives/stats.py:76, O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/direct_stage.py:141, O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:1410, O:scripts/autopilot/eval_tower.py:4868
evidence: `_init_primitives` reuses one per-worker `state._real_primitives` for every non-request-specific /chat (routing.py:526-533). On each new request it then calls `primitives.reset_counters()`, which clears `call_log`, `total_tokens_generated`, `total_prompt_tokens_reported`, `total_generation_ms`, `_recursion_depth` and `_completed_queries` (stats.py:82-94; all plain instance attributes, not ContextVars). It also sets `primitives.cache_prompt = request.cache_prompt` (routing.py:552) on the shared object. Every stage builds its response from these shared counters (direct_stage.py:141,150,154; repl_executor.py:589,598; delegation_stage.py:163,172,181; proactive_stage.py:373-388). eval_tower reads `tokens_generated` from /chat responses (eval_tower.py:4868). eval_batch_serving, the only path that forces a fresh instance (routing.py:142-178), is off in production (test_runtime_flags.py:366). The project already knows about the sharing: TD-21.21 moved `_last_inference_meta` to a ContextVar (primitives.py:219-233; chat.py:1177-1188) but left the counters and cache_prompt shared. AppState never declares `_real_primitives`; it is created ad hoc via hasattr (routing.py:527). Failure scenario: request A (REPL, 30 s) and request B land on the same worker. B arrives at t=10 s and A's token counter is zeroed. A then reports only the tokens decoded after 10 s plus B's tokens, so AutoPilot's per-trial token metrics are wrong under concurrency. If B sent cache_prompt=false, prompt caching is off for A's remaining calls, and `_recursion_depth=0` resets A's recursion guard mid-flight.
proposal: Move the per-request counters into a `RequestStats` dataclass held in a ContextVar. `request_context()` already installs ten ContextVars (primitives.py:171-213); `reset_counters()` would install a fresh RequestStats and the `total_*` attributes become properties reading it. Pass cache_prompt as a `request_context(cache_prompt=...)` kwarg instead of mutating the shared instance. Declare the shared instance as a typed AppState field. /v1 builds a new LLMPrimitives per request (openai_compat.py:1410) only because trace keys are instance-level (primitives.py:234-238). Once request keys are a ContextVar, /v1 and legacy /chat/stream (chat.py:1400) can reuse the worker instance, keeping connection pools and skipping the per-construction stack-priors parse (backend.py:166).
CORRECTED: As proposed (RequestStats in a ContextVar, cache_prompt via request_context), plus: make `_recursion_depth` a ContextVar[int] and delete the depth reset from reset_counters(), since nesting is per call stack and not per instance. The batch paths already copy context (inference.py:1810, 1933; primitives.py:493). Add a test: 6 threads call llm_call on one shared primitives with a fake backend blocked on an Event; none may raise RecursionError, and a 6-deep nested call still raises.
verify-note: Verified: routing.py:526-533 reuses `state._real_primitives` and calls `reset_counters()`, which clears call_log and the total_* counters and sets `_recursion_depth = 0` (stats.py:82-94). routing.py:552 writes `primitives.cache_prompt` on the shared object. direct_stage.py:141/150 builds the response from `primitives.total_tokens_generated`. eval_tower.py:4868 reads `tokens_generated` from the /chat response. test_runtime_flags.py:366 pins EVAL_BATCH_SERVING=0. AppState never declares `_real_primitives`: grep finds it only in routing.py, chat.py comments, dashboard.py and primitives.py comments. The finder missed a worse failure mode with the same root cause. `_recursion_depth` is a plain shared int, incremented before dispatch (primitives.py:719-725, guard `>= max_recursion_depth`, default 5 at config/__init__.py:463) and decremented in a finally (750). Concurrent requests on one worker therefore share it. Five in-flight llm_calls on a worker, including calls queued on admission inside llm_call, make the sixth raise a spurious RecursionError. A reset_counters() mid-flight then drives the counter negative, which silently widens the guard. The non-atomic `+=` across to_thread workers also drifts.
verification: New test: run two `_handle_chat` coroutines concurrently against one fake-backend primitives where A makes 3 calls of 10 tokens and B makes 1 call of 7 tokens with B starting mid-A. Assert A.tokens_generated==30 and B==7. Today A reports 7+(tokens after reset). Existing: tests/unit/test_invocation_log_request_scope.py, test_chat_routes.py, test_openai_client_tool_mode.py.

## A1-03 [sev 4] [performance/perf] [effort S] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=25
**stack_priors.yaml (107 KB) re-parsed with pure-Python yaml on every call: 2-4 parses per /chat request, on the event loop**
files: O:src/registry/stack_priors.py:264, O:src/api/routes/chat_routing.py:305, O:src/api/routes/vision_serving.py:51, O:src/api/routes/chat.py:948, O:src/chat_completions_roles.py:52, O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:1142, O:src/api/routes/health.py:206
evidence: `load_stack_priors_artifact` does `yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())` with no cache (stack_priors.py:264-275), and `live_stack_role_records` calls it on every invocation (277-293). Measured here: 107,598 bytes parse in 285 ms on average (yaml.__with_libyaml__ False; safe_load never uses CSafeLoader anyway). Unconditional per-/chat hits: `_heuristic_role_priors` → `_live_heuristic_prior_roles` (routing.py:302 → chat_routing.py:305) and `_vision_roles()` (chat.py:948 → vision_serving.py:51). Direct mode adds `chat_completions_roles()` (chat.py:1238; also 547 in cheap-first) → `_live_chat_completions_roles` (chat_completions_roles.py:52), which is uncached even though the sibling thinking-role set in the same file has an mtime cache (`_priors_cache_key`, 162-208, 'this runs per request'). Other hits: each /v1 role override goes through `_servable_role_names` → `available_roles` → `_live_stack_role_ids` (openai_compat.py:1142,1181); each LLMPrimitives construction calls `static_chat_completions_roles` (backend.py:166), which /v1 does per request; /health parses 1-2x per probe (health.py:206,146). About 20 call sites in src/api in total.
proposal: Memoize `load_stack_priors_artifact` on (path, st_mtime_ns, st_size) using the existing `_priors_cache_key` pattern, and return a deep-frozen view or a `copy.deepcopy` so callers cannot poison the cache. Load with `yaml.load(text, Loader=getattr(yaml, 'CSafeLoader', yaml.SafeLoader))`. Behaviour is unchanged because callers already re-read live and the cache invalidates on mtime.
verify-note: stack_priors.py:263-275: `yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())`, no cache. live_stack_role_records (277-293) calls it on every call. The file is 107,598 bytes, and I measured 354 ms per parse here (yaml.__with_libyaml__ False). Unconditional per-/chat callers: routing.py:302 → chat_routing.py:304-305 `live_stack_role_records()`, and chat.py:948 `_vision_roles()` → vision_stage.py:51 → vision_serving.py:51. No lru_cache exists in chat_routing.py or vision_serving.py. Conditional per-request callers: chat_completions_roles() at chat.py:547/1238 → static_chat_completions_roles (chat_completions_roles.py:119-131), which parses unless ORCHESTRATOR_USE_CHAT_COMPLETIONS_ROLES is set, and grep finds no launcher that sets it. The sibling thinking-role cache with `_priors_cache_key` (162-208) confirms the mtime-cache pattern already exists. All of this runs on the event loop (see A1-01).
verification: Unit test: write a priors file, call live_stack_role_records twice (assert one parse via a patched yaml.load counter), touch it with new content and assert a reload. Then run tests/unit/test_stack_priors*.py, test_chat_vision.py and test_openai_compat_roles.py. On the host, compare RI-16 `routing_stage_ms['priors']` in /chat responses before and after.

## A1-04 [sev 4] [performance/perf] [effort M] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=20
**Dashboard snapshot tick parses the day's progress JSONL 4x (one result discarded), runs ps 3x and re-parses contention YAML up to 8x, per client, on the event loop**
files: O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:6333, O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:7218, O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:7420, O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:1077, O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:6954, O:src/api/routes/dashboard_topology.py:570, O:src/api/routes/dashboard_snapshot.py:81, O:src/scheduling/contention.py:207
evidence: Each SSE client's `_snapshot_payloads` (7420-7458) calls `snapshot()` up to 2 Hz. `_snapshot_impl` (7218-7380) does the following synchronously:
(a) Reads the full day's progress JSONL line by line through `_scan_recent_decisions` (7222) and `_scan_orchestrator_tasks` (7286), then twice more inside `_topology_activity_payload` (6333-6339). The scan at 6333 assigns `completed_pairs, _rolling, _cum` and never uses any of them; vulture flags completed_pairs and grep finds no other use. It has been like this since commit c8f14ddcb (2026-05-23).
(b) Runs `_discover_llama_ports()` uncached three times: 7243, inside `_poll_all_slots` (6954) and in `_topology_activity_payload` (6213). Each runs `subprocess.run(['ps','-eo','pid,etimes,cmd'])` (dashboard_topology.py:570-577). A TTL-cached `_port_roles_cached` exists (6732) but is not used here.
(c) `_sample_slots_with_placement_window` (1077-1120) calls the 747-line `_region_locks_payload` 2-4 times, and that function retries itself recursively (1790). Each call re-parses contention_matrix.yaml (44 ms measured) and reopens /proc/locks once per lock file (cpu_region_lock.py:571-591).
(d) After serializing the frame, it is immediately parsed back with `json.loads(resp.body)` just to read lifecycle sequences (7439-7440).
The docstring at 7012-7016 says 'without paying the cost of scanning the whole day's JSONL', while scan_recent_decisions iterates every line (dashboard_snapshot.py:81-128). The hub machine.html also polls region_locks every 5 s (machine.html:2333).
proposal: 1) Delete the discarded scan at 6333. 2) Thread `port_roles`, `recent_completed` and `in_flight_tasks` from `_snapshot_impl` into `_poll_all_slots(ports=...)` and `_topology_activity_payload(...)` instead of recomputing them. 3) Cache `load_contention_matrix` on mtime and read /proc/locks once per `_region_locks_payload` into an inode→pids map. 4) Replace the per-client builders with one per-worker frame producer: build in `asyncio.to_thread` at most every 0.5 s and broadcast to subscribers. 5) Make `_snapshot_impl` return the dict and serialize once. 6) Replace full-day rescans with an incremental tailer that keeps the file offset and rolling aggregates.
CORRECTED: As proposed. Also fold `_structured_tap_requests_for_dashboard` into the single per-worker frame producer, so one 1 MB tail parse per tick is shared across the snapshot and structured-tap streams and across clients.
verify-note: dashboard.py:6333 `completed_pairs, _rolling, _cum = _scan_recent_decisions(log_path)`. grep finds no other use of completed_pairs, and blame shows c8f14ddcb (2026-05-23). `_scan_recent_decisions_impl` iterates every line of the file (dashboard_snapshot.py:81-128), which contradicts the 7012-7016 comment. `_discover_llama_ports()` runs uncached at 6213, 6954 and 7243, and each run calls `ps -eo` via subprocess (dashboard_topology.py:570-577). `_sample_slots_with_placement_window` calls `_region_locks_payload` twice per attempt with max_attempts=2 (1092/1097). That function recurses on occupancy change (1791) and calls `load_contention_matrix()` (1612-1614), which is uncached (`yaml.safe_load` at contention.py:207). `_snapshot_payloads` does `json.loads(resp.body)` on every frame (7438-7440), and each SSE client runs its own loop. The finder missed one more per-tick cost: `_structured_tap_requests_for_dashboard` parses a 1 MB tap tail per call (dashboard.py:608). It runs in both `_snapshot_impl` (7303) and the structured-tap producer (7479), per client.
verification: Run the 15 tests/unit/test_dashboard_*.py files; they monkeypatch `_todays_progress_log`, `_discover_llama_ports`, `_region_locks_payload` and `_poll_all_slots`. Add a test asserting `_discover_llama_ports` and `_scan_recent_decisions` are each called exactly once per `_snapshot_impl`. On the host, compare `_SNAPSHOT_BUILD_STATS['last_duration_s']` (exposed via /dashboard/api/health) before and after.

## A1-05 [sev 4] [modularity/refactor] [effort L] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=-300
**openai_chat_completions: 1006-line god function with modes implemented twice and SSE error mapping pasted 4x, already diverging**
files: O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:1240, O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:1558, O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:1978, O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:2031, O:src/api/__init__.py:424
evidence: openai_compat.py:1240-2245 is 1006 lines, cc=141, nesting depth 9, with 23 commits since Aug 1. The four modes (client, vision, direct, REPL) exist twice: streaming at 1529-1816 and non-streaming at 2031-2131, including two copies of the REPL loop (1730-1815, 2092-2131). The AdmissionDenied/ContentionDenied → ContextOverflowError → Exception → SSE-error triad is pasted four times (1558-1586, 1600-1628, 1662-1690, 1767-1799). The function has 17 `yield "data: [DONE]\n\n"` exits and 7 hand-built `chat.completion.chunk` dicts. Mock streaming replays characters one per chunk (1486-1503), contradicting the comment 'never simulate tokens by replaying characters' (1882-1884). It reads `locals().get("repl_for_metadata")` (1942) because that name is unbound in mock mode. Behaviour has already diverged: a retryable ContextOverflow on stream=true becomes JSONResponse 503 with `Retry-After: 5` and body `{error:{message,type,code},detail}` (1978-1986). On stream=false the same exception reaches the app handler, which returns `Retry-After: 10` and `{error:'context_overflow',...,context_overflow:{...}}` (src/api/__init__.py:424-447).
proposal: Introduce `@dataclass _V1Result(text, reasoning, tool_calls, finish_reason, usage, repl)` and a single `async def _run_v1_mode(ctx, on_chunk=None) -> _V1Result`; ctx carries role, prompt, context, sampling and client_messages. That function does the client/vision/direct/REPL dispatch once, with escalation applied after it. Add `_sse_error_for(exc) -> (status, type, retry_after)`, defined next to and shared with the app-level exception handlers so stream and non-stream use one mapping, plus a `_chunk(delta, finish_reason=None, **extra)` builder. The route then becomes validation → `_run_v1_mode` → a JSON renderer or an SSE renderer, about 120 lines.
verify-note: Metrics confirm openai_chat_completions at 1240, len=1006, cc=141, depth=9. The four exception triads sit at 1558/1567/1575, 1600/1609/1617, 1662/1671/1679 and 1767/1776/1784. The file has 17 `data: [DONE]` yields and 7 chunk dicts. Mock mode replays characters (1486-1503). `locals().get("repl_for_metadata")` is at 1942. I confirmed the divergence. Streaming promotes a first-event 503 to a JSONResponse with `Retry-After: 5` and body `{error:{...},detail}` (1975-1986). Non-streaming re-raises ContextOverflowError (2169-2172) to the app handler, which returns `Retry-After: 10` and `{error:'context_overflow',...,context_overflow:{}}` (__init__.py:424-447). A further divergence: the promotion covers only code 503, so a non-retryable 413 overflow on stream=true returns HTTP 200 with an SSE error event, while stream=false returns 413.
verification: Byte-identical goldens: pytest -p no:cacheprovider tests/unit/test_openai_compat_default_golden.py tests/unit/test_v1_escalation_off_golden.py. Also run test_openai_compat_backend_failure_status.py (16 tests), test_openai_client_tool_mode.py, test_openai_direct_prompt_contract.py, test_openai_compat_bare_name_final.py and test_v1_escalation.py. Add one test asserting stream and non-stream ContextOverflow return the same Retry-After; the intended unification would set both to 10.

## A1-07 [sev 4] [modularity/refactor] [effort L] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=-40
**dashboard.py: 8,173 lines with 300-750-line payload builders and module-global test seams**
files: O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:1543, O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:4305, O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:1123, O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:5540, O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:6151, O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:63
evidence: 176 functions and the highest churn in src/api (29 commits since Aug 1). Longest builders: `_region_locks_payload` 747 lines, cc169 (1543); `_insight_graph_payload` 666 lines, cc181 (4305); `_annotate_live_observability_frame` 375, cc133 (1123); `pareto` 445, cc87 (5540); `_topology_activity_payload` 356 (6151); `autopilot_progress` 271 (5266); `gepa_status` 195 (7939). `_insight_graph_payload` defines 10 nested closures (4338-4530) before a 400-line build → link → BFS pipeline. Comments already mark the phases of `_region_locks_payload` ('Pass 1a' 1718, 'Pass 1b', 'Pass 2', coherence, display-only rows 2099, speech fold-in 2135). The 2026-05-21 extraction left dashboard.py importing private names back from its submodules (`_parse_structured_tap_requests`, `_INFERENCE_TAP_PATH`, `_TAP_SENTINEL_PATH` at 63-73). Tests patch module globals directly: `_AUTOPILOT_JOURNAL_PATH` 30x, `_AUTOPILOT_STATE_PATH` 14x, `AUTOPILOT_LOG` 10x, `_discover_llama_ports` 9x. A naive split breaks those patches.
proposal: Split by plane, with dashboard.py keeping only router aggregation and re-exports: `dashboard_live.py` (snapshot, region locks, slots, SSE), `dashboard_autopilot.py` (pareto, progress, gepa, autopilot_snapshot, control) and `dashboard_insight_graph.py`. The insight graph becomes a pure `build_insight_graph(journal_rows, strategy_rows, hint_rows, focus, depth, limit)` that needs no IO to test. Split `_region_locks_payload` along its labelled passes into `_scan_region_lock_files`, `_resolve_holders`, `_synthesize_panel_rows`, `_display_only_rows` and `_coherence_block`. Replace path globals with one `DashboardPaths` dataclass, resolved at call time from a module attribute, so tests patch one object.
verify-note: dashboard.py is 8,173 lines with 176 defs (grep), and churn ranks it first in src/api with 29 commits. Function metrics match orch_src.md: _region_locks_payload 747/cc169, _insight_graph_payload 666/cc181, _annotate_live_observability_frame 375/cc133, pareto 445/cc87, _topology_activity_payload 356. Lines 63-73 import the private dashboard_tap names. Test patch counts are about 30 for _AUTOPILOT_JOURNAL_PATH, 14 for _AUTOPILOT_STATE_PATH, 11 for AUTOPILOT_LOG and about 19 references to _discover_llama_ports. The split proposal and the router-path equality check are sound.
verification: Run the 15 tests/unit/test_dashboard_*.py files unchanged, or with a mechanical path-patch update. Check that `python -c 'import src.api.routes.dashboard as d; print(sorted(r.path for r in d.router.routes))'` is identical before and after. Golden JSON test for `_region_locks_payload` and `_insight_graph_payload` on fixture inputs.

proposal: Keep one implementation. Recommended: keep stream_adapter, which reuses the /chat stages `_route_request/_preprocess/_init_primitives`, and delete chat.py:1319-1695. If the operator wants no production behaviour change before an observation window, delete stream_adapter.py and test_stream_adapter.py instead; either way one copy goes. The finder also missed a non-test reference: scripts/benchmark/feature_validation_offline.py:468-480 imports stream_adapter. More important, the endpoint itself appears to have no live caller. The only /chat/stream client in all three repos is src/gradio_ui.py:69. That module is marked `.. deprecated:: This module is not actively used` (lines 3-7), is excluded in pyproject.toml:164, and gradio is recorded as not installed (epyc-root progress/2026-01/2026-01-31.md:256). So 'production serves the legacy path' means only that the legacy path would serve if anything called the endpoint.

## A1-09 [sev 3] [best_practice/refactor] [effort M] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=0
**API imports 13 scripts.* modules, including private functions of an analysis CLI, and double-imports stack_manifest via sys.path**
files: O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:136, O:src/api/routes/dashboard.py:147, O:src/api/routes/dashboard_topology.py:448, O:scripts/analysis/task_rate_goodput_replay.py:14, O:src/api/admission.py:79
evidence: `grep -rhoE 'from scripts\.[a-z_.]+ import' src/api` finds 13 distinct modules, 11 of the imports being scripts.server.stack_manifest. dashboard.py:136-140 imports the underscore-private `_archive`, `_frontier_for` and `_tokens_per_solved_task` from the analysis CLI scripts/analysis/task_rate_goodput_replay.py, whose import inserts the repo root into sys.path (lines 14-16). Runtime lock primitives live under scripts/autopilot (state_lock, state_ownership at dashboard.py:147-153). `role_aliases()` (dashboard_topology.py:448-469) inserts scripts/server into sys.path at call time and runs `from stack_manifest import ROLE_LAUNCH_META`. That creates a second module object alongside `scripts.server.stack_manifest` and re-runs its import-time work, including validate_serving_shape_capacity (stack_manifest.py:1744, fact a). Its comment says the lazy import 'keeps dashboard_topology importable without the scripts tree', but the same file imports `from scripts.server.realized_fleet` at module top (line 19). `_snapshot_impl` calls role_aliases for every role on every tick (7251).
proposal: Move the pure helpers into src: the task-rate replay archive/frontier/tokens-per-solved functions to src/autopilot_core/task_rate.py (the CLI then imports from src), and state_lock/state_ownership to src/autopilot_core. Change role_aliases to `from scripts.server.stack_manifest import ROLE_LAUNCH_META`. Add tests/unit/test_api_layering.py, an AST scan asserting src/api imports nothing from scripts.analysis and no underscore names from scripts.*, and that `sys.modules` has no bare 'stack_manifest' after `role_aliases('frontdoor')`.
verify-note: `grep -rhoE 'from scripts\.[a-z_.]+ import' src/api` finds 13 distinct modules, 11 of them stack_manifest imports. dashboard.py:136-140 imports `_archive`, `_frontier_for` and `_tokens_per_solved_task` from scripts/analysis/task_rate_goodput_replay.py, which mutates sys.path at 14-16. role_aliases (dashboard_topology.py:448-469) inserts scripts/server into sys.path and runs `from stack_manifest import ROLE_LAUNCH_META`, while line 19 of the same file imports `scripts.server.realized_fleet` at top level. The duplicate bare `stack_manifest` module re-runs validate_serving_shape_capacity (stack_manifest.py:1744) once per worker, not once per tick, because sys.modules caches it. No module in scripts/server shadows a stdlib name.
verification: The new layering test, plus test_dashboard_task_rate_panel.py, test_dashboard_helpers.py and test_dashboard_extern_attribution.py. `grep -rn 'from scripts.analysis' src` should return 0.

## A1-10 [sev 3] [tests/test] [effort M] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=-60
**Production code branches on PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST, and the tested DI providers are unused by any route**
files: O:src/api/__init__.py:301, O:src/api/routes/chat.py:268, O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/proactive_stage.py:34, O:src/api/dependencies.py:62, O:tests/integration/test_di_overrides.py:18
evidence: src/api/__init__.py:301 disables the whole app lifespan under pytest ('TestClient startup can deadlock'), so no test covers AppState, MemRL or registry initialization. chat.py:268 disables the disconnect watcher under pytest, so cancellation is never tested. proactive_stage.py:34-39 runs the architect plan call inline on the event loop when PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST is set or llm_call is a unittest.mock, so tests take a different concurrency path than production. These are exactly the paths where A1-01 lives. Separately, `dep_llm_primitives`, `dep_progress_logger`, `dep_hybrid_router`, `dep_tool_registry`, `dep_script_registry` and `dep_registry_loader` (dependencies.py:62-114) are referenced only by tests/integration/test_di_overrides.py (405 lines) and tests/unit/test_dependencies.py (299 lines); I grepped all three repos. The production `Depends(dep_*)` census covers only app_state(11), session_store(16), gate_runner(3), features(2), vision_*(8), health_tracker(1) and document_preprocessor(2). Routes read `state.tool_registry` and `state.hybrid_router` directly (e.g. openai_compat.py:1719-1720), so these tests validate an injection seam production does not use.
proposal: Replace the env sniffing with explicit configuration: `create_app(*, run_lifespan=True, watch_disconnect=True)`, with conftest building the app with the flags it needs. Fix the TestClient deadlock at its cause (recursive config bootstrap) instead of skipping lifespan. Drop `_should_inline_plan_call_for_test` and let tests await the to_thread path. For DI, either have routes declare `Depends(dep_tool_registry)` and the others, so tests stop mutating global get_state(), or delete the six unused providers and their override tests.

I grepped O, R and epyc-root across *.py, *.md and *.yaml.
verification: After the fact (a) conftest fix: run pytest -p no:cacheprovider tests/unit/test_dependencies.py tests/integration/test_di_overrides.py tests/unit/test_chat_routes.py. ## A1-12 [sev 3] [modularity/refactor] [effort M] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=-80
**passthrough.py re-implements the admission → KV-pool → region-lock protocol, kept in parity by comments and private imports**
files: O:src/api/routes/passthrough.py:475, O:src/api/routes/passthrough.py:500, O:src/api/routes/passthrough.py:628, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1344
evidence: The `gate` contextmanager (passthrough.py:500-625) and `_region_lock` (475-497) re-implement `_call_caching_backend`'s sequence of request semaphore, SharedKVPoolAdmission and cpu_region_lock/inference_lock. Parity is asserted only in comments ('Same bounded wait as _call_caching_backend', 519; the module docstring 'the same CPU region / inference lock as _call_caching_backend'). The module imports private helpers `_extract_port`, `_per_region_locks_enabled` (481) and `_role_config_for_backend` (962) from src.llm_primitives.inference. `_derived_prefill_timeout_enabled` (628-634) is a verbatim copy of inference.py:198-204 (flagged in the duplicate-bodies metric). The canonical path also handles native-batch shared locks and deadline_s (inference.py:1344-1386), which the copy omits. The lock is released through a manual `lock_ctx.__exit__` wrapped in `contextlib.suppress(Exception)` (610-614), so a failed release leaves no log line.
proposal: Extract `src/scheduling/admission_gate.py` holding `AdmissionGate(role, url, prompt_tokens_est, new_tokens, cancel_check, deadline_s=None)` as a contextmanager that yields a `first_output()` hook. Use it from both `_call_caching_backend` and passthrough. Manage lock lifetime with `contextlib.ExitStack` and log release failures at WARNING. Make the shared helpers public in a small module (`src/runtime/lock_selection.py`).
verify-note: Verified: `_region_lock` (475-497), the `gate` contextmanager (500-625) with the 'Same bounded wait as _call_caching_backend' comment at 519, private imports at 481 and 962, and `_derived_prefill_timeout_enabled` (628-634) byte-identical to inference.py:198-204. The manual `lock_ctx.__exit__` sits inside `contextlib.suppress(Exception)` (610-614). The canonical path (inference.py:1340-1390) also handles deadline_s, native-batch shared locks and `_backend_manages_region_locks(backend)`. A shared AdmissionGate must keep that last skip-branch even though passthrough always targets a single direct URL.
verification: Run tests/unit/test_passthrough_route.py and the inference/admission suites (test_admission.py, test_kv_pool_admission*.py). Add a test that a raising lock `__exit__` is logged, and a parity test that both callers select the same lock for a given (role, port, flag) tuple.

evidence: dashboard.py has 36 swallowed and 88 broad excepts (metrics); ruff BLE001 reports 926 project-wide. Replace bare `pass`/`return None` in the 36 dashboard sites with it. In request-path code, log at DEBUG with exc_info at minimum, or narrow the exception type.

## A1-14 [sev 3] [best_practice/refactor] [effort S] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=-5
**API modules do config, YAML and host work at import (beyond fact a), with dead module constants**
files: O:src/api/admission.py:100, O:src/api/admission.py:115, O:src/api/routes/chat_utils.py:34, O:src/api/routes/dashboard_topology.py:153, O:src/api/state.py:94
evidence: admission.py:100 computes `FALLBACK_LIMITS = _limits_from_stack_manifest()` at import, which imports scripts.server.stack_manifest and its import-time host-memory validation. admission.py:115 computes `DEFAULT_LIMITS = _load_default_limits()`, another stack_priors.yaml parse (285 ms). DEFAULT_LIMITS is never read anywhere: grep across O, R and epyc-root finds only its definition and the docstring line 28. FALLBACK_LIMITS is read only by tests/unit/test_admission.py:251,260. AppState's default factory (state.py:94) then calls `AdmissionController.from_defaults()` → `_load_default_limits()` again. chat_utils.py:34-36,102,121 freeze `get_config()` values into ROLE_TIMEOUTS, THREE_STAGE_CONFIG and QWEN_STOP at import, while `role_timeout_for` reads the live config, giving two sources of truth. dashboard_topology.py:153 builds `_PORT_HINTS` from stack priors at import, so port labels are frozen for the worker's lifetime after a stack change without an API reload. Add a test that imports every `src.api.routes.*` module with `get_config` patched to raise and asserts the imports succeed.

FALLBACK_LIMITS is read only by tests/unit/test_admission.py:251,260. state.py:94 `default_factory=AdmissionController.from_defaults` re-parses. chat_utils.py:34-36,102,121 freeze config at import while role_timeout_for (39-46) reads live. dashboard_topology.py:153 `_PORT_HINTS = _build_port_hints()` at import is read at 388.

## A1-M1 [sev 3] [debuggability/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [verifier-added] loc_delta=15
**/v1/chat/completions never binds primitives.request_context outside the streamed client/direct chunk path: no cancellation, no deadline, no task_id on locks or tap**
files: O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:1465, O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:2031, O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:1716, O:src/api/routes/chat.py:829, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:968, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1109
evidence: In openai_compat.py, the only `primitives.request_context(` is at 1465, inside `stream_call`. It binds only `cancel_check` and `session_id`. The non-streaming dispatch (2031-2131), the streaming REPL loop (1716-1815), the inline client/direct fallbacks (1552, 1654) and vision never bind a context. By contrast /chat binds cancel_check, deadline_s, task_id, request_id, priority, workload_class and max_queue_wait_ms (chat.py:829-844). The inference layer reads these ContextVars: `inference_lock(..., cancel_check=self.get_request_cancel_check(), deadline_s=self.get_request_deadline_s(), request_tag=self.get_request_task_id())` (inference.py:964-969), and `request.task_id = self.get_request_task_id(); request.parent_request_id = self.get_request_id(); inference_request_id = f"{parent_request_id or task_id}:..."` (1109-1112). grep counts 16 cancel/deadline reads in src/llm_primitives. The ContextVar defaults are None (primitives.py:171-215). Cost: a /v1 client that disconnects or times out (Aider, harnesses) leaves its generation and up to 5 REPL turns running, holding the region lock and admission slot. Region-lock holders and tap rows for /v1 carry request_tag/task_id None ('None:<hex>' ids), so the dashboard's lock-holder attribution cannot link them to a chat_id.
proposal: Create one `cancel_event` and a disconnect watcher per /v1 request, mirroring chat.py:256-270. Wrap the whole mode dispatch for both stream and non-stream in `primitives.request_context(cancel_check=cancel_event.is_set, deadline_s=..., task_id=chat_id, request_id=chat_id, session_id=request.x_session_id)`. With A1-05's `_run_v1_mode`, this is a single call site; set it in the coroutine before any to_thread so the context is copied.
verify-note: 
verification: New unit test with a fake LLMPrimitives whose llm_call and chat_completion_call record get_request_task_id() and get_request_cancel_check(). For stream=false direct, client and REPL modes, and for stream=true REPL mode, assert task_id == chat_id and cancel_check is not None. Existing goldens must stay byte-identical: test_openai_compat_default_golden.py, test_v1_escalation_off_golden.py, test_openai_compat_backend_failure_status.py.

## A1-M2 [sev 3] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [verifier-added] loc_delta=10
**Shared _recursion_depth on the per-worker LLMPrimitives raises spurious RecursionError under concurrency and is zeroed mid-flight by reset_counters**
files: O:src/llm_primitives/primitives.py:719, O:src/llm_primitives/primitives.py:725, O:src/llm_primitives/primitives.py:750, O:src/llm_primitives/stats.py:91, O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/routing.py:531, O:src/config/__init__.py:463
evidence: llm_call checks `if self._recursion_depth >= self.config.max_recursion_depth: raise RecursionError` (primitives.py:719-723), then does `self._recursion_depth += 1` (725) before dispatch and decrements in a finally (750). max_recursion_depth defaults to 5 (config/__init__.py:463) and no launcher overrides it (grep for MAX_RECURSION_DEPTH in O finds only the config line). The instance is shared across all non-request-specific real-mode /chat requests on a worker (routing.py:526-533), and each stage runs in its own to_thread. The counter therefore tallies every in-flight llm_call on the worker, including calls queued on admission or the region lock inside `_llm_call_impl`, and nested REPL sub-calls. It does not count one request's call stack. With 5 such calls in flight, the 6th request's first call raises RecursionError. `reset_counters()` sets `_recursion_depth = 0` (stats.py:91) while other calls are in flight; their finally blocks then drive it negative, which raises the effective cap. The `+=` read-modify-write on an attribute is not atomic across threads either.
proposal: Make the depth a per-instance `contextvars.ContextVar[int]` (default 0). llm_call does `token = depth_ctx.set(depth_ctx.get() + 1)` and calls `depth_ctx.reset(token)` in its finally. Remove the depth reset from reset_counters(). to_thread and the existing copy_context() batch paths (inference.py:1810, 1933; primitives.py:493) carry the parent depth into children, so nesting semantics are preserved and concurrent requests become independent. Track `_max_recursion_depth_reached` in the per-request stats from A1-02.
verify-note: 
verification: New test: one LLMPrimitives with a fake backend whose call blocks on a threading.Event. Start 6 threads calling llm_call, release them, and assert none raised RecursionError. A REPL-style nested chain of 6 llm_calls in one thread must still raise at depth 5. Existing: tests/unit/test_llm_primitives*.py and test_chat_routes.py.

So `routing.skill_ids` is always [] (routing.py:446), and every ChatResponse reports `skills_retrieved=0` and `skill_ids=[]` (direct_stage.py:157-158, delegation_stage.py:184-185, proactive_stage.py:391-392, chat.py:915-916), even when `routing.skill_context` was injected into the REPL context (repl_executor.py, 'Inject SkillBank skill context').

verify-note: The code is as described. extract_skill_ids reads `_last_skill_ids` (routing_decision.py:717-725), and grep across all three repos finds only that read. `git log --all -S"_last_skill_ids ="` returns nothing; `-S"_last_skill_ids"` returns only 06ccfdab and 6b8cd439. # A2 — orchestrator inference hot path  (findings kept: 24, refuted: 0, bloat_est: 2300)

This unit is about 35k LOC. It works, but it is fragile under concurrency, and code that was copied between paths has already drifted apart into real bugs. The most serious problem: the production API shares one LLMPrimitives across requests (chat_pipeline/routing.py:526-545), yet it keeps per-request state in plain attributes. Under concurrency that silently reroutes requests: a concurrent request's top-level frontdoor call was remapped to worker_general in a reproduction. It also causes spurious RecursionError at 6 in-flight calls, cross-wired early-stop hooks, and token counters that another request can zero. Second, the default production path (streaming /v1/chat/completions) is missing the failure handling the batch and /completion lanes have. A read timeout with partial output comes back as success=True and counts toward backend health; an empty read timeout comes back with error_message=None; context-limit truncation is never detected. Third, two hot-path costs are measured and avoidable. The per-chunk early-stop re-joins and re-regexes the whole output (25 s of CPU for 16k chunks with an open FINAL('''). /v1, voice and legacy /chat build a new LLMPrimitives per request, which means ~20-40 httpx pools that are never closed. Errors lose their type twice: RuntimeError("Inference failed: ...") becomes the "[ERROR: ...]" string. The ContextOverflowError handler in _execute_turn can never run, and two classify_error implementations disagree on whether an error is an infrastructure failure. On structure, the god functions (_execute_turn 767 lines/cc172, _call_caching_backend 710/15 args, four ~270-370 line llama_server lanes) and 12 copies of the node escalation chain account for most of the consolidation. Bloat estimate: ~640 LOC of provably dead or broken code (legacy ModelServer path calling a non-existent signature, _real_call_monitored, worker-pool batch needing an uninstalled nest_asyncio, unused slot APIs, infer_streaming). About 1,700 LOC more could be consolidated (llama_server lanes ~600, node chains ~500, call-path threading ~250, _execute_turn ~120, small helpers and classifiers ~200), roughly 2,300 LOC (~6.5% of the unit).

## A2-01 [sev 5] [best_practice/bug] [effort M] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=-30
**Shared LLMPrimitives keeps per-request state in plain attributes: concurrent requests get rerouted to other roles, hit spurious RecursionError, cross-wire early-stop hooks and corrupt token accounting**
files: O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/routing.py:526, O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/routing.py:532, O:src/llm_primitives/primitives.py:719, O:src/llm_primitives/primitives.py:569, O:src/llm_primitives/primitives.py:62, O:src/llm_primitives/stats.py:76, O:src/graph/helpers.py:1116, O:src/graph/helpers.py:1188
evidence: routing.py:526-545 reuses state._real_primitives for every request without request-specific URLs, and calls primitives.reset_counters() (line 532) at the start of each request. The code acknowledges the sharing at primitives.py:220-227 ('often SHARED'). Yet these stay plain instance attributes. (1) _recursion_depth (primitives.py:719-743) guards max_recursion_depth=5 and also drives _resolve_depth_override_role (primitives.py:569-575). depth_model_overrides has default_prod=True (features.py:147) and the default map is {1: worker_general} (primitives.py:62). Reproduction (scratchpad a2/depth.py, real LLMPrimitives, _real_call stubbed): request A's frontdoor call is in flight; request B's TOP-LEVEL llm_call(role='frontdoor') is dispatched as worker_general. Output: [('request-A','frontdoor'),('request-B','worker_general')]. Reproduction a2/recur.py: with 7 concurrent llm_calls, calls 6 and 7 raise 'RecursionError: Maximum recursion depth (5) exceeded'. reset_counters() mid-flight sets _recursion_depth=0 (stats.py:91), so the counter ends at -5 and the guard stays loosened by 5 for the rest of the process. (2) _early_stop_check is assigned and cleared on the shared object by the REPL turn (helpers.py:1116/1188), chat_delegation.py:159/174/456/468 and stages.py:202/211. It is read once per call at inference.py:1493. Request A's finally can disable request B's FINAL/CALL stop, or apply the architect TOON predicate to a REPL stream. (3) total_tokens_generated, which inference.py:1712 calls the 'SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH' for the Pareto speed objective, is zeroed by any other request's reset_counters, and ChatResponse.tokens_used reads it (delegation_stage.py:163/297, direct_stage.py:141). (4) cache_prompt is set per request on the shared object (routing.py:550).
proposal: Introduce src/llm_primitives/request_scope.py with a dataclass RequestScope: recursion_depth, max_depth_reached, early_stop, cache_prompt, counters (tokens/prompt_ms/gen_ms/overhead/calls), call_log, last_inference_meta, plus the 12 values now held in 12 separate ContextVars (primitives.py:171-217). Keep exactly ONE ContextVar[RequestScope]. Replace reset_counters() with begin_request() -> RequestScope, which binds a fresh scope in the request task's context. asyncio.to_thread copies the context, and the scope object is shared by reference, so counters accumulate correctly within one request. Replace the _early_stop_check attribute with a context manager primitives.early_stop(predicate) that sets scope.early_stop. Keep the old attribute names as properties delegating to the scope for one release. This is also the prerequisite for A2-04 (sharing backends safely).
CORRECTED: The RequestScope design stands, with two corrections. (1) Recursion depth must NOT live in the shared per-request scope. _real_batch runs up to batch_parallelism threads through ctx.run(copy_context()) (inference.py:1807-1811, 1930-1933); with a shared scope, 4 parallel batch calls would push depth to 4 and recreate the spurious override and RecursionError inside one request. Keep depth in its own ContextVar[int], set/reset with tokens in llm_call, so it follows the call stack and each copied context has its own value. (2) The scope's counters are shared by those batch threads, so += needs a lock (or per-thread partials merged at the end). Everything else stands: begin_request() replaces reset_counters, an early_stop() context manager replaces the attribute, and the old attributes become delegating properties for one release.
verify-note: routing.py:526-533 reuses state._real_primitives and calls reset_counters() per request. reset_counters (stats.py:76-91) zeroes _recursion_depth. llm_call (primitives.py:719-743) uses a plain-attribute depth guard, and _resolve_depth_override_role (569-608) remaps ANY role when depth>=1. depth_model_overrides is default_prod=True (features.py:147). The stack's _production_feature_env (orchestrator_stack.py:2408-2418) exports default_prod for every flag, and the wave overrides do not touch this one, so it is ON in production. I ran the finder's scratchpad repros with the O venv: depth.py printed [('request-A','frontdoor'),('request-B','worker_general')]; recur.py printed RecursionError for calls 6 and 7 and 'depth after all calls returned: -5'. _early_stop_check is assigned and cleared on the shared object at helpers.py:1116/1188, stages.py:202/211 and chat_delegation.py:159/174/456/468, and read once at inference.py:1493. tokens_used reads total_tokens_generated (delegation_stage.py:163/297, direct_stage.py:141). cache_prompt is set at routing.py:550.
verification: Add tests/unit/test_primitives_request_scope.py from the two scratchpad reproductions: (a) 7 threads x llm_call on one instance gives no RecursionError and depth returns to 0; (b) a concurrent top-level call keeps its role with depth overrides on; (c) two asyncio tasks each wrapped in begin_request() report independent total_tokens_generated; (d) early_stop set in task A is not visible in task B. Then run tests/unit/test_inference_mixin.py, test_graph_helpers_last_inference_meta.py and test_graph_helpers.py with -p no:cacheprovider (after the hermetic-conftest fix).

## A2-02 [sev 4] [debuggability/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=40
**The default streaming /v1/chat/completions lane reports a read-timeout truncation as success, an empty timeout as an untyped failure, and never detects context-limit truncation**
files: O:src/backends/llama_server.py:1859, O:src/backends/llama_server.py:1923, O:src/backends/llama_server.py:892, O:src/backends/llama_server.py:1338, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1580, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1700, O:tests/unit/test_llama_server.py:493
evidence: Streaming is the default transport: _call_caching_backend uses infer_stream_text whenever the call is not batch_only (inference.py:1580-1590, and the tap branch at 1546). frontdoor and the worker fleet run on the chat lane. In _infer_stream_text_chat_completions, a ReadTimeout only sets completion_reason='read_timeout' (1859); the result is then success=(bool(output) or ...) (1923-1924) with partial=False and degraded=False. The /completion stream lane instead returns success=False, partial=True, degraded=True, failure_reason='read_timeout' (1338-1361), and that contract is pinned by test_infer_stream_text_partial_timeout_sets_partial_flags. Reproduced in isolation (scratchpad a2/chat_rt.py): chat lane 'success=True partial=False degraded=False failure_reason='' completion_reason=read_timeout' versus completion lane 'success=False partial=True degraded=True failure_reason='read_timeout''. Consequences: inference.py:1702-1703 calls health_tracker.record_success, and the truncated text is returned as a complete answer. With zero chunks the chat lane returns success=False, error_message=None, failure_reason='', so inference.py:1711 raises 'Inference failed: None'. The batch chat lane converts finish_reason=length below max_tokens into context_limit (892-906); the streaming chat lane has no such check, so on the default path a context-truncated answer is reported as an ordinary 'length' stop. History: the divergence was introduced in 2c1711ae ('/v1/chat/completions backend for gemma-4'), which copied the lane without the /completion lane's partial handling.
proposal: Make both chat paths reuse the /completion terminal-state rules. On ReadTimeout with chunks, return success=False, partial=True, degraded=True, failure_stage='stream_read', failure_reason='read_timeout', completion_reason='read_timeout_partial'. With no chunks, use failure_reason='timeout' and a non-None error_message. Apply the length<max_tokens -> context_limit rule (move it into a shared helper _length_stop_is_context_limit(reason, generated, max_tokens)). This is best done as part of A2-06's shared _finish(). The output text is unchanged; only the flags change.
verify-note: llama_server.py:1854-1859: on ReadTimeout the chat stream lane only sets completion_reason='read_timeout'. It then returns success=(bool(output) or ...) with partial=False and degraded=False (1918-1927), and with no output error_message=None and failure_reason=''. The /completion stream lane returns success=False, partial=True, degraded=True, failure_reason='read_timeout', completion_reason='read_timeout_partial' (1362-1391), pinned by test_llama_server.py:493. The context_limit rule exists only in the batch chat lane (892-901). Chat-lane roles (frontdoor and the explore worker pool, via static_chat_completions_roles) take infer_stream_text whenever not batch_only (inference.py:1546/1580). inference.py:1702-1711 then records health success, or raises 'Inference failed: None'. `git log -S` traces the code to 2c1711ae.
verification: Add the scratchpad chat_rt.py scenarios as tests in tests/unit/test_llama_server.py: chat-lane partial read timeout, chat-lane empty read timeout, chat-lane finish_reason=length with predicted_n<max_tokens. Re-run tests/unit/test_llama_server.py, test_chat_stream_timings.py and test_inference_mixin.py.

## A2-04 [sev 4] [performance/perf] [effort M] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=60
**/v1, voice and legacy /chat rebuild the whole backend graph per request: ~20-40 httpx connection pools, PrefixRouters and a tokenizer client, never closed**
files: O:src/api/routes/openai_compat.py:1410, O:src/api/routes/voice.py:200, O:src/api/routes/chat.py:1400, O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/routing.py:535, O:src/llm_primitives/primitives.py:122, O:src/llm_primitives/backend.py:135, O:src/backends/llama_server.py:337, O:src/backends/llama_server.py:1961
evidence: openai_compat.py:1410, voice.py:200 and chat.py:1400 construct LLMPrimitives(mock_mode=False, server_urls=get_config().server_urls.as_dict(), ...) per request, and routing.py:535 does so per request when the request carries its own URLs. __init__ calls _init_caching_backends (primitives.py:122-123). That creates one LlamaServerBackend per (role, URL), each owning an httpx.Client(max_connections=20, keepalive) (llama_server.py:337-353), plus ConcurrencyAwareBackend/RoundRobinBackend wrappers and topology lookups (backend.py:135-360). It also creates a LlamaTokenizer client when accurate_token_counting is on (primitives.py:126-137). as_dict() returns ~16 roles (config/models.py:1020-1086), and multi-instance roles carry 5 URLs each. Nothing ever closes them: LlamaServerBackend.close() (llama_server.py:1961) has zero callers ('grep -rn "backend.close()" src' finds nothing), and LLMPrimitives has no close(). The keep-alive pool that the comment at llama_server.py:336 credits with '~6x latency reduction' therefore never survives past one request on these endpoints, and sockets and FDs wait for garbage collection.
proposal: Add a process-wide BackendPool (src/backends/pool.py) keyed by (base_url, use_chat_completions) that owns the LlamaServerBackend/CachingBackend/PrefixRouter instances and the per-role composite backends, built once from config with a close_all() wired to the FastAPI shutdown hook. LLMPrimitives(server_urls=...) borrows composites from the pool and builds only the request-specific URLs it lacks. Land this together with A2-01, so that sharing backends does not share request state; backends hold only cache_stats, which should become a locked or atomic counter.
CORRECTED: The proposal stands, with an ordering constraint. Pooling backends process-wide makes ConcurrencyAwareBackend's session maps (A2-12) accumulate across ALL endpoints; today they die with each per-request instance on /v1, voice and /chat/stream. Land A2-12's eviction and A2-01's request scoping before or with the pool.
verify-note: openai_compat.py:1410, voice.py:200 and chat.py:1400 construct LLMPrimitives(mock_mode=False, server_urls=get_config().server_urls.as_dict(), ...) per request. Correction: chat.py:1400 is inside the /chat/stream endpoint (chat.py:1266), not 'legacy /chat'. __init__ calls _init_caching_backends (primitives.py:122-123), and each LlamaServerBackend builds its own httpx.Client with max_connections=20 (llama_server.py:337-353). LlamaServerBackend.close() (1961) has no caller anywhere in src (grep of '.close()' over src/api and src/llm_primitives finds only unrelated objects), and LLMPrimitives has no close().
verification: Unit test: two LLMPrimitives(server_urls=same) return identical backend objects (`is`), and a request-specific URL builds a new one. Integration: count httpx.Client constructions via a patch across 10 /v1 requests (expect 0 after warm-up). Run tests/unit/test_inference_mixin.py, test_fleet_layer_fallback.py and test_llama_server.py.

## A2-M1 [sev 4] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [verifier-added] loc_delta=12
**Streaming FINAL early-stop matches inside an unclosed triple-quoted FINAL('''...''') and cuts code answers off mid-body**
files: O:src/graph/answer_resolution.py:49, O:src/graph/helpers.py:1116, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1519, O:src/repl_environment/context.py:228, O:src/prompt_builders/code_utils.py:713
evidence: The REPL early-stop predicate is `_FINAL_RE.search(text) or _CALL_STOP_RE.search(text)` (helpers.py:1116-1118). _FINAL_RE (answer_resolution.py:49-52) tries `'{3}(.+?)'{3}` first. While the closing ''' has not streamed yet, that alternative fails, and the single-quote alternative `["'](.+?)["']\s*\)` matches from the FIRST quote of ''' up to any later `'...')` or `"...")` in the body. The prompts explicitly tell models to answer as FINAL('''def foo(): ...''') (repl_environment/context.py:228; code_utils.py:713 builds the same form). Reproduction with the exact regex, streaming "FINAL('''\nimport sys\ndef main():\n    a, b = map(int, input().split(' '))\n    print(a + b)\nmain()\n''')" in 3-char chunks: the match fires at char 72 of 102. Text at the stop: "FINAL('''\nimport sys\ndef main():\n    a, b = map(int, input().split(' '))". The extracted value is "''\nimport sys\ndef main():\n    a, b = map(int, input().split(' ". _on_chunk_guarded raises StopIteration (inference.py:1519-1520), and both llama lanes treat that as a normal finish (llama_server.py:1211, 1819). The truncated code then reaches the REPL as the turn's output: an unterminated string, or a garbage FINAL rescue. Any code answer containing print('x'), split(' ') or similar is affected whenever the tap branch is active.
proposal: Give early-stop its own stop regex instead of reusing the extraction regex. For example, _FINAL_STOP_RE = re.compile(r"FINAL\(\s*(?:'''.*?'''|\"\"\".*?\"\"\"|'(?!'')[^'\n]*'|\"(?!\"\")[^\"\n]*\"|-?[\d.]+(?:e[+-]?\d+)?|True|False|None)\s*\)", re.DOTALL), whose single-quote alternatives cannot start at a triple quote or cross a newline. Apply the same (?!'') / (?!"") guard to _FINAL_RE's third alternative so _extract_final_from_raw cannot return the garbage slice either. The new regex still ends in ')' with no '$', so A2-03's skip-unless-')' optimization stays exact.
verify-note: 
verification: New unit test in tests/unit/test_answer_resolution*.py (or test_graph_helpers.py): stream the sample answer above, and variants with """ and with CALL(...), through the predicate in 1-, 3- and 7-char chunks. Assert no stop before the closing ''') and a stop exactly when it arrives. Assert _extract_final_from_raw on complete strings is unchanged against the existing FINAL extraction tests. Run tests/unit/test_graph_helpers.py -p no:cacheprovider.

## A2-03 [sev 3] [performance/perf] [effort S] [behav none] [partially] loc_delta=25
**Per-chunk early-stop check is quadratic in output length (full join plus full-text regex on every streamed chunk)**
files: O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1508, O:src/graph/helpers.py:1116, O:src/graph/answer_resolution.py:49, O:src/graph/helpers.py:407, O:src/api/routes/chat_delegation.py:140
evidence: _on_chunk_guarded (inference.py:1508-1531) runs, for EVERY chunk, `_stop_check("".join(_acc))`. For REPL turns (every graph turn, helpers.py:1116) the predicate runs _FINAL_RE.search and _CALL_STOP_RE.search over the whole accumulated text. _FINAL_RE uses lazy DOTALL alternatives `'{3}(.+?)'{3}` (answer_resolution.py:49-52), so after an unclosed FINAL(''' each search rescans to the end. Microbenchmark with the exact regexes (scratchpad a2/quad.py, 4-char chunks): 2000 chunks 0.03s/0.34s, 4000 0.09s/1.48s, 8000 0.32s/5.80s, 16000 1.43s/24.95s (plain / with an open FINAL('''). This runs on the inference thread while it holds the CPU-region/inference lock and the GIL, and the competitive-programming nudge at helpers.py:1466-1473 explicitly tells models to emit FINAL(solution) with long strings. The architect predicate (chat_delegation.py:140-157) also runs _strip_think plus `</think>.*<think>` (DOTALL) over the full text on every chunk.
proposal: Model early stop as EarlyStop(predicate, trigger) and keep the joined text incrementally (a list plus a cached join refreshed only when a check actually runs). Both REPL regexes can only produce a NEW match ending at a ')' character. Neither has lookahead, and the text is append-only, so a match ending inside the old text would already have matched. Skipping the check when the new chunk contains no ')' is therefore exactly behavior-preserving. Give the architect predicate triggers {'\n','|','>'}, or make it incremental over the tail since the last '</think>'. Keep _detect_streaming_repetition at its 50-chunk interval.
CORRECTED: Keep the REPL optimization: skip both regexes unless the new chunk contains ')', and cache the join. For the architect predicate, do NOT use trigger-char skipping. Make it incremental instead: track the offset of the last '</think>' and run _toon_re only on the tail since then, evaluated on every chunk so the end-of-text lookahead semantics are preserved. Scope the claim to the tap-active branch, or better, land it together with moving early-stop out of the tap branch (see missed A2-M2). Fix the FINAL premature-match bug (A2-M1) first, because it changes the predicate.
verify-note: The quadratic cost is real: _on_chunk_guarded (inference.py:1508-1531) calls _stop_check(''.join(_acc)) on every chunk. I re-ran the benchmark: 2000/4000/8000 chunks took 0.02/0.08/0.36 s plain and 0.41/1.48/5.64 s with an open FINAL('''. However, this branch runs ONLY when the inference tap is active: can_stream = tap_enabled and ... (inference.py:1457-1462), and _stop_check is read only inside `if can_stream`. The non-tap branch (_cancel_only, 1583) never applies the early-stop. The hot path exists only where the tap sentinel is present. Per the tests/unit/conftest.py:109-125 docstring the sentinel is 'left in place on this host', so it is plausible on prod, but not 'every graph turn' everywhere. The REPL half of the proposal is exactly behavior-preserving: both _FINAL_RE and _CALL_STOP_RE end in a literal ')', have no lookahead or '$', and the text is append-only. The architect half is not: _toon_re = r'D\|[A-D](?=[^a-zA-Z]|$)|...' (chat_delegation.py:146) can match ONLY at end-of-text through '$'. With trigger chars {'\n','|','>'}, a chunk 'A' after 'D|' is skipped, and if the next chunk starts with a letter the match is lost permanently. _strip_think is also not an append-only transform.
verification: Replay test: for recorded tap streams (or synthetic chunk sequences including FINAL('''...'''), CALL("x", a=1) and TOON D|A), assert StopIteration fires at the same chunk index before and after. Add a benchmark assertion that 16k chunks with an open FINAL(''' takes under 0.5s. Run tests/unit/test_inference_mixin.py and tests/unit/test_graph_helpers.py.

## A2-05 [sev 3] [debuggability/bug] [effort M] [behav intended] [partially] loc_delta=40
**Backend failures lose their type twice (RuntimeError text, then the '[ERROR: ...]' string); _execute_turn's ContextOverflowError/InferenceError handlers can never run**
files: O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1711, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1330, O:src/llm_primitives/primitives.py:1006, O:src/graph/helpers.py:1144, O:src/graph/helpers.py:1235, O:src/exceptions.py:20, O:src/api/health_tracker.py:235
evidence: InferenceResult carries failure_stage/failure_reason/completion_reason (e.g. timeout, http_status, empty_generation, read_timeout), but inference.py:1711 raises plain RuntimeError(f"Inference failed: {result.error_message}"). Circuit-open raises plain RuntimeError at 1330/1332, although src/exceptions.py already defines InferenceTimeoutError and BackendUnavailableError (lines 20-25) and nothing in this path raises them. _llm_call_impl then catches every exception and returns f"[ERROR: {e}]" (primitives.py:1006-1012). As a result, the 20-line `except ContextOverflowError` block in _execute_turn (helpers.py:1144-1163), together with its comment 'escalation to a larger-context role is the error classifier's decision', and the InferenceError/OSError handlers below it can never run for inference failures, because llm_call never raises them. grep confirms the only `raise` in primitives.py is in chat_completion_call (line 842). Overflow arrives as a sentinel, _backend_infra_sentinel (helpers.py:1235-1243) marks _infra_failure, and the node ends the task (nodes.py `_is_infra_failure` -> _make_end_result); the classifier is never consulted. Model fallback then string-classifies the RuntimeError (health_tracker.classify_failure, health_tracker.py:235-237 `'circuit open' in msg or 'unavailable' in msg`), the same string matching TD-21 already had to patch for ContentionDenied (comment at 217-227).
proposal: Add `class InferenceFailed(InferenceError, RuntimeError)` carrying role, backend_url, failure_stage, failure_reason and completion_reason; subclassing RuntimeError keeps every existing `except RuntimeError` working, the same way ContextOverflowError does. Raise it at inference.py:1711, and BackendUnavailableError(RuntimeError) at 1330/1332. Make classify_failure read exc.failure_reason before string-matching. In _llm_call_impl, either re-raise InferenceError subclasses for graph callers (behind a keyword such as raise_typed=True passed by _execute_turn) or put the typed reason into the sentinel. Delete or redo the unreachable handler block in helpers.py:1144-1190 accordingly.
CORRECTED: Same typed-exception plan, but keep the INC-20260924 contract explicit. With raise_typed=True, _execute_turn should map BackendUnavailableError/AdmissionDenied to the existing infra-failure return ({'_infra_failure': True}). Only ContextOverflowError and InferenceFailed(failure_reason=...) should reach the classifier. Add a test pinning both branches.
verify-note: I confirmed every step of the chain. inference.py:1711 raises plain RuntimeError. Circuit-open raises plain RuntimeError (1328-1332). ContextOverflowError is raised at inference.py:1136/1269/1282, but _llm_call_impl's `except Exception` (primitives.py:1006-1012) turns it into '[ERROR: ...]'. The infra-sentinel docstring itself says llm_call never raises (helpers.py:424-435). So the ContextOverflowError and InferenceError handlers in _execute_turn (helpers.py:1144-1175) cannot run for the real LLMPrimitives. health_tracker.classify_failure string-matches (health_tracker.py:235-245). I downgraded severity because ending infra denials as infra failures is a deliberate INC-20260924 decision. The live misbehavior is narrower: a context overflow also ends the task, instead of reaching the classifier and escalating as the dead handler's comment intends. The rest is dead code plus string-typed classification.
verification: Unit tests: a fake backend returning failure_reason='timeout' makes _real_call raise InferenceFailed whose failure_reason is 'timeout'; classify_failure returns 'timeout' without string matching; _execute_turn gets a typed overflow and the classifier path is exercised. Run tests/unit/test_inference_mixin.py, test_graph_helpers.py and test_health_tracker*.

## A2-08 [sev 3] [modularity/refactor] [effort L] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=-250
**_call_caching_backend (710 lines, 15 args) sits at the bottom of a 6-hop chain that re-declares 13-17 parameters; admission release is manual, with a leak window**
files: O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1030, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:577, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:728, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:879, O:src/llm_primitives/primitives.py:869, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1201, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1325, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1508
evidence: llm_call (17 params) -> _llm_call_impl (17, primitives.py:869) -> _real_call (13, inference.py:577) -> _real_call_impl (13, 728) -> _real_call_single (13, 879) -> _call_caching_backend (15, 1030) -> InferenceRequest. Each hop re-lists prompt, role, n_tokens, stop_sequences, json_schema, grammar, temperature, seed, top_p, top_k, n_probs, post_sampling_probs and on_chunk; adding post_sampling_probs (TD-1d.2) meant editing 7 signatures and 9 call sites. _call_caching_backend holds request building (1063-1110), context clamp and refusal (1111-1155), HTTP admission (1199-1211), KV-pool admission (1229-1300), lock selection (1336-1388), three near-identical chunk callbacks (_on_chunk_guarded 1508, _on_chunk_tap 1542, _cancel_only 1583: each calls _emit_first_output, forwards to on_chunk and checks cancel) and finalization (1610-1735). admission.acquire succeeds at 1201, but the try/finally that releases it starts at 1325. Admission is also released by hand in two raise sites (1263, 1279). Any unexpected exception in between (pool_admission.reservation_tokens, bind_lifecycle_context at 1323, the imports) leaks a queue slot for the rest of the process.
proposal: (1) Add a frozen dataclass CallSpec(prompt, role, n_tokens, stop, json_schema, grammar, sampling: Sampling, n_probs, post_sampling_probs, on_chunk) and pass it through every hop. (2) Add an AdmissionLease context manager covering HTTP admission and the KV-pool ticket (release on __exit__, with success taken from a flag), entered via contextlib.ExitStack right after acquisition. (3) Add a ChunkObserver(tap, on_chunk, cancel, early_stop, rep_guard, first_output_cb) class replacing the three closures. (4) Split into _prepare_request(spec) -> (request, clamp), _admit(...) -> lease, _lock_ctx(...), _dispatch(backend, request, observer) -> result, _finalize(result, ...) -> str. This is pure restructuring; the InferenceRequest field values stay identical.
verify-note: _call_caching_backend is 710 lines with 15 args (metrics). admission.acquire succeeds at inference.py:1201-1210, but the try/finally that releases it starts at 1325 (finally at 1734-1739). The manual releases are at 1268 and 1281 (the finder cited 1263/1279). In the window: estimate_tokens_conservative, pool_admission.reservation_tokens, pool_admission.acquire raising anything other than KVPoolQueueFull, serving_calls.prefix_ladder, _prefix_index_kwargs and bind_lifecycle_context (1323). Any exception there leaks the admission slot, and the pool_ticket too once it is acquired. The three chunk closures (1508, 1542, 1583) share the _emit_first_output, on_chunk and cancel logic.
verification: Golden test: patch the backend to capture the InferenceRequest and assert all fields for a matrix of kwargs, before and after. Fault-injection test: make reservation_tokens raise and assert admission.release was called. Run tests/unit/test_inference_mixin.py, test_fleet_layer_fallback.py and test_generation_monitor.py.
