**The node failure->escalation chain is copied 12 times (6 pydantic_graph nodes plus 6 LangGraph nodes); the copies share a log bug that always reports 'after 0 failures'**
files: O:src/graph/nodes.py:228, O:src/graph/nodes.py:263, O:src/graph/nodes.py:379, O:src/graph/langgraph/nodes.py:181, O:src/graph/langgraph/nodes.py:206, O:src/graph/decision_gates.py:1
evidence: Both graph/nodes.py (868 LOC) and graph/langgraph/nodes.py (731 LOC) contain 13 _should_think_harder/_should_escalate sites and 6 copies of: infra check -> consecutive_failures += 1 -> _classify_error -> _record_failure -> early-abort gate -> think-harder -> escalate -> retry -> fail, plus the nudge-promotion tail (compare FrontdoorNode 211-300 with WorkerNode 337-404). Bug shared by the copies: `state.consecutive_failures = 0` runs BEFORE `_log_escalation(..., f"Escalating after {state.consecutive_failures} failures")`, at nodes.py:263, 379, 499, 606 and 712 and langgraph/nodes.py:206 and 303, so every escalation record says 'after 0 failures'. The LangGraph backend runs only behind 7 default-off flags (features.py:182-188) and the TM-7 parity script, so the duplication exists only to keep the two backends at parity.
proposal: Add decide_after_turn(ctx, output, error, artifacts, *, self_node, escalate_to: Role) -> Transition (enum: END_OK(answer) | END_FAIL(msg) | RETRY | THINK_HARDER | ESCALATE(role)) in graph/decision_gates.py, implemented once. Both backends map the Transition to a node class or a next_node string. Capture n = state.consecutive_failures before resetting and log n. Each node.run shrinks to roughly 20 lines.
CORRECTED: Implement decide_after_turn once and migrate the LangGraph nodes FIRST (the production path), then point the pydantic bodies at it. Better still, since production has fully cut over, plan retiring the pydantic node bodies behind the per-node flags; that is the larger LOC win. Verify with tests/unit/test_langgraph_phase3.py plus the parity script, and add a test asserting the escalation log carries the pre-reset count on the LangGraph path.
verify-note: The duplication and the log bug are real. `state.consecutive_failures = 0` precedes f'Escalating after {state.consecutive_failures} failures' at nodes.py:257/263, 373/379, 493/499, 600/606 and 706/712, and at langgraph/nodes.py:201/206, 298/303, 401/406, 492/497 and 581/586. The framing is wrong, though. The finder says LangGraph 'runs only behind default-off flags'. In fact orchestrator_stack.py:2598-2601 unconditionally sets ORCHESTRATOR_LANGGRAPH_{INGEST,WORKER,FRONTDOOR,CODER,CODER_ESCALATION,ARCHITECT}=1 (LANGGRAPH_PHASE3_LIVE_ENV_VARS, 2397). The env key ORCHESTRATOR_<env_var> is checked first in _compute_feature_flags (features.py:883-885), and FrontdoorNode.run starts with `if langgraph_frontdoor: return await _run_via_langgraph(...)` (nodes.py:193). So in production the LangGraph copies are the LIVE path. The pydantic_graph node bodies are the dormant copies on stack launches, and they are what default-test unit tests exercise.
verification: Table-driven test over (error category x state flags) asserting the Transition. Run the existing graph node tests and scripts/trace/run_task_lg_parity.py --arms run_task_lg,run_task on its fixed task set, expecting an identical decision chain. Assert the escalation log text contains the pre-reset count.

## A2-12 [sev 3] [performance/perf] [effort S] [behav possible] [partially] loc_delta=30
**ConcurrencyAwareBackend session maps grow without bound and are deep-copied under the dispatch lock into every ChatResponse**
files: O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:1394, O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:702, O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:1112, O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:1957, O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:945, O:src/llm_primitives/backend.py:510, O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/stages.py:314
evidence: _session_last_seen is written on every dispatch with a session_id (1394) and never popped (only read at 964). _session_state (written by _set_session_state at 702, 15 call sites) and _session_quarter are popped only in clear_session (1112-1118), which has zero callers (grep 'clear_session' across all 3 repos finds only the definition and a March progress note), and in reverse migration. get_stats (1957-1997) copies the whole _session_quarter and deep-copies every _session_state entry while holding self._lock, the same lock _dispatch takes at 1393, 1802 and 1847. BackendMixin.get_cache_stats (backend.py:510-520) calls it for every role, and the chat pipeline calls primitives.get_cache_stats() at the end of every real request (stages.py:314, repl_executor.py:584/807, delegation_stage.py:167/262, direct_stage.py:145/257), embedding the result in ChatResponse.cache_stats. Lock hold time and response size therefore grow with the number of distinct sessions since boot. _maybe_spawn_reverse_migration also scans all of _session_quarter linearly under the lock on every quarter release (945).
proposal: Evict entries older than max(reverse-migration window, 2x role timeout) on dispatch, or use an OrderedDict LRU capped at N. Keep a reverse index quarter -> session for the release path. Make get_stats return counts plus only active and migration_pending sessions; keep the full map behind a debug flag or the dashboard-only kv_migration_status.
CORRECTED: The proposal stands, but must land before or with A2-04: pooling backends would otherwise extend the growth to every endpoint.
verify-note: _session_last_seen is written at concurrency_aware.py:1394 and never popped. _session_quarter and _session_state are popped only by clear_session (1112-1118), which has zero callers in all three repos (grep finds only its definition and progress/2026-03-29.md), and by reverse migration and remove_quarter. get_stats (1957-1966) copies _session_quarter and dict-copies every _session_state entry under self._lock. ConcurrencyAwareBackend is stored directly in _backends (llm_primitives/backend.py:307), and get_cache_stats calls get_stats per role (backend.py:510-520), invoked per request at stages.py:314. Correction: unbounded growth occurs only on long-lived instances, i.e. the shared state._real_primitives of the chat pipeline (routing.py:526-545). /v1, voice and /chat/stream build per-request backends (A2-04) whose maps are discarded. The copy is a shallow dict(state) per entry, not a deep copy.
verification: Unit test: dispatching 10k distinct session_ids leaves the map size bounded, and get_stats size stays constant. Run tests/unit/test_concurrency_aware_migration_sm.py, test_concurrency_aware_quarter_preference.py, test_kv_migration_status.py and test_load_transition_migration.py.

## A2-M2 [sev 3] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [verifier-added] loc_delta=-15
**FINAL/CALL/TOON early stop and the degenerate-loop repetition guard run only on the inference-tap branch, so generation behavior depends on whether a TUI sentinel file exists, and unit tests force it off**
files: O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1457, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1493, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1522, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1583, O:src/runtime/inference_tap.py:162, O:tests/unit/conftest.py:109
evidence: can_stream = tap_enabled and hasattr(backend, 'infer_stream_text') and _tap_should_stream_role(role) and not batch_only (inference.py:1457-1462). tap_enabled = is_active() and bool(backend_url), and is_active() is true only when INFERENCE_TAP_FILE is set or the '.inference_tap_active' sentinel written by the autopilot/seeding TUI exists (runtime/inference_tap.py:162-164; dashboard_tap.py:23). The _early_stop_check read (1493), the StopIteration on a predicate hit (1519) and the _detect_streaming_repetition guard (1522-1530) live only inside `if can_stream`. The non-tap streaming branch, _cancel_only (1583-1590), forwards chunks and checks cancellation only. Without the sentinel, the REPL's 'abort the moment FINAL/CALL is detected' (helpers.py:1110-1118), the architect TOON stop (chat_delegation.py:136-159) and the repetition-loop abort silently never fire, and generation runs to the stop sequence or n_tokens. tests/unit/conftest.py:109-140 (_hermetic_inference_tap) forces the tap OFF for every unit test, so the suite never exercises early stop or the repetition guard unless a test opts into real_inference_tap.
proposal: Move early stop and the repetition guard into one ChunkObserver (the class proposed in A2-08) applied on BOTH streaming branches. The tap only adds tap.write_chunk and the tap metadata. The predicate is read once per call from the request scope (A2-01), not from a shared attribute.
verify-note: 
verification: Unit test with the tap forced off (the default fixture): a fake backend streams 'x = 1\nFINAL("42")\nmore text...'. Assert the backend saw StopIteration at the FINAL chunk and the returned output ends at FINAL("42"). A second test streams a repetitive loop and asserts the abort after the 50-chunk check. Run tests/unit/test_inference_mixin.py and test_graph_helpers.py -p no:cacheprovider.

## A2-11 [sev 2] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [partially] loc_delta=-45
**Two divergent classify_error implementations: the API's escalation policy never sees INFRASTRUCTURE**
files: O:src/graph/error_classifier.py:8, O:src/prompt_builders/code_utils.py:791, O:src/api/routes/chat.py:1631, O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/stream_adapter.py:353
evidence: graph/error_classifier.py:8-36 checks timeout -> json/schema -> format -> abort -> backend/connection/502/503 (INFRASTRUCTURE) -> code -> logic. prompt_builders/code_utils.py:791-840 has NO INFRASTRUCTURE branch, checks schema/format first, and maps any 'expected'/'actual' to LOGIC. The API stream paths use the second (chat.py:49/1631 and stream_adapter.py:29/353 import it via src.prompt_builders) and feed it to EscalationPolicy().decide. Examples: 'Backend unavailable (circuit open): http://...' is INFRASTRUCTURE in the graph but UNKNOWN in the API; 'Expected 200 from backend' is LOGIC in the API, which counts toward escalating to a bigger model. ErrorCategory.INFRASTRUCTURE is documented as 'Backend or network failure (not task failure)' (orchestration/escalation.py:59).
proposal: Delete prompt_builders/code_utils.classify_error and re-export graph.error_classifier.classify_error from src.prompt_builders (keeping the gate_name parameter as an optional pre-check that maps gate names to FORMAT). Better still, move the single implementation next to ErrorCategory in src/orchestration/escalation.py. Once A2-05 lands, prefer the typed failure_reason over string matching.
CORRECTED: Keep one classifier, but re-justify it with the TIMEOUT, SCHEMA and EARLY_ABORT divergences above rather than INFRASTRUCTURE. Before unifying, note that the API classifier's gate_name pre-check and its CODE keywords (attributeerror, modulenotfound, indentationerror) are absent from the graph version; merge them as a union with an explicit precedence order, pinned by the parametrized test.
verify-note: Two divergent implementations exist (graph/error_classifier.py:8-36 and prompt_builders/code_utils.py:791-840). The API stream loops use the latter (chat.py:1631, stream_adapter.py:353). The headline consequence is inert, however. EscalationPolicy special-cases only TIMEOUT (orchestration/escalation.py:275), EARLY_ABORT (285), SCHEMA (303) and no_escalate_categories={FORMAT} (191-196). INFRASTRUCTURE appears nowhere else in src except its definition and the graph classifier, so INFRASTRUCTURE and UNKNOWN produce the same decision. The divergences that do matter are ordering and keyword differences: 'json.decoder.JSONDecodeError: Expecting value' is SCHEMA in the graph but UNKNOWN in the API (no 'json' keyword, and 'expecting' does not match 'expected'); 'Expected 5, got timeout' is TIMEOUT in the graph but LOGIC in the API.
verification: Parametrized test over ~20 representative error strings (circuit open, HTTP 500/503, timeout, SyntaxError, AssertionError, schema) asserting one category per string, run against both import paths. Run tests/unit/test_graph_helpers.py and the stream_adapter/chat tests.

## A2-13 [sev 2] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav none] [partially] loc_delta=-5
**_dispatch enters cross-process region locks by hand; an exception before the try/yield leaks a flock held by the API process**
files: O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:1444, O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:1468, O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:1748, O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:1786, O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:1855, O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:1877
evidence: Region locks are entered with ctx.__enter__() in three places: native-batch shared_ctx (1444-1451), _try_instance (1468-1483) and the blocking fallbacks (1748-1774). The matching __exit__ runs only in the finally of the try that starts at 1877. In between, 1786-1876 runs a `with self._lock` state update, MigrationTransaction(...) construction, threading.Thread(...).start(), which raises RuntimeError when no thread can be started, _set_session_state and the self._quarters[chosen_idx] index. An exception in that window leaves the flock held by the long-lived uvicorn process. Every other process or request that needs those regions then polls until ContentionDenied ('placement timeout ... after 60.0s', 1729) until the API restarts. Production runs this path: orchestrator_stack.py:2512/2535 set ORCHESTRATOR_PER_REGION_LOCKS=1 and ORCHESTRATOR_PLACEMENT_STATE_MACHINE=1.
proposal: Create a contextlib.ExitStack at the top of _dispatch and acquire every lock via stack.enter_context(cpu_region_lock_for_instance(...)). For _try_instance, return the context and let the caller enter_context it on success. Wrap everything after acquisition in try/finally with stack.close() (keeping the in-process _release). No semantic change on success.
CORRECTED: The ExitStack refactor stands (deterministic release, and _release in the same finally). Restate the impact as 'non-deterministic flock release (GC-dependent) plus permanently stuck in-process active flags', not a held-until-restart lock.
verify-note: The manual __enter__ calls exist at 1456 (native batch), 1485 (_try_instance), and 1765/1782 (blocking fallbacks). The matching __exit__ happens only in the finally at 1886-1892, and 1797-1876 runs the migration state update, the MigrationTransaction(...) construction and threading.Thread(...).start() in between. The consequence is overstated. cpu_region_lock_for_instance and cpu_region_lock are @contextmanager generators (cpu_region_lock.py:865, 1218) whose finally closes the flock handles (around 1156-1161). An abandoned, entered generator is closed when garbage-collected (PEP 442), so in CPython the flock is released once the propagating traceback is dropped, not 'until the API restarts'. What really leaks is the in-process _full_active/_quarter_active telemetry, because _release is skipped. The flock is also held for a non-deterministic window if a reference cycle keeps the frame alive.
verification: Fault-injection unit test with a fake cpu_region_lock_for_instance that records enter/exit: patch threading.Thread.start to raise and assert exit was called; the same for MigrationTransaction raising. Run tests/unit/test_concurrency_aware_*.py and test_load_transition_migration.py.

## A2-15 [sev 2] [performance/perf] [effort M] [behav none] [partially] loc_delta=15
**Blocking I/O (Kùzu query, corpus index search, file reads and writes) runs on the event loop inside async _execute_turn every turn**
files: O:src/graph/helpers.py:892, O:src/graph/helpers.py:911, O:src/graph/task_ir_helpers.py:63, O:src/graph/task_ir_helpers.py:97, O:orchestration/repl_memory/failure_graph.py:301, O:src/prompt_builders/builder.py:1065
evidence: The LLM call is offloaded (helpers.py:774 asyncio.to_thread), but these run synchronously on the loop thread inside `async def _execute_turn`: build_corpus_context (helpers.py:911 -> builder.py:1065 retriever.retrieve, a sharded n-gram index query with its own slow_query_ms budget); _check_anti_pattern (helpers.py:892 -> task_ir_helpers.py:97 fg.find_matching_failures -> a Kùzu Cypher MATCH plus get_as_df() at failure_graph.py:315-325), on every turn after a failure; _auto_gather_context (task_ir_helpers.py:63-80) reading up to 10 files via repl._peek on every turn; and the _spill_if_truncated, _persist_solution_file and _record_session_turn file appends. While these run, every other request served by that uvicorn worker (SSE token streams, /health) stalls.
proposal: Collect the synchronous pre-prompt work into one function _gather_turn_inputs(ctx, role) and run it with `await asyncio.to_thread(...)` (or deps.run_blocking from A2-14). Do the same for the post-REPL persistence calls (_persist_solution_file, _record_session_turn), which are fire-and-forget. Order and results are unchanged because each turn is sequential.
verify-note: These calls are synchronous inside async _execute_turn: _auto_gather_context (helpers.py:899), _check_anti_pattern (helpers.py:900, which calls fg.find_matching_failures, task_ir_helpers.py:97) and build_corpus_context (helpers.py:920, a corpus index retrieve). The LLM call itself is offloaded. Correction: _auto_gather_context skips every path already in state.gathered_files (task_ir_helpers.py:68-73), so it reads files on the first turn and for newly named files, not 'up to 10 files on every turn'. _check_anti_pattern runs only after a failure. build_corpus_context returns early for roles that are not lookup-enabled. The stall is real but bounded to these cases.
verification: Test with a deliberately slow FailureGraph stub (time.sleep(0.5)): a concurrent asyncio task's heartbeat must keep ticking (gap < 50 ms) while _execute_turn runs. Run tests/unit/test_graph_helpers.py.

## A2-16 [sev 2] [debuggability/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=10
**Chat lanes catch bare Exception and record client-side bugs as backend transport failures, which can trip the circuit on a healthy server**
files: O:src/backends/llama_server.py:976, O:src/backends/llama_server.py:1949, O:src/backends/llama_server.py:871, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1702, O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:1280
evidence: _infer_chat_completions ends with `except Exception as e: ... failure_stage='transport', failure_reason='request_error'` (976-983), and the streaming variant does the same (1949-1959); neither logs a traceback. A parsing bug or an unexpected server shape is reported as a transport failure, e.g. `int(usage.get('prompt_tokens', 0))` (871) raises TypeError when the server sends "prompt_tokens": null. _call_caching_backend then calls health_tracker.record_failure(backend_url) (inference.py:1702-1704), and ConcurrencyAwareBackend._record_endpoint_result does too (1280-1297). Repeated client bugs therefore open the circuit and fast-fail every request to a healthy server ('Backend unavailable (circuit open)'). The /completion lanes catch only httpx exceptions (619-706, 1301-1433), so the two lanes disagree.
proposal: Catch httpx.HTTPError and json.JSONDecodeError as transport. For any other exception, log.exception and return failure_stage='client', failure_reason='client_parse_error', which _record_endpoint_result and the primitives layer skip for circuit accounting (as they already do for context_overflow). Alternatively let it propagate. Coerce usage fields with a safe _int(v).
verify-note: _infer_chat_completions ends in `except Exception` mapped to failure_stage='transport', failure_reason='request_error' (llama_server.py:976-983), and the stream variant does the same (1949-1959). Neither logs a traceback. `int(usage.get('prompt_tokens', 0))` at 877 raises TypeError on a null. inference.py:1702-1704 and concurrency_aware._record_endpoint_result (1280-1297) both record_failure for non-partial failures, skipping only context_overflow. I also checked that StopIteration from on_chunk is caught before this (1819), so early stops are not misrecorded.
verification: Tests: a MockTransport returning usage.prompt_tokens=null gives no TypeError and prompt_tokens=None; a RuntimeError injected in parse gives failure_reason='client_parse_error' and health_tracker.record_failure is not called. Run tests/unit/test_llama_server.py and test_inference_mixin.py.

## A2-17 [sev 2] [performance/perf] [effort S] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=-20
**The placement poll loop re-reads all of /proc/locks once per (role, region), twice per iteration, from two inconsistent snapshots**
files: O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:1601, O:src/backends/concurrency_aware.py:1641, O:src/runtime/cpu_region_lock.py:571, O:src/runtime/cpu_region_lock.py:714, O:src/runtime/cpu_region_lock.py:817
evidence: Every _dispatch on the production path (PLACEMENT_STATE_MACHINE=1, orchestrator_stack.py:2535) runs at least one poll iteration, and contended requests repeat every 150 ms (1599). Each iteration calls active_region_holders() (1601) and held_regions_by_role() (1641). Each of those calls _current_lock_owner_pids(lock_path) per (role, region), and each such call opens and scans the whole /proc/locks (cpu_region_lock.py:571-591). With the live topology (7 instances, 5 roles, 12 role-region pairs, measured via get_instance_regions()) that is up to 24 full /proc/locks reads per iteration. The docstring at 714 claims 'one /proc/locks scan'. The two calls read separate snapshots, so holders_for_role and same_role_regions can disagree within one placement decision.
proposal: Add read_lock_owner_snapshot() -> dict[inode, list[pid]] (one /proc/locks read) and give both functions an optional `owners` parameter; _dispatch takes one snapshot per poll iteration and passes it to both. Fix the docstring.
verify-note: The poll loop calls active_region_holders() at concurrency_aware.py:1588 (cited as 1601) and held_regions_by_role() at 1641 on every iteration (poll_interval 0.150 s, 1579). Both call _current_lock_owner_pids per (role, region) (cpu_region_lock.py:740, 803), and each call opens and scans all of /proc/locks (571-591). The docstring at 714 says 'one /proc/locks scan'. The cost per iteration is modest (milliseconds), hence severity 2.
verification: Unit test with a fake /proc/locks file (monkeypatch open) asserting one read per poll iteration and identical outputs to the current functions. Run tests/unit/test_scheduling_contention_gate.py, test_concurrency_aware_*.py and the cpu_region_lock tests.

# A3 — orchestrator config/registry/runtime/decisions  (findings kept: 27, refuted: 0, bloat_est: 2700)

This unit (about 46k LOC) works, but it fails open in several places, ignores some of its own reset and override settings, and every new knob has to be written out up to four times. I confirmed five bugs by running them in this container: (1) the stack-priors YAML is re-parsed on every real chat request (238 ms each, from fence_nonserving_route); (2) compile_stack_priors reports "compiled" with zero gaps and zero live roles when scripts.server.stack_manifest fails to import, which defeats the fail-closed kernel guard written in the same function; (3) a vestigial recursion guard in config/validation.py lets two threads that touch it at the same time permanently fill the registry-timeout cache with fallback values; (4) the config hierarchy is declared four times (dataclass, pydantic Settings, a dead _load_from_env fallback, get_config mapping), which has already caused one 503 outage and still silently drops ORCHESTRATOR_TIMEOUTS_WORKER_CODER and ARCHITECT_CRITIC; (5) the typed-decision shadow's "daemon" executor still blocks interpreter exit. Hermeticity problems go beyond fact (a): the registry path is resolved relative to the working directory, and Settings class bodies freeze registry values at import, so reset_config() cannot refresh them (demonstrated). get_instance_regions() also caches {} forever on any topology import error, which silently turns every CPU-region lock into a no-op. Modularity debt sits in the 548-line _launch_runtime_record, a 3,122-line stack_priors.py where 51 import sites use only the ~400-line reader API, seven or more divergent tmp/registry path resolvers, three versions of "active roles", and hand-copied alias tables that disagree with the registry. On bloat: about 2,700 LOC could be removed outright (worker_pool/draft_cache/comfyui with zero callers ~1.4k, about 700 lines of duplicated config plumbing, dead config sections, duplicated study helpers). About 9.1k LOC of offline typed_decisions study code could move out of the production package.

## A3-01 [sev 4] [performance/perf] [effort S] [behav possible] [confirmed] loc_delta=-20
**Stack-priors artifact (107 KB YAML) is re-parsed on every chat request; consumers reinvent ad-hoc caches**
files: O:src/registry/stack_priors.py:263, O:src/registry/stack_priors.py:332, O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/routing_decision.py:121, O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/routing.py:343, O:src/api/routes/chat_delegation_config.py:42, O:src/api/routes/chat_delegation_decision.py:227, O:src/chat_completions_roles.py:163
evidence: load_stack_priors_artifact() (stack_priors.py:263) runs yaml.safe_load(path.read_text()) on every call and has no cache. Every live_* reader calls it (32 import sites use live_stack_role_records). On the request path, _route_request -> fence_nonserving_route (routing.py:343, runs for every non-mock chat request) -> live_stack_role_ids() -> a full parse. Measured in this container: live_stack_role_ids() takes 237.6 ms; orchestration/derived/stack_priors.yaml is 3,238 lines / 107,598 B. yaml.CSafeLoader on the same text takes 26.6 ms. _valid_delegate_roles() (chat_delegation_config.py:42) re-parses as well and is called up to 4 times per delegation decision (chat_delegation_decision.py:227,232,263,267). Other per-request readers: chat_routing.py:305, chat_summarization.py:96, chat_vision.py:76, factual_risk.py:206. Because the loader has no cache, consumers built their own: chat_completions_roles._priors_cache_key (mtime key) and chat_delegation_decision._STACK_PRIOR_ARCHITECT_BUDGET_ROLES_CACHE.
proposal: Put one cache inside load_stack_priors_artifact, keyed by (resolved path, st_mtime_ns, st_size), with a lock. Parse with getattr(yaml, 'CSafeLoader', yaml.SafeLoader). Return a read-only view (types.MappingProxyType at the top level) or document that callers must not mutate; if an audit finds a mutating caller, return copy.deepcopy, which is still about 20x cheaper than parsing. Then delete the consumer-side caches (_priors_cache_key, _STACK_PRIOR_ARCHITECT_BUDGET_ROLES_CACHE) and expose reset_stack_priors_cache() for tests.
verify-note: Reproduced in the venv. load_stack_priors_artifact (stack_priors.py:263) calls yaml.safe_load with no cache. live_stack_role_ids() took 263 ms. yaml.safe_load on stack_priors.yaml (107,598 B) took 245 ms and yaml.CSafeLoader took 27 ms (the venv has libyaml). fence_nonserving_route (routing_decision.py:111-126) calls live_stack_role_ids() on every non-mock request (routing.py:342-346). The consumer-side caches exist as described: chat_completions_roles._priors_cache_key at :163 and chat_delegation_decision._STACK_PRIOR_ARCHITECT_BUDGET_ROLES_CACHE at :459. One correction: _valid_delegate_roles() runs at most twice per parse, not four times. The I| branch (227/232) and the JSON branch (263/267) are mutually exclusive.
verification: New unit test: patch yaml loader, call live_stack_role_ids() twice and assert one parse; os.utime bump and assert a reparse. Run tests/unit/test_stack_priors*.py, test_chat_completions_roles*.py, test_chat_delegation*.py, test_routing_decision*.py. Microbenchmark: live_stack_role_ids() 238 ms -> <1 ms when warm.

## A3-02 [sev 4] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=-4
**compile_stack_priors fails open: an import error in scripts.server compiles 'status: compiled', 0 gaps, 0 live roles**
files: O:src/registry/stack_priors.py:1108, O:src/registry/stack_priors.py:1125, O:src/registry/stack_priors.py:2030, O:src/registry/stack_priors.py:2052, O:src/registry/stack_priors.py:3088, O:src/registry/registry_validator.py:207
evidence: _stack_manifest_info() wraps `from scripts.server.stack_manifest import ...` in `except Exception: return {}, {}` (line 1125). _launch_runtime_record() does the same with `except Exception: return {}` (line 2052). scripts.server.stack_paths resolves the kernel store at import (stack_paths.py:54 -> kernel_paths.backend_dir raises KernelPathError), and stack_manifest validates host capacity and lean parity at import (fact a). Run here: `compile_stack_priors()` with the default allow_incomplete=False returned status='compiled', known_global_gaps={}, validate_stack_priors_contract()==[], and 0 of 16 roles live_stack (all 'benchmark_or_candidate', 0 launch entries, runtime={}). This silently defeats the FAIL CLOSED rationale written inside the same function (lines ~2062-2080: 'a dangling production/<backend> symlink ... compiled a GPU role onto a CPU build, silently'). A dangling production/cpu makes the earlier import raise, and that error is swallowed. registry_validator.py:207 states the repo's own rule: 'no `except: return {}` — a cross-check that silently degrades ... passes hardest exactly when the topology is broken'. Separately, main() (line ~3088) compiles twice on a write: compile_stack_priors(), then write_stack_priors() compiles again, and the printed and written compiled_at differ.
proposal: Delete both broad excepts. Let ImportError, KernelPathError and ValueError from scripts.server.* propagate as StackPriorsCompileError({'*': ['stack manifest unavailable: <exc>']}) so strict compiles refuse. Under allow_incomplete, record the error in known_global_gaps instead of returning empty structures. In main(), call write_stack_priors once and print from its return value.
CORRECTED: As proposed, but drop the compiled_at claim. The main() fix is only to avoid the redundant compile: call write_stack_priors() once and print len(result['roles']).
verify-note: Reproduced. In this container `import scripts.server.stack_manifest` raises ValueError (host RAM oversubscribed by 302.74 GiB). Even so, compile_stack_priors() with allow_incomplete=False returned status='compiled', known_global_gaps={}, 16 roles all 'benchmark_or_candidate', and validate_stack_priors_contract()==[]. Both broad excepts are present: `except Exception: return {}, {}` at :1124-1125 and `except Exception: return {}` at :2051-2052. The FAIL CLOSED comment at :2063-2071 sits in the same function. One side claim is wrong: main() (:3079-3119) prints only the role count, never compiled_at, so 'printed and written compiled_at differ' is not observable. The double compile on a write path is real but only wastes work.
verification: New test: monkeypatch sys.modules['scripts.server.stack_manifest'] to raise on import, then assert compile_stack_priors() raises StackPriorsCompileError and that allow_incomplete=True yields a known_global_gaps entry. On host: `uv run python scripts/registry/stack_change_pipeline.py check` must stay byte-identical. Run tests/unit/test_stack_priors_compiler.py and test_stack_change_pipeline*.py.

## A3-03 [sev 4] [bloat/refactor] [effort M] [behav intended] [partially] loc_delta=-700
**Configuration is declared four times (dataclass + pydantic Settings + dead _load_from_env + get_config mapping): about 830 lines of plumbing**
files: O:src/config/__init__.py:87, O:src/config/__init__.py:451, O:src/config/__init__.py:723, O:src/config/__init__.py:140, O:src/config/models.py:1005, O:pyproject.toml:15
evidence: Each of about 160 fields is written in the dataclass (models.py), again in 14 BaseSettings classes (__init__.py:87-446, ~360 lines), again in _load_from_env (451-701, ~250 lines), and again in get_config's field-by-field copy (723-938, ~216 lines). pydantic-settings is a hard dependency (pyproject.toml:15), so _load_from_env never runs in production; its only caller is tests/unit/test_config_consolidation.py:882. The progress log 2026-02-19 shows the cost: '+2 fields in ChatPipelineConfig, ChatPipelineSettings, _load_from_env, get_config'. The in-code comment at __init__.py:888-911 documents the outage this caused: architect_critic was added to the dataclass but not to Settings, so every real_mode request returned 503. A further cost: each of the 19 ServerURLsSettings default_factory lambdas builds a whole ServerURLsConfig() (line 140), so one get_config() makes 361 _server_url_default calls for 19 fields (measured).
proposal: Make the dataclasses the only schema. Add src/config/env_overlay.py with apply_env(obj, prefix) that recurses over dataclasses.fields, reads ORCHESTRATOR_<SECTION>_<FIELD> via src.env_parsing for int/float/bool/str/Optional[float]/Path, and keeps a two-entry legacy alias table (chat.try_cheap_first_q_threshold <- ..._QUALITY_THRESHOLD; memrl_retrieval.cost_tau <- DAR_COST_TAU). get_config() then becomes `apply_env(OrchestratorConfigData(), 'ORCHESTRATOR_')`. Delete the Settings classes, _load_from_env, _build_server_urls and the mapping block. Note: env_bool accepts {1,true,yes,on}, while pydantic also accepts y/t/off; widen env_bool or document the change.
CORRECTED: Keep the dataclasses as the single schema. Apply the env overlay only to the 14 sections that have Settings classes today, using an explicit {section: prefix} table (features -> 'ORCHESTRATOR_', others -> 'ORCHESTRATOR_<SECTION>_'). Leave paths/vision/delegation/services on their own default factories. The overlay must RAISE (ConfigError naming the variable) on unparsable int/float/bool values, matching today's pydantic ValidationError, rather than reusing env_int/env_float's silent fallback. Keep the two legacy aliases. Write the golden asdict(get_config()) test first, including one malformed-value case that must raise.
verify-note: The 4x declaration is real. There are 15 BaseSettings classes (:91-441). _load_from_env (:451-701) is reached only via `else` at :938 and from tests/unit/test_config_consolidation.py:882, and pydantic-settings is a hard dependency (pyproject.toml:15). The get_config mapping runs :723-938. I measured 361 _server_url_default calls per get_config(), but they cost about 15 ms warm because the stack-priors URL cache exists, so that point is a curiosity, not a cost. The proposed overlay is NOT behavior-preserving as written, for four reasons. (1) `apply_env(OrchestratorConfigData(), 'ORCHESTRATOR_')` would make paths/vision/delegation/services/worker_pool newly env-overridable under names those dataclasses don't use today (VisionConfig reads ORCHESTRATOR_PATHS_VISION_DIR, not ORCHESTRATOR_VISION_BASE_DIR). (2) FeaturesSettings uses the bare ORCHESTRATOR_ prefix, not ORCHESTRATOR_FEATURES_. (3) Pydantic fails loud on malformed values: ORCHESTRATOR_LLM_OUTPUT_CAP=abc raises ValidationError, while src/env_parsing.env_int/env_float return the default on ValueError (lines 17-20, 42-45), so a naive overlay turns fail-closed into silent defaults. (4) Pydantic env matching is case-insensitive. On the plus side, the refactor would structurally fix the ORCHESTRATOR_REPL/CHAT/SERVER collision crash (see missed A3-M1).
verification: Before refactoring, write a golden test that dumps dataclasses.asdict(get_config()) to JSON for (a) an empty env and (b) every ORCHESTRATOR_<SECTION>_<FIELD> set to a type-appropriate sentinel. After refactoring, diff the JSON; the only diffs allowed are the A3-04 fixes. Run tests/unit/test_config.py, test_config_consolidation.py and test_config_validation.py.

## A3-07 [sev 3] [tests/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=-5
**Pydantic Settings class bodies freeze registry values at import, so reset_config() cannot refresh them (hermeticity)**
files: O:src/config/__init__.py:101, O:src/config/__init__.py:220, O:src/config/__init__.py:941
evidence: LLMSettings evaluates _registry_runtime_value(...) in its class body (lines 101-105), and TimeoutsSettings runs `_timeout_defaults = TimeoutsConfig()` at class creation (line 220). Both execute once, at `import src.config`, using whatever CWD and env exist at that moment. Demonstrated: after import, I pointed ORCHESTRATOR_PATHS_REGISTRY_PATH at a registry with frontdoor=4242, architect_general=4343, depth_override_max_depth=9, then called reset_validation_caches() and reset_config(). get_config() still returned 180/600/3, while TimeoutsConfig()/LLMConfig() returned 4242/4343/9. reset_config()'s docstring ('Call this if environment variables change during runtime') is therefore false. Its only callers are 14 test files, so tests that set registry env after import silently test the import-time registry. Combined with A3-06, an import from the wrong CWD freezes fallback timeouts for the life of the process.
proposal: Move registry-derived defaults into PydanticField(default_factory=...) (or remove the Settings layer entirely per A3-03) so they are evaluated on each get_config(). Make reset_config() also call reset_validation_caches() so the timeout cache is reset too.
verify-note: Reproduced with a scratch registry (frontdoor=4242, architect_general=4343, depth_override_max_depth=9). After setting ORCHESTRATOR_PATHS_REGISTRY_PATH and calling reset_validation_caches() then reset_config(), get_config() still returned 180/600/3, while TimeoutsConfig()/LLMConfig() returned 4242/4343/9. reset_config() (:941-948) calls only reset_runtime_defaults_cache(), not the timeouts reset. It has no callers in src/ or scripts/, so production is unaffected, but tests that patch the registry after import silently exercise the import-time values. That matters for the test-hermeticity work.
verification: Turn the demo into a unit test in tests/unit/test_config_validation.py (tmp registry YAML + reset_config + assert new values). Run the full tests/unit/test_config*.py.

## A3-08 [sev 3] [debuggability/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=3
**get_instance_regions() caches {} forever on any topology import error, so all CPU-region locks become no-ops**
files: O:src/runtime/instance_topology.py:401, O:src/runtime/instance_topology.py:417, O:src/runtime/cpu_region_lock.py:1219, O:scripts/server/stack_numa.py:472
evidence: get_instance_regions() wraps `from scripts.server.stack_numa import NUMA_CONFIG` in `except Exception: _INSTANCE_REGIONS_CACHE = {}`, with no log, and the empty dict is cached for the process lifetime (line 417). stack_numa deliberately raises AssertionError/ValueError/FileNotFoundError at import for a malformed stack_topology.yaml (stack_numa.py:301-472). cpu_region_lock_for_instance then looks up `.get((role, idx), frozenset())`, and cpu_region_lock() treats an empty set as 'no-op context' (cpu_region_lock.py:918-922). One topology typo during a stack change followed by an API reload therefore silently disables every region lock until restart. contention.py:627 and cpu_region_lock.py:722/780/839 also read this mapping.
proposal: Do not cache failures. Log at ERROR with exc_info and re-raise (callers on the dispatch path already handle CpuRegionLockTimeout). If degraded operation is truly wanted, return {} without caching and expose the failure via a health/diagnostics field, so the next call retries.
verify-note: The code matches the finding: instance_topology.py:411-416 does `except Exception: _INSTANCE_REGIONS_CACHE = {}` with no log, and the comment justifies it by 'import path differs'. cpu_region_lock.py:918-922 treats empty regions as a no-op `yield {}`. stack_numa raises ValueError/AssertionError/FileNotFoundError at import for a malformed topology (stack_numa.py:301, 412-456). Every src import of stack_numa is lazy, so the API can boot with stack_numa broken and silently cache {} for the life of the process.
verification: Unit test: monkeypatch the import to raise, then assert get_instance_regions() raises (or logs and does not cache). Run tests/unit/test_instance_topology*.py, test_cpu_region_lock*.py and test_contention*.py.

## A3-09 [sev 3] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=-40
**typed-decision shadow 'daemon' executor still blocks interpreter exit; it mirrors private CPython internals**
files: O:src/typed_decisions/shadow.py:114, O:src/typed_decisions/shadow.py:134, O:src/typed_decisions/shadow.py:172, O:tests/unit/test_typed_decisions_shadow.py:443
evidence: _DaemonThreadPoolExecutor overrides the private ThreadPoolExecutor._adjust_thread_count and inspects the signature of the private concurrent.futures.thread._worker (_daemon_worker_mode, lines 114-131) to start a daemon=True worker. It then registers that thread in impl._threads_queues (line 172). CPython's _python_exit (registered via threading._register_atexit) puts None and calls t.join() on every thread in _threads_queues, whether or not it is a daemon. Probe: submitting time.sleep(4) and exiting took 4.42 s against a 0.59 s import-only baseline, so exit blocked on the in-flight item. In production that item is an LLM call (worker_general, call timeout up to 600 s) whenever typed_decisions_shadow is on. The test only asserts thread.daemon (line 443-464), not the property that matters.
proposal: Replace the ~60 lines of stdlib mirroring with a plain bounded worker: queue.Queue(maxsize=MAX_PENDING), and one threading.Thread(target=_drain, daemon=True, name='td-shadow') started lazily under _executor_lock. A full queue increments _dropped. drain_shadow() puts a sentinel and joins with a timeout. This removes the inspect/weakref/_threads_queues coupling.
verify-note: I wrote a probe script that imports _DaemonThreadPoolExecutor, submits time.sleep(4) and exits. On Python 3.13 the mode is 'legacy', thread.daemon is True, and the process took 4.48 s wall time to exit. The thread is registered in impl._threads_queues (shadow.py:172), so concurrent.futures' atexit _python_exit joins it, contradicting the docstring's promise (shadow.py:21-22) that 'a stuck shadow call can never hold interpreter shutdown'. The test at tests/unit/test_typed_decisions_shadow.py:443-464 asserts only thread.daemon.
verification: New subprocess test: submit a 3 s fake shadow task, exit, and assert wall time < 1.5 s. Run tests/unit/test_typed_decisions_shadow.py and test_conversation_shadow.py.

It is validated against a pinned workflow revision (ap_me1_me3_capture.py:286-291), so it does not break now but must be regenerated at the next pin bump. (4) The header of scripts/diffusion/start_comfyui_server.sh still says it is invoked by start_comfyui(), which no longer exists. ## A3-11 [sev 3] [modularity/refactor] [effort M] [behav none] [partially] loc_delta=-60
**_launch_runtime_record is a 548-line, CC-86 god function with duplicated spec branches**
files: O:src/registry/stack_priors.py:2017, O:src/registry/stack_priors.py:2227, O:src/registry/stack_priors.py:2330, O:src/registry/stack_priors.py:2477
evidence: One function does binary/LD path resolution (2063-2125), slot resolution with about 50 comment lines (2127-2190), kv types, speculative-decoding config, context tokens, ubatch/batch, and a ~140-line literal return dict that computes about 25 values inline. The worker-explore branch (2227-2282) and the external-drafter branch (2283-2370) build the same 13-key spec dict, each with identical ngram_mod_n_min/max/match and draft_p_min/p_split expressions. _draft_kv_types_prior(server_cfg, role_cfg) is called twice for the k and v fields (2477-2478). stack_priors.py is the top-churn file in my scope (13 commits since 2026-08-01), so every edit touches this function. Rationale comments (incident history) are interleaved with logic, which buries the control flow.
proposal: Introduce a frozen _LaunchInputs dataclass (role, primary_role, mode, worker_type, vision_type, requirements, acceleration, server_cfg, role_cfg, launch). Extract _resolve_binary(inputs) -> (binary_path, binary_dir, ld_paths, family, explicit_override); _resolve_slots(inputs) -> (slots, lane_shape); _resolve_spec(inputs, models_dir) with a shared _spec_tuning_fields(acceleration) for the common keys; _resolve_context_tokens(inputs, lane_shape); _cache_record(...) and _flags_record(...). Move each incident rationale into the docstring of the helper that enforces it, keeping the text. _launch_runtime_record becomes about 40 lines of composition.
CORRECTED: Extract only the genuinely shared fields (draft_min, draft_p_split, ngram_mod_n_min/max/match) into _spec_tuning_fields(acceleration), and keep each branch's draft_max/draft_p_min/type semantics explicit. The rest of the decomposition (_resolve_binary, _resolve_slots, ...) stands. Make the golden-dict test cover a worker-explore role, an external-drafter role and an alias role, because these branches are exactly where a 'harmless' merge would change output.
verify-note: The function at stack_priors.py:2017-2566 is 548 lines with CC 86 per the metrics, and the file has 13 commits since 2026-08-01. The claim that the two spec branches build 'identical' expressions is wrong. In the worker-explore branch (2236-2282), draft_max = _positive_int_prior(fallback=2), draft_p_min = _number_prior(fallback=0.0) unconditionally, and type falls back to 'draft-mtp'. In the external-drafter branch (2283-2365), draft_max is the raw int or None, draft_p_min is None when the key is absent, and the branch adds n_gpu_layers_draft. Only draft_min, draft_p_split and the three ngram_mod_* fields are shared. The _draft_kv_types_prior double call is at about :2482, not 2477-2478.
verification: Golden: compile_stack_priors(allow_incomplete=True) on the checked-in registry fixtures, before and after, must give identical dicts; on host, `scripts/registry/stack_change_pipeline.py check` must report fresh. Run tests/unit/test_stack_priors_compiler.py, test_external_drafter_launch.py, test_stackchg_8083_batch.py and test_stackchg_8083_idle_slots.py.

## A3-14 [sev 3] [modularity/refactor] [effort M] [behav possible] [confirmed] loc_delta=-60
**Tmp-dir and registry-path resolution is reimplemented in 7+ places with four different precedence orders**
files: O:src/runtime/cpu_region_lock.py:86, O:src/runtime/gpu_window_executor.py:164, O:src/runtime/live_telemetry.py:418, O:src/runtime/quiescence.py:148, O:src/runtime/inference_tap.py:64, O:src/runtime/config_attestation.py:13, O:src/services/document_preprocessor.py:55, O:src/config/models.py:550
evidence: cpu_region_lock._tmp_dir, gpu_window_executor._tmp_dir and live_telemetry._telemetry_root use ORCHESTRATOR_TMP_DIR -> ORCHESTRATOR_PATHS_TMP_DIR -> literal /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp, and ignore ORCHESTRATOR_PATHS_LLM_ROOT. quiescence.hold_dir, inference_tap._get_sentinel, config_attestation.attestation_dir and document_preprocessor._get_tmp_dir use get_config().paths.tmp_dir, which honours PATHS_TMP_DIR and LLM_ROOT but ignores ORCHESTRATOR_TMP_DIR, and they silently fall back to /mnt/raid0 on any exception (_get_sentinel caches that fallback forever). models._bootstrap_runtime_selected_servers reads $TMPDIR (line 550), while the runtime-facts writer uses stack_paths._PATHS['tmp_dir']. scripts/server/stack_paths._get_paths is a second PathsConfig. With ORCHESTRATOR_TMP_DIR set (the documented test override), region locks and the FIFO queue land in one directory while quiescence holds and the tap sentinel land in another. Registry-path copies are listed in A3-06. 104 hardcoded /mnt/raid0 literals in src, 42 of them in this unit.
proposal: Create a stdlib-only src/paths.py with no import cycle into config: llm_root(), project_root(), tmp_dir() (one precedence: ORCHESTRATOR_TMP_DIR > ORCHESTRATOR_PATHS_TMP_DIR > llm_root()/tmp), lean_registry_path() and stack_priors_path(). Make PathsConfig default factories, stack_paths._get_paths and all the runtime helpers above delegate to it. Delete the per-module _tmp_dir copies and the broad-except /mnt/raid0 fallbacks.
verify-note: Verified each resolver. cpu_region_lock._tmp_dir (:84), gpu_window_executor._tmp_dir (:164-169) and live_telemetry._telemetry_root (:418-425) use ORCHESTRATOR_TMP_DIR -> ORCHESTRATOR_PATHS_TMP_DIR -> literal /mnt/raid0/llm/tmp. quiescence.hold_dir (:148-157) and inference_tap._get_sentinel (:64-72; it caches the fallback forever) use get_config().paths.tmp_dir with a broad-except raid fallback. models.py:550 reads $TMPDIR for runtime facts. cpu_region_lock deliberately avoids importing src.config (docstring), so the shared resolver must be stdlib-only, which the proposal already says. The tmp-override hazard is real: orchestrator_stack.py:2433-2440 records a 21 h production incident caused by an inherited ORCHESTRATOR_TMP_DIR. I count 44 /mnt/raid0 lines in the unit, including comments.
verification: Parametrized test over env combinations, asserting every consumer (cpu_region_lock.region_lock_path, quiescence.hold_dir, inference_tap._get_sentinel, live_telemetry._telemetry_root, attestation_dir) resolves under the same root. Run tests/unit/test_cpu_region_lock*.py, test_oab3_quiescence_witness.py, test_inference_tap.py and test_lock_fifo_queue.py.

## A3-15 [sev 3] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=5
**document_client.ocr_pdf_with_partial_success ignores its page_timeout, renders every page up front and leaks the PdfDocument**
files: O:src/services/document_client.py:290, O:src/services/document_client.py:320, O:src/services/document_client.py:332, O:src/services/document_client.py:41, O:src/api/routes/chat_vision.py:748
evidence: vulture reports 'unused variable page_timeout (100% confidence)'. The parameter is documented as 'Timeout per page in seconds' (line 305), but process_page calls self.ocr_image(image, output_format), which always uses the module constant SINGLE_PAGE_TIMEOUT, frozen at import (line 41). Pages are all rendered to PIL at 200 DPI before any OCR starts (lines 325-332): up to max_pages=100 x ~11 MB = ~1.1 GB resident on the chat_vision path (chat_vision.py:748). pdfium.PdfDocument(path) (line 320) is never closed.
proposal: Add a `timeout: float | None = None` parameter to ocr_image and pass page_timeout through. Render each page inside process_page under the semaphore, so at most max_concurrent bitmaps are alive. Wrap the document in try/finally pdf.close().
verify-note: page_timeout is accepted (:290) but never used. ocr_image uses the import-frozen SINGLE_PAGE_TIMEOUT (:41, used at about :218). All pages are rendered to PIL before OCR starts (:325-332). The function is called on the chat_vision upload path (chat_vision.py:748). One overstatement: pypdfium2's PdfDocument has a GC finalizer, so it is not a true leak, only a non-deterministic close. Explicit close is still right.
verification: Unit test with a fake transport that asserts the per-request timeout equals page_timeout. A memory test with tracemalloc on a 20-page synthetic PDF should show peak ~max_concurrent pages. Run tests/unit/test_document_client*.py.

## A3-M1 [sev 3] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [verifier-added] loc_delta=15
**Documented ORCHESTRATOR_REPL=1 (and ORCHESTRATOR_CHAT/SERVER/LLM/...=1) crashes get_config() via nested-Settings env collision**
files: O:src/config/__init__.py:413, O:src/config/__init__.py:441, O:src/features.py:30, O:docs/chapters/01-runtime-environment.md:23
evidence: OrchestratorSettings (env_prefix='ORCHESTRATOR_') declares nested BaseSettings fields named llm, escalation, repl, server, monitor, features, server_urls, timeouts, chat, memrl_retrieval, think_harder, session_persistence, session_lifecycle and health_tracker. pydantic-settings therefore reads ORCHESTRATOR_<FIELD> as a JSON value for the whole sub-model. Reproduced here: ORCHESTRATOR_REPL=1, ORCHESTRATOR_REPL=true, ORCHESTRATOR_CHAT=1 and ORCHESTRATOR_SERVER=1 each make get_config() raise `ValidationError ... Input should be a valid dictionary or instance of REPLSettings`. ORCHESTRATOR_TOOLS=1 works because tools is not a nested field. src/features.py:30 and docs/chapters/01-runtime-environment.md:23 still tell operators to set ORCHESTRATOR_REPL=1. This collision has already taken the API down twice (epyc-root progress/2026-02/2026-02-01.md:379-385 and progress/2026-06/2026-06-12.md:255,264), and the only fix so far has been comments saying 'do NOT set ORCHESTRATOR_REPL' (orchestrator_stack.py:2559).
proposal: Stopgap: remove the ORCHESTRATOR_REPL lines from features.py and the docs chapter, document ORCHESTRATOR_FEATURE_REPL instead, and add a unit test parametrized over every nested section name that sets ORCHESTRATOR_<SECTION>=1 and asserts get_config() succeeds or raises a clear ConfigError naming the variable. Structural: A3-03's dataclass overlay with per-section prefixes and no nested BaseSettings removes the collision class entirely.
verify-note: 
verification: New test fails today on repl/chat/server and passes after the fix. Run tests/unit/test_config*.py and test_runtime_flags.py.

## A3-04 [sev 2] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=6
**Config drift silently drops env overrides: TIMEOUTS_WORKER_CODER ignored, ARCHITECT_CRITIC unsupported, default_request 600 vs 120**
files: O:src/config/__init__.py:226, O:src/config/__init__.py:812, O:src/config/__init__.py:575, O:src/config/models.py:1124, O:src/config/models.py:1184
evidence: Ran with ORCHESTRATOR_TIMEOUTS_WORKER_CODER=99, ORCHESTRATOR_TIMEOUTS_ARCHITECT_CRITIC=77 and ORCHESTRATOR_TIMEOUTS_WORKER_FAST=55. get_config().timeouts gave worker_coder=30, architect_critic=600, worker_fast=55, and for_role('worker_coder')=30; _load_from_env() gave worker_coder=99. Cause: TimeoutsSettings declares worker_coder (line 226), but the get_config TimeoutsConfig(...) call (line 812) never passes it, and neither Settings nor _load_from_env has architect_critic. default_request also differs by path: the dataclass reads _registry_timeout('', 'default', 600) -> 600, while _load_from_env reads ('roles','default_request',120) -> 120. TimeoutsConfig._timeout_role_map maps 'worker_explore' to self.worker_general (models.py:1184), so the worker_explore field and its env var have no effect. The module docstring promises 'All settings can be overridden via environment variables'.
proposal: If A3-03 lands, these go away structurally. Before that, a 3-line stopgap: add worker_coder and architect_critic to the get_config mapping and Settings, use 'worker_explore': self.worker_explore, and pick a single registry key for default_request. Add a parity test asserting set(TimeoutsSettings.model_fields) == {f.name for f in fields(TimeoutsConfig)} and that every field is mapped.
verify-note: Reproduced with ORCHESTRATOR_TIMEOUTS_WORKER_CODER=99, ARCHITECT_CRITIC=77, WORKER_FAST=55, WORKER_EXPLORE=44. get_config() returned worker_coder=30, architect_critic=600, worker_fast=55, worker_explore=44, for_role('worker_explore')=60, default_request=600. _load_from_env returned worker_coder=99 and default_request=120. The TimeoutsConfig(...) mapping at :812-838 omits worker_coder, and architect_critic appears in neither Settings nor _load_from_env. I lowered the severity because no launcher, script or doc in any of the three repos sets an ORCHESTRATOR_TIMEOUTS_* variable; the grep found only DEFAULT_REQUEST in the config docstring. Registry values still flow through the dataclass defaults, so only env overrides are lost.
verification: Parity test as described, plus an env-override test per timeout field. Run tests/unit/test_config*.py.

## A3-06 [sev 2] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=0
**Registry runtime defaults are read from a CWD-relative path; run from any other directory, every registry timeout silently falls back**
files: O:src/config/validation.py:166, O:src/config/models.py:910, O:src/registry/registry_loader.py:42, O:scripts/lib/env.sh:134
evidence: validation.py:166-171 defaults to Path(os.environ.get('ORCHESTRATOR_PATHS_REGISTRY_PATH', 'orchestration/model_registry.yaml')), a path relative to the working directory. PathsConfig.registry_path (models.py:910) is deliberately anchored to __file__ ('control-plane reads must follow the code, not the host', models.py:260). Run from cwd=/tmp: _registry_timeout('roles','architect_general',-1) returned -1 with status 'missing_registry', and the only signal is a diagnostics dict. Only scripts/lib/env.sh:134 sets the env var, so direct python invocations (epyc-root scripts/harness importing src.inference_lock, ad-hoc pytest from another dir) get hardcoded timeouts. The same file is also defined in stack_priors.DEFAULT_REGISTRY, model_descriptors.DEFAULT_LEAN_REGISTRY, scheduling/device_model.py:71, autopilot_core/verification_families.py:49, registry_validator.py:449 and registry_compiler.py:453. registry_loader.py:296-301 falls back to DEFAULT_REGISTRY_PATH, which is the MASTER research registry (/mnt/raid0/llm/epyc-inference-research/...), not the lean registry the config uses.
proposal: Resolve the default via models._get_default_project_root() (or the shared src/paths.py from A3-14): `Path(os.environ.get(..., f'{project_root}/orchestration/model_registry.yaml'))`. Make RegistryLoader's fallback DEFAULT_LEAN_REGISTRY_PATH. Log a WARNING (not only diagnostics) when the registry is missing.
verify-note: validation.py:166-171 defaults to the CWD-relative 'orchestration/model_registry.yaml'. Run from cwd=/tmp, _registry_timeout('roles','architect_general',-1) returned -1 with runtime_defaults status 'missing'. With the registry missing, TimeoutsConfig differs on frontdoor (180 -> 90) and vision_escalation (60 -> 120). PathsConfig.registry_path (models.py:910-916) is __file__-anchored. RegistryLoader falls back to DEFAULT_RESEARCH_REGISTRY (registry_loader.py:42-45, 296-301). I lowered the severity because the production API is started by orchestrator_stack.py via Popen(..., cwd=str(_PATHS['project_root'])) at about line 2655, so the live path resolves correctly. The impact is limited to ad-hoc scripts, pytest from another directory, and importers from other repos.
verification: Test: chdir(tmp_path), reset_validation_caches(), and assert _registry_timeout('roles','frontdoor',-1) != -1. Run tests/unit/test_config_validation.py and test_registry_loader*.py.

Compiler symbols appear 5 times: compile_stack_priors x2, write_stack_priors, main, validate_stack_priors_contract. After `import src.registry_loader`, sys.modules['src.registry.registry_loader'].__name__ == 'src.registry_loader'. The pyproject entry point at :133 uses the shim. The import counts are overstated: src.registry_loader appears in 38 files (18 in src/scripts), src.executor in 5, script_registry in 4, concurrency in 3. The fix is unaffected.

## A3-17 [sev 2] [modularity/refactor] [effort S] [behav possible] [partially] loc_delta=-25
**Three diverging implementations of 'active roles from ROLE_LAUNCH_META'; one creates a duplicate module instance**
files: O:src/registry/registry_compiler.py:250, O:src/registry/model_descriptors.py:1389, O:scripts/registry/stack_change_pipeline.py:589, O:orchestration/launch_manifest.yaml:309
evidence: registry_compiler.active_roles_from_launch_meta skips launcher_only, adds aliases and adds launcher_tenant_roles. model_descriptors._roles_from_stack_manifest (line 1389) has the same logic minus tenant roles. It also runs sys.path.insert(0, 'scripts/server') on every call and does `from stack_manifest import ROLE_LAUNCH_META`, which loads a second copy of the module under the top-level name 'stack_manifest' and re-runs its import-time validation. stack_change_pipeline._roles_from_stack_manifest (line 589) does not skip launcher_only, so it includes eval_batch_frontdoor, which launch_manifest.yaml:307 says 'keeps this warm process out of lean-registry and descriptor compile inputs'. As a result, `python -m src.registry.model_descriptors` and the pipeline's descriptor step compile different role sets for the same output file.
proposal: Keep one function, active_roles_from_launch_meta(meta, *, include_launcher_only_keys=False, include_tenants=True), in registry_compiler. Replace both copies with calls to it and import via scripts.server.stack_manifest only (drop the sys.path insert).
CORRECTED: As proposed, but justify it as removing a latent divergence rather than a current output mismatch. Before and after, also run `python -m src.registry.model_descriptors --dry-run` and the pipeline descriptor step on the host and diff the two.
verify-note: The three implementations exist and diverge as described. registry_compiler.active_roles_from_launch_meta (:250) skips launcher_only and adds tenants. model_descriptors._roles_from_stack_manifest (:1389) skips launcher_only, adds no tenants, and does sys.path.insert plus a bare `from stack_manifest import`, which creates a second module instance. stack_change_pipeline._roles_from_stack_manifest (:589) does not skip launcher_only, so eval_batch_frontdoor is included. But the claim that the two paths 'compile different role sets for the same output file' is unproven: eval_batch_frontdoor appears in neither the lean registry nor the master registry, and _resolve_role_dependencies ignores names that have no roles/server_mode entry. Divergent inputs are confirmed; divergent outputs are not. The sys.path path runs only in model_descriptors' CLI main().
verification: Unit test feeding a synthetic ROLE_LAUNCH_META with launcher_only and tenant entries through all call sites. On host: `stack_change_pipeline.py check` stays fresh. Run tests/unit/test_registry_compiler*.py and test_model_descriptors*.py.

## A3-18 [sev 2] [modularity/refactor] [effort S] [behav possible] [partially] loc_delta=-30
**Hand-copied alias and host-role tables disagree with the registry they restate**
files: O:src/config/models.py:374, O:src/config/models.py:381, O:src/config/models.py:390, O:src/api/routes/health.py:24, O:scripts/server/stack_env.py:134, O:src/registry/registry_loader.py:1009
evidence: _RUNTIME_SELECTED_ROLE_ALIASES (models.py:374) has a byte-identical copy in api/routes/health.py:24. The comment at line 381 claims a third 'identical' table in scripts/server/stack_env.py, but that file only has {'worker': 'worker_general'}. Compared with registry_loader.serving_host_by_role(load_server_mode()), the hand table maps worker_explore/worker_math/toolrunner to worker_general (itself an alias), while the registry maps them to frontdoor, and the hand table omits ingest_long_context -> architect_critic. _BOOTSTRAP_QUARTERABLE_HOST_ROLES = {frontdoor, worker_general, ingest_long_context} (line 390), but the derived _quarterable_host_roles() returns {'frontdoor'} (measured). So the bootstrap producer requires a listening port for the ingest_long_context group (the MI210 :8083 process) and rejects a valid lineup whenever the GPU server is down. _LEGACY_SERVER_URL_FALLBACKS already calls itself the 'SIXTH surface that restates shared_with'; these tables are seventh and eighth.

CORRECTED: As proposed, but restate the impact as 'the bootstrap producer falls through whenever the GPU host is down'. Add the parity test against serving_host_by_role, and fix or delete the false lockstep comment.
verify-note: Measured. serving_host_by_role(load_server_mode()) maps worker_explore/worker_math/toolrunner/worker_general/worker/worker_summarize -> frontdoor and ingest_long_context -> architect_critic. The hand table maps three of those to worker_general and omits ingest_long_context. _quarterable_host_roles() returns {'frontdoor'}, while _BOOTSTRAP_QUARTERABLE_HOST_ROLES has three entries. health.py:24 is a copy. stack_env.py:134 has only {'worker':'worker_general'}, so the 'identical tables' comment at models.py:381-382 is false. The consequence is overstated: when the ingest_long_context group has no listening port, _bootstrap_runtime_selected_servers (models.py:548-640) returns None, and _runtime_or_env_selected_servers falls through to producer 1 (validated runtime facts), then producer 2 and stack priors. The lineup is not rejected; only the bootstrap fast path is lost while :8083 is down.
verification: Parity test asserting the derived map equals the expected host map for the checked-in registry. Run tests/unit/test_config.py::TestServerURLsConfig, test_full_slot_demotion.py and test_health*.py.

## A3-19 [sev 2] [debuggability/perf] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=5
**chat_template_kwargs_for_role retries a 0.38 s registry parse on every request after a failure, silently**
files: O:src/registry/registry_loader.py:952, O:src/backends/llama_server.py:162, O:src/backends/openai.py:279, O:src/runtime/inference_tap.py:64
evidence: chat_template_kwargs_for_role caches only success: `if _CTK_LOADER is None: try: _CTK_LOADER = RegistryLoader(validate_paths=False) except Exception: return None`. It is called per /v1/chat/completions request (llama_server.py:162, openai.py:279). If the registry fails to load, every request re-parses the 3,046-line registry (measured 0.38 s with the pure-Python loader) and returns None with no log, so per-role chat_template_kwargs such as enable_thinking=false are silently dropped. inference_tap._get_sentinel shows the opposite failure: it permanently caches the /mnt/raid0 fallback after one failed get_config().
proposal: Add a small helper in src/registry (cached_or_backoff(loader, retry_after_s=30)) that caches the result or the failure with a timestamp and logs the failure once at WARNING. Use it for _CTK_LOADER, _get_sentinel and instance_topology (A3-08).
verify-note: registry_loader.py:951-966 caches success only and returns None silently on construction failure. It is called per request from llama_server.py:162 and openai.py:279. In this container, RegistryLoader(validate_paths=False) took 1.57 s cold and yaml.safe_load of the registry took 0.25 s, so a failing registry costs that much on every request with no log. inference_tap._get_sentinel caches its fallback permanently (:64-72).

## A3-05 [sev 1] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [partially] loc_delta=-15
**Registry-defaults caches are poisoned by concurrent first access (vestigial recursion guard)**
files: O:src/config/validation.py:31, O:src/config/validation.py:66, O:src/config/validation.py:92, O:src/config/validation.py:159
evidence: _load_registry_runtime_defaults and _load_registry_timeouts guard against recursion with module-global booleans (_LOADING_RUNTIME_DEFAULTS, _LOADING_REGISTRY_TIMEOUTS; 9 global statements). Neither function can actually recurse: the loader only does os.environ + yaml.safe_load, which takes 0.38 s on the 3,046-line registry. While thread A is parsing, thread B sees the flag, gets {}, and line 92 stores `_REGISTRY_TIMEOUTS_CACHE = flat` ({'default': 600}) permanently. Reproduced: serial architect_general timeout = 600. With a concurrent first access, B got the fallback (-1), and afterwards _registry_timeout still returned -1 with diagnostics status 'fallback' for the process lifetime. 23 files outside src/config call _registry_timeout/_registry_runtime_value, many from threaded paths (tools/web/fetch.py, repl_environment, autopilot eval_tower).
proposal: Replace the boolean flags with one module-level threading.Lock taken around both loads (double-checked cache), never cache a result produced under a guard, and delete the 'recursive_guard' diagnostic branch. If you want, collapse to a functools.cache'd _load_registry(path) keyed by the resolved path (see A3-06).
CORRECTED: Treat this as low-priority cleanup, not a production bug. Replace the two boolean guards with one threading.Lock (double-checked) so tests that call reset_validation_caches() concurrently are sound, and delete the 'recursive_guard' diagnostic branch. Bundle it with A3-06/A3-07 rather than shipping it alone.
verify-note: The guard logic is as described, and the poisoning sequence is valid in isolation: A is in _load_registry_runtime_defaults; B enters _load_registry_timeouts, gets {} from the runtime guard, and caches {'default':600} at :92. In production it cannot happen. Both caches are filled during `import src.config`: the LLMSettings class body calls _registry_runtime_value (:101-105), and the TimeoutsSettings body calls TimeoutsConfig(), which calls _registry_timeout. Both run under the module import lock. I verified _REGISTRY_TIMEOUTS_CACHE holds 59 entries right after import. The timeouts cache is only cleared by reset_validation_caches(), whose sole caller is one test file. reset_config() clears only the runtime-defaults cache, and on that path the guard returns {} without caching, so the fallback is transient, not permanent. The '23 threaded callers' therefore always see a warm cache.
verification: New threaded test: reset_validation_caches(); start thread A on _registry_runtime_value, then call _registry_timeout immediately in the main thread; assert both get registry values. Run tests/unit/test_config_validation.py.

# A4 — orchestrator REPL/tools/session/trace/delegation + top-level modules  (findings kept: 26, refuted: 0, bloat_est: 3000)

The unit is about 69.7k LOC. Its biggest problems are not size hotspots but plumbing that is duplicated and has drifted apart. There are four parallel tool stacks: YAML orchestration/tools, src/builtin_tools.py, src/tools/*, and the REPL-mixin globals. One concrete result: the production `read_file` silently swaps the YAML allowlisted, size-capped handler for a bare `open(path)`, because register_handler defaults to update=True. Another: the REPL `web_search`/`web_fetch` globals skip the role permissions, the no_web task policy and the per-request telemetry chokepoint. The REPL's structured (ReAct) mode has two real defects: a regex FINAL() pre-check that fires on comments and strings and skips the tool calls before it, and a hand-maintained tool-name list that is missing 10 globals, so the chain policy and parallel dispatch never see them. The REPL security story is overstated. In production, run_python_code runs plain unsandboxed `python3`, so the AST denylist and path allowlists are guardrails, not a boundary. The fence docs also list residual gaps that the code and tests show are closed. features.py has 7 flags nobody reads, one flag read behind its back (mcp_server), dataclass defaults that contradict the registry for 5 flags, and a hand-coded validate() that misses a declared dependency. The TD-21 import race fix was applied to only 1 of 15 sys.modules shims. Several src modules are test-only or offline tooling: pipeline_monitor, model_grader, skill_hub_interop, tool_output_compressor_mcp, gradio_ui, and the duplicated SessionStore Protocol. Dead code and duplication plausibly removable is about 3,000 LOC: ~900 duplicate tool impls, ~630 proven-dead symbols including gradio_ui, ~400 SessionStore Protocol, ~250 features.py restatement, ~1,000 test-only modules subject to owner sign-off. Relocating pipeline_monitor/reviewer-lab code is neutral in LOC but clarifies the serving boundary. print() in library code is not an issue in this unit: all 206 prints are in CLI entry points (cli*.py, trace/cli.py, gradio_ui.py).

## A4-01 [sev 4] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=-80
**Production read_file/lint_python silently replaced by builtin versions with no path allowlist or size cap**
files: O:src/builtin_tools.py:277, O:src/builtin_tools.py:154, O:src/api/__init__.py:158, O:src/registry/tool_registry.py:314, O:orchestration/tools/system.py:19
evidence: Lifespan first loads orchestration/tool_registry.yaml (api/__init__.py:142-150). Its read_file -> orchestration.tools.system.read_file is guarded by `_is_path_allowed` (ALLOWED_PATHS=['/mnt/raid0/llm/','/tmp/']) and has max_bytes=1_000_000. Lifespan then calls register_builtin_tools (api/__init__.py:162). `_register_file_tools` (builtin_tools.py:277-315) re-registers read_file via `registry.register_handler(...)`, whose default is `update: bool = True` (tool_registry.py:314 -> register_tool(update=True) overwrites). The served handler is therefore `read_file(path, max_lines=None)`: a bare `open(path)`/`f.read()` with no allowlist and no cap. The comment at api/__init__.py:158 "These may overlap with YAML tools - duplicates are skipped" is false; only the 5 compat tools check `_tool_registered`. YAML's lint_python is overridden the same way. Builtin run_tests (154-190) runs `python -m pytest <path>` for any path. No global policy layer is ever installed (no caller of add_global_policy), and cascading_tool_policy is default_prod=True. So roles without tool_permissions in model_registry.yaml (coder_escalation, architect_*, ingest_long_context; only frontdoor and worker_general declare them) resolve to every tool. Failure: TOOL('read_file', path='/home/u/.ssh/id_rsa') returns the key. read_file on a 20 GB GGUF reads it all into memory. test_builtin_tools_registration.py has a path-refusal test for write_json but none for read_file.
proposal: In builtin_tools._register_code_tools/_register_file_tools, apply the existing `_tool_registered(registry, name)` guard per tool (as _register_compatibility_tools already does), or pass update=False and catch per tool. Preferably delete the builtin read_file/list_files/lint_python/run_tests and keep one implementation, the YAML one, with `_is_path_allowed` switched to realpath + _fence_rules.is_under (abspath allows symlink escape). Fix the misleading comment.
CORRECTED: The original proposal stands, with one addendum. If YAML's orchestration.tools.system.read_file becomes the single implementation, also change its oversize branch to a bounded read (`with open(p,'rb') as fh: fh.read(max_bytes)`), and replace abspath+startswith with realpath + a separator-aware is_under. Also repoint tests/test_tool_sentinels.py READ_FILE_ROOTS at the implementation that is actually served, or derive it from it.
verify-note: Checked first-hand. api/__init__.py:142-163 loads the YAML registry first and then calls register_builtin_tools. register_handler defaults to `update: bool = True` (tool_registry.py:314) and register_tool(update=True) overwrites silently. YAML read_file is orchestration.tools.system.read_file (tool_registry.yaml:651), guarded by _is_path_allowed with ALLOWED_PATHS ['/mnt/raid0/llm/','/tmp/']. builtin_tools.py:293-315 replaces it with a bare open(path)/f.read(). Only _register_compatibility_tools checks _tool_registered. The comment at api/__init__.py:158 ('duplicates are skipped') is false. add_global_policy has zero callers in all three repos. cascading_tool_policy is default_prod=True (features.py:117), and resolve_policy_chain with an empty chain returns all_tools, so a role without tool_permissions gets every tool. Only frontdoor (model_registry.yaml:2043) and worker_general (:2453) declare tool_permissions. The legacy path (test default) denies unknown roles instead, so tests run deny-by-default while prod runs allow-by-default. test_builtin_tools_registration.py has write_json_blocks_non_raid_path (323) but only success/not-found cases for read_file (367-397). Additional concrete consequence: tests/test_tool_sentinels.py:33-35 anchors the eval-secret isolation invariant to 'read_file's allowed roots (src/tools/file/read.py)'. That module is production-dead, and the served builtin read_file can read the default secret path /dev/shm/epyc_eval_secrets.json (eval_secret.py:41) when AUTOPILOT_TOOL_SENTINELS=1. One caveat on the evidence: the YAML handler is not memory-capped either. system.py:38 does `p.read_bytes()[:max_bytes]`, which reads the whole file before slicing.
verification: New test that replays lifespan registration: ToolRegistry(); load_from_yaml(registry,'orchestration/tool_registry.yaml'); register_builtin_tools(registry). Assert registry._tools['read_file'].handler.__module__ == 'orchestration.tools.system' and that the handler refuses '/etc/hostname'.
