## A4-02 [sev 3] [best_practice/bug] [effort M] [behav intended] [partially] loc_delta=-30
**REPL web_search()/web_fetch() bypass role permissions, the no_web task policy and per-request tool telemetry**
files: O:src/repl_environment/combined_ops.py:104, O:src/repl_environment/external_access.py:46, O:src/repl_environment/context.py:640, O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:192, O:src/tool_policy.py:63
evidence: The single tool chokepoint `_dispatch_tool` calls registry.invoke with `context=getattr(self,'tool_context',None)` (context.py:640-647). That context is how WS-3 `no_web` (NO_WEB_TASK_TYPES={'math','thinking'}, tool_policy.py:63) denies group:web. The REPL global `web_search()` (combined_ops.py:104-129; not gated by REPL_COMBINED_OPS) instead calls `self.tool_registry.invoke('web_search', self.role, caller_type='direct', query=...)` with no context= and outside `_invoke_tool`. On a math task CALL('web_search',..) is therefore denied but web_search(..) succeeds. The call is also missing from `_invoked_tools`, which environment.py:226-232 calls 'the ONLY source the API uses for per-request tool telemetry'. batch_web_search/search_and_verify (combined_ops.py:165,228,240) repeat the pattern. `web_fetch()` (external_access.py:46-70) does a raw `requests.get(url)` with no permission check at all. worker_general, the REPL default role ('Default to restricted role', environment.py:192), has `web_access: false` in model_registry.yaml (worker_general block @2395), yet web_fetch works for it. web_fetch has no loopback/SSRF guard (http://127.0.0.1:8000/... reaches the orchestrator API) and increments _exploration_calls without _state_lock.
proposal: Send every REPL web builtin through `self._invoke_tool(...)`: web_search -> registry 'web_search', web_fetch -> registry 'fetch_docs' or a newly registered 'web_fetch' tool. Permission, no_web context, eval fence, typed-args and telemetry then apply uniformly. Delete the bespoke try/except wrappers in combined_ops (the batch variants call _invoke_tool in a loop).
CORRECTED: Route web_search and batch_web_search/search_and_verify through self._invoke_tool. For web_fetch, register a real 'web_fetch' tool that moves the HTML-to-text logic into orchestration/tools/web.py next to http_get, add a loopback/link-local refusal, and have the REPL global call _invoke_tool('web_fetch', ...). Do not map it to 'fetch_docs', which the served registry does not contain. Pair this with the missed finding that 5 of 9 REPLEnvironment construction sites never pass tool_context, otherwise no_web still does not apply on those paths.
verify-note: Confirmed: combined_ops.py:114-120 calls tool_registry.invoke('web_search', self.role, caller_type='direct', ...) with no context= and outside _invoke_tool/_dispatch_tool (context.py:637-645 passes context=tool_context). So no_web (tool_registry.py:483-484) is not applied, and the call never reaches _invoked_tools. web_fetch (external_access.py:46-70) is a raw requests.get with no permission check, does an unlocked `self._exploration_calls += 1`, and has no loopback guard. Correction: the title and summary overstate the web_search part. registry.invoke still enforces role permissions (can_use_tool at tool_registry.py:526) and the eval fence (check_tool_call at :545). Only the task-level no_web context and the per-request telemetry are bypassed; web_fetch is the one that bypasses role permissions. The proposed mapping is also wrong: there is no 'fetch_docs' tool in the production registry. tool_registry.yaml has http_get; fetch_docs exists only in the production-dead src/tools/web register path. Severity is lowered because run_python_code (A4-05) already lets any role make arbitrary HTTP requests.
verification: Unit test: REPLEnvironment(context='', tool_registry=stub, role='worker_general', tool_context={'no_web': True}). web_search('x') must yield a permission error and append exactly one record to _invoked_tools. web_fetch must be denied for web_access=false. Re-run tests/unit/test_combined_ops.py, test_repl_tool_telemetry_isolation.py, test_invocation_log_request_scope.py.

## A4-03 [sev 3] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [partially] loc_delta=-10
**Structured (ReAct) mode treats FINAL("...") in comments/strings as completion and skips preceding tool calls**
files: O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:915, O:src/api/routes/chat_pipeline/stages.py:174, O:src/graph/answer_resolution.py:50, O:src/api/routes/chat_delegation.py:450
evidence: _execute_structured runs three regexes over the raw source BEFORE exec (environment.py:915-919) and returns is_final=True on a match. Reproduced with the identical patterns. `# do not FINAL("draft") yet\nr = peek(100)\nprint(r)` gives final_answer 'draft'. `print("call FINAL('x') when done")` gives 'x'. `x = run_shell("ls")\nFINAL("done")` returns 'done' without executing run_shell. The non-structured execute() path instead relies on FinalSignal raised by the real FINAL() during exec (environment.py:1531), so the two modes disagree. Structured mode is a production path: _execute_react builds REPLEnvironment(structured_mode=True) (stages.py:174-181), and react_mode is default_prod=True. FINAL extraction regexes exist 4 times with different semantics: answer_resolution.py:50 (numeric literals), stages.py:196 and chat_delegation.py:450 (`\S+?`), and environment.py (string literal only).
proposal: Drop the regex pre-check: exec the code and catch FinalSignal as execute() does. FINAL_VAR already raises through _final_var. If a no-exec fast path is wanted, detect a top-level FINAL Call node via ast (extract_tool_calls already parses the code). Keep a single text-level extractor (answer_resolution._FINAL_RE) for model prose.
CORRECTED: Exec first, and treat FINAL as complete only when FinalSignal is raised. The structured path already catches it at ~1188. Keep the regex extraction only as a fallback when exec raises SyntaxError, since no preceding tool call could have run in that case. This fixes the comment, string and skipped-tool cases and keeps the leniency for malformed FINAL literals. Add tests for all four cases in test_repl_environment.py with structured_mode=True.
verify-note: Defect reproduced with the exact three regexes from environment.py:915-919. A comment yields 'draft', a string literal yields 'x', and `x = run_shell("ls")\nFINAL("done")` returns 'done' without executing run_shell. The same function's next block says 'Count tool function calls using AST, so comments/strings do not trigger false positives', which contradicts the pre-check. Correction on the production path: the cited stages.py:174 _execute_react is reachable only via an explicit request.force_mode='react'. chat_routing.py:185-186 maps the router's 'react' to 'repl', and chat_pipeline/__init__.py:15 calls it '(legacy, unreachable)'. The real production structured path is delegated specialists: chat_delegation.py:340/372-381 builds REPLEnvironment(structured_mode=structured) with delegate_mode defaulting to 'react' (chat_delegation_decision.py:209,223,236). The proposal is also not strictly safe. The regex pre-check (present since a4643396) also finalizes code that would not compile, e.g. FINAL('''...''') whose body contains an unescaped delimiter. Dropping it outright turns those turns into SyntaxError retries.
verification: Add 3 structured_mode=True cases to tests/unit/test_repl_environment.py using the inputs above: expect is_final False for the comment and string cases, and run_shell invoked for the third. Run tests/unit/test_tool_chaining.py, test_chain_audit.py, test_credential_redaction.py.

## A4-04 [sev 3] [modularity/bug] [effort M] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=-40
**REPL tool inventory hand-maintained in 5 places; structured mode misses 10 globals (web_search, code_search, doc_search, combined ops)**
files: O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:942, O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:365, O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:387, O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:1069, O:src/repl_environment/parallel_dispatch.py:44
evidence: The REPL tools are listed separately in: `_build_globals` (387-520), the `tool_functions` literal in _execute_structured (942-987, 44 names), `_READ_ONLY_REPL_TOOLS` (365-371), `_CHAINABLE_REPL_TOOLS` and `_PARALLEL_MUTATION_REPL_TOOLS` (372-384). An AST diff shows these globals are missing from tool_functions: web_search, code_search, doc_search, batch_web_search, search_and_verify, peek_grep, batch_llm_query, workspace_scan, remember, STUCK. Effects: (a) code_search/doc_search are declared read-only but are never counted, so `a=code_search(..); b=doc_search(..)` never takes the parallel_read_only path. (b) `web_search('q'); run_shell('ls')` counts as one tool call, so the multi-tool chain-opt-in policy (error text at 1069) is skipped and both run. _execute_dependency_aware_chain receives the same stale set.
proposal: Add one declarative table in a new repl_environment/tool_table.py: `REPL_TOOLS = {name: ReplTool(attr='_peek', read_only=True, chainable=False, parallel_mutation=False), ...}`. _build_globals iterates it, tool_functions = REPL_TOOLS.keys(), and the three frozensets are derived. Registry-provided read-only/chainable tools still merge in as today.
verify-note: An AST diff of _build_globals (387-520) against the tool_functions literal in _execute_structured (942-987, 44 names) gives exactly these missing globals: web_search, code_search, doc_search, batch_web_search, search_and_verify, peek_grep, batch_llm_query, workspace_scan, remember, STUCK. FINAL and FINAL_VAR are handled earlier. code_search and doc_search are in _READ_ONLY_REPL_TOOLS (365-371) but are never extracted by extract_tool_calls, so they cannot trigger parallel_read_only. With `web_search('q'); run_shell('ls')`, tool_calls == ['run_shell'], so the len>1 chain gate (blocked-tools error at 1064-1072) is skipped. If web_search were counted, it would land in non_chainable and the turn would be blocked. All 5 inventories are confirmed hand-maintained.
verification: Test that set(REPL_TOOLS) equals the callable, non-module keys of REPLEnvironment('')._globals. Re-run tests/unit/test_tool_chaining.py, test_tool_dependencies.py, test_allowed_callers.py, test_repl_environment.py.

## A4-05 [sev 3] [documentation/doc] [effort S] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=10
**REPL security model overstated: run_python_code is an unsandboxed escape hatch in production; fence residual-gap docs are stale**
files: O:src/repl_environment/external_access.py:318, O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:131, O:src/repl_environment/security.py:68, O:src/repl_environment/knowledge_fence.py:71, O:src/repl_environment/_fence_audit_hook.py:37
evidence: REPLEnvironment is described as a 'Sandboxed Python REPL' (environment.py:131). It has an AST denylist (security.py FORBIDDEN_ATTRS/CALLS, 291 LOC), SAFE_IMPORT_MODULES, and `ALLOWED_FILE_PATHS ... (security)` (environment.py:150-151). But run_python_code is always in globals: it writes the model's code to a temp file and runs `subprocess.run(['python3', script_path], cwd=tmp_dir)` with the orchestrator uid and environment (external_access.py:318-326). The audit-hook/Landlock launch applies only when an eval-fence carrier exists ('production launches exactly as before'). Any role can therefore run run_python_code("print(open('/etc/...').read())"), so in production the in-process checks are accident guards, not isolation. Separately, knowledge_fence.py:71-74 says 'Residual gaps: The audit hook does not see ctypes/libc calls. It does not see shell variable indirection inside a sh -c string'. That is contradicted by _fence_audit_hook.py:37-40 and 329-337, which refuse ctypes.dlopen/dlsym and $/backtick sh -c under hook-only, and by tests/unit/test_ap54_eval_knowledge_fence.py:1055 test_hook_only_denies_ctypes_and_shell_indirection.
proposal: Add a THREAT MODEL section (repl_environment/README.md or the environment.py module docstring) stating what each layer guarantees. AST denylist = UX guardrail and prompt-injection speed bump. Path allowlists = accident prevention. Real boundary = OS user, plus fence_kernel when armed. Record an explicit decision to either run run_python_code under fence_kernel in production or accept the risk. Replace the stale residual-gap list with the accurate one already in _fence_audit_hook.py:42-45 (os.open(rel, dir_fd=) and raw ctypes function pointers, hook-only).
verify-note: external_access.py:318-326 runs `subprocess.run(['python3', script_path], ..., cwd=tmp_dir)` whenever python_fence_launch returns None. The only pre-checks are scope_refusal (task_root only) and check_python_source (a no-op unless the eval fence is armed). The model's code is a string argument, so the outer _validate_code AST denylist never inspects it. The class docstring says 'Sandboxed Python REPL' (environment.py:131), and ALLOWED_FILE_PATHS is labelled '(security)' (150-151). knowledge_fence.py:71-74 still lists 'does not see ctypes/libc calls' and 'shell variable indirection inside sh -c' as residual gaps. _fence_audit_hook.py:37-45 documents that both are refused under hook-only, and test_hook_only_denies_ctypes_and_shell_indirection exists at test_ap54_eval_knowledge_fence.py:1055. The accurate remaining residual is os.open(rel, dir_fd=) plus raw ctypes function pointers (hook docstring 42-45).
verification: Doc-only. Check that the residual-gap text in knowledge_fence.py matches _fence_audit_hook.py's docstring. tests/unit/test_ap54_eval_knowledge_fence.py stays green.

## A4-M1 [sev 3] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [verifier-added] loc_delta=10
**WS-3 no_web task policy is passed at only 4 of 9 REPLEnvironment construction sites; delegated specialists, architect, OpenAI-compat and streaming REPLs never get it**

A math/thinking task delegated to a specialist, or served via /v1/chat/completions, can therefore CALL('web_search', ...) even though WS-3 forbids group:web for those task types.
proposal: Add one helper, e.g. src/tool_policy.tool_context_for(task_ir) -> dict, and pass tool_context=tool_context_for(routing.task_ir) at every REPLEnvironment construction site. Better: add a factory build_request_repl(request, routing, state, **kw) in chat_pipeline that all 9 sites use, so tool_context, script_registry and the MemRL kwargs cannot drift per site.
verify-note: 
verification: Unit test: for each construction helper, build with task_type='math' and assert repl.tool_context == {'no_web': True}. A registry test with cascading_tool_policy=True asserts CALL('web_search') is denied from a delegated specialist REPL. `grep -n 'REPLEnvironment(' src/api` then shows every site passing tool_context.

## A4-M2 [sev 3] [documentation/doc] [effort S] [behav none] [verifier-added] loc_delta=-5

files: root:coordination/inference-batch/manifest.yaml:15, root:coordination/inference-batch/manifest.yaml:29, O:src/proactive_delegation/review_service.py:1617, O:scripts/review/review_replay_50.py:297, O:src/features.py:272

Its rationale (line 15) says the 1-of-6 propagation hazard 'silently corrupts TM-8 coverage if review_decision_shadow is not live on ALL workers'. But `grep -rn 'review_decision_shadow\|shadow_decide' src scripts` shows no code reading the flag. Its only mentions are docstrings (review_service.py:1634) and spec rows. shadow_decide's sole caller is the offline replay scripts/review/review_replay_50.py:297, which calls it unconditionally ('TM-3/8 always-on', line 248). A P0 campaign gate therefore depends on a flag with no runtime effect: a failed attestation can block the campaign for nothing, and a passing one proves nothing about TM-8 coverage.
proposal: Pick one in the owning handoff (bulk-inference-campaign.md): (a) wire the flag, gating the review_candidate -> shadow_decide call in the serving review path on features().review_decision_shadow and refusing replay when it is off; or (b) drop flags_required/--expect-feature from RCP-W1 and correct the rationale. Until then, do not delete the flag (see A4-06).

## A4-08 [sev 2] [modularity/bug] [effort M] [behav none] [partially] loc_delta=-84
**TD-21 shim import race fixed for 1 of 15 sys.modules-swapping compat shims**
files: O:src/__init__.py:47, O:src/tool_registry.py:1, O:src/model_server.py:1, O:src/registry_loader.py:1, O:src/escalation.py:1
evidence: src/__init__.py:24-82 and src/inference_lock.py:1-29 document that a shim body doing `sys.modules[__name__] = _real` races under concurrent first import: ~1% ImportError at 24-way, reproduced by tests/unit/test_inference_lock_concurrent_import.py. The fix is a MetaPathFinder hardcoded to the single name 'src.inference_lock'. The same racy body remains in 14 top-level shims: concurrency, delegation_cache, delegation_reports, dispatcher, escalation, executor, llm_cache, model_server, prefix_cache, registry_loader, script_registry, task_ir, tool_loader, tool_registry. Importer files across O/R/root: registry_loader 42, model_server 40, escalation 37, tool_registry 30. 46 of the src imports are function-level lazy imports that can run concurrently on request threads, e.g. api/services/memrl.py:217,223, proactive_delegation/delegator.py:62, formalizer.py:33, mcp_server.py:80.
proposal: Generalize _InferenceLockAliasFinder into a mapping finder, `_ALIASES = {'src.tool_registry': 'src.registry.tool_registry', ...}` with 15 entries, and reduce each shim file to a docstring-only fallback. Better, codemod imports to the canonical modules (sed over O, R and root, since the finder keeps module identity so monkeypatch targets stay valid) and delete the 14 shims.
CORRECTED: Generalize the alias finder to a 14-entry mapping as proposed, but first grep for code that reads the renamed __name__: runtime logging.getLogger(__name__) inside functions, and `__name__ == '__main__'` in src/registry/registry_loader.py:1041 and src/inference/model_server.py:919, which the pyproject `registry-loader = src.registry_loader:main` entry reaches through the alias. Then codemod importers to the canonical paths and delete the shims. Parametrize test_inference_lock_concurrent_import.py over all aliases.
verify-note: Confirmed that 14 top-level shims (concurrency ... tool_registry) are 6-line bodies doing `_real = import_module(...); _real.__name__ = __name__; sys.modules[__name__] = _real`. Only src.inference_lock goes through the MetaPathFinder in src/__init__.py:24-84. Corrections. (1) There is a 16th compat shim, src/inference_tap.py, that already mitigates the race with eager re-exports, so the count is '1 finder + 1 eager re-export + 14 unmitigated'. (2) Importer counts are overstated: my dotted-import grep over .py files in O/R/root gives registry_loader 37, model_server 38, escalation 26, tool_registry 29. The 46 function-level imports in src is correct. (3) The finding missed a side effect. Every one of the 14 shims sets `_real.__name__ = __name__`, so importing the alias renames the canonical module, e.g. src.registry.tool_registry.__name__ becomes 'src.tool_registry'. The proposed finder approach would stop doing that, a small behavior change to verify. The race needs concurrent FIRST import, and most aliases are already imported at module level during startup, so severity 2.
verification: Parametrize test_inference_lock_concurrent_import.py over every alias. Assert sys.modules[alias] is sys.modules[target]. After the codemod, `grep -rE 'src\.(tool_registry|model_server|registry_loader|escalation|...)\b'` returns 0 hits.

## A4-14 [sev 2] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [partially] loc_delta=-10
**Data race on ResearchContext ids under default-on parallel read-only dispatch; state_lock parameter never used**
files: O:src/research_context.py:93, O:src/research_context.py:242, O:src/repl_environment/file_exploration.py:74, O:src/repl_environment/parallel_dispatch.py:238, O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:254
evidence: parallel_tools is True in both test and prod. execute_parallel_calls runs peek/grep on up to 4 threads. Each call does `_increment_exploration` under _state_lock, but `_exploration_log.add_event` and `_track_research` -> ResearchContext.add run unlocked. ResearchContext._get_next_id (93-98) is an unlocked read-modify-write of self._counters, so two concurrent peeks can both get 'P1', and `self.nodes[node_id] = node` (242) overwrites one. _find_string_refs iterating self.nodes while another thread inserts can raise 'dict changed size'; file_exploration.py:91 swallows it with `except Exception: pass`. execute_parallel_calls accepts `state_lock` but never uses it (parallel_dispatch.py:238-243, vulture 100%), so environment.py:254's comment that the lock 'protects state mutations in _exploration_calls, _exploration_log, _grep_hits_buffer, artifacts' is only partly true. The `lock = getattr(self,'_state_lock',None); if lock: with lock: ... else: ...` block is copy-pasted 4 times (routing.py:91, code_search.py:251,393, file_exploration.py:67).
proposal: Wrap _track_research and add_event in self._state_lock (or give ResearchContext its own lock). Remove the dead parameter. Replace the 4 copies with one `_locked()` context-manager helper on the mixin base.
CORRECTED: Give ResearchContext its own threading.Lock held only around id allocation plus the nodes insert, and around snapshotting nodes before ref scans. Do not hold the REPL-wide _state_lock across embedding work. Remove the unused state_lock parameter, add a `_locked()` helper for the 4 copies, and fix the environment.py:254 comment.
verify-note: The real parts check out. parallel_tools is True in test and prod (features.py:111). execute_parallel_calls never uses state_lock (parallel_dispatch.py:238-283). _track_research to ResearchContext.add runs unlocked, and _get_next_id (research_context.py:93-98) is an unlocked read-modify-write. The environment.py:254-255 comment overclaims. The lock boilerplate is copy-pasted (file_exploration.py:67, routing.py:91, code_search.py:251/393). Corrections. The 'dict changed size' path is not _find_string_refs: it snapshots with list(self.nodes.keys()) (line 129), which is atomic under the GIL. The vulnerable iteration is _find_semantic_refs at research_context.py:181, which runs only when the embedder is available. The id-collision window is a few bytecodes wide, so this is real but rare. On the proposal: wrapping _track_research in self._state_lock would hold the shared lock across embedding computation and serialize the parallel peeks the feature exists to overlap.
verification: Stress test: 200 iterations of parallel `a=peek(10,file_path=f1); b=peek(10,file_path=f2)` in structured mode, asserting unique research node ids and node count == 2*iterations. Run the existing parallel dispatch tests.

## A4-15 [sev 2] [modularity/refactor] [effort M] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=-60
**execute() and _execute_structured() duplicate the exec pipeline and apply the context-bundle cap in a different order**
files: O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:1106, O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:1444, O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:723
evidence: Both methods carry their own SIGALRM install/restore, redirect_stdout/stderr + exec (1160 and 1502), redact_if_enabled, FinalSignal/SyntaxError/REPLTimeout mapping and _tool_hint_if_relevant, about 75 lines each. They have already diverged. The non-structured path caps bundle output BEFORE _spill_output, as `_bundle_cap_output` documents ('Runs BEFORE _spill_output, so a capped turn never spills or asks the worker for a summary', 723-726). The structured path spills first, sending uncapped output to the worker summarizer and writing it to the spill file, and execute() applies the cap afterwards (1452-1458). The structured path also skips _record_code_log. This is latent today only because bundles are attached in repl_executor.py:441, which builds a non-structured REPL, while structured REPLs come only from stages.py:179. Nothing enforces that separation.
proposal: Extract `_exec_captured(code, start_time) -> (output, exc)` (alarm, redirect, exec, redact) and one post-processing pipeline (cap -> spill -> suggestions) shared by both modes. Make attach_context_bundle refuse structured mode explicitly, or apply the cap before spill there.
verify-note: Verified. The structured path spills first (environment.py:1178, and 1113 for parallel), and execute() caps afterwards (1453-1458). The non-structured path caps at 1514 before spilling at 1517, as _bundle_cap_output's docstring (730-733) requires. The structured path also returns before _record_code_log (1468/1478). There is a further divergence the finding missed: the structured exception branch already calls _bundle_cap_output on stdout (1213), and execute() then caps result.output again (1455). context_bundle.record_printed (context_bundle.py:687-697) accumulates printed_bytes/shown_bytes/_turns_capped, so with a bundle attached one error turn is double-counted. This is latent because attach_context_bundle has a single caller (repl_executor.py:442) and structured REPLs are built at stages.py:174 and chat_delegation.py:372 without bundles.
verification: tests/unit/test_repl_environment.py, test_repl_spill_output.py, test_oab7_context_bundle.py, test_credential_redaction.py. Add a structured-mode + bundle test asserting the spill file holds only capped bytes.

In production, batch_web_search/search_and_verify/peek_grep/batch_llm_query/workspace_scan are always exported as REPL globals (environment.py:480-486) yet always return '[ERROR: Combined ops disabled. Set REPL_COMBINED_OPS=1 to enable.]'. The flag is invisible to /config, runtime_flags.json, runtime_flags.spec.yaml and the drift checker. REPL_FRECENCY is parsed 3 times with ('1','true','on'), without 'yes' (code_search.py:298,511, file_exploration.py:386). ORCHESTRATOR_TOOL_CHAIN_MODE and _PARALLEL_MUTATIONS are read ad hoc per REPL construction (environment.py:211-218). ORCHESTRATOR_PER_REGION_LOCKS is parsed in 5 places with .strip() but no .lower() (contention_gate.py:166,184, llm_primitives/inference.py:288, backends/concurrency_aware.py:1145, api/routes/dashboard.py:2242), so 'True' is off there while env_parsing.env_bool would read it as on.
proposal: Register REPL_COMBINED_OPS and REPL_FRECENCY as FeatureSpecs (or keep the env names as aliases), or at least parse them with env_bool. Export the combined-op globals only when enabled. Add one per_region_locks_enabled() helper built on env_bool.
verify-note: REPL_COMBINED_OPS appears only in combined_ops.py and tests/unit/test_combined_ops.py across O/R/root, including scripts/, orchestration/ and root scripts. The 5 globals are always exported (environment.py:480-486) and return the 'Combined ops disabled' error in prod. Real-world impact is limited because no prompt in orchestration/ or src/prompt_builders advertises them. REPL_FRECENCY is parsed 3 times with ('1','true','on') (code_search.py:298,511; file_exploration.py:386). ORCHESTRATOR_PER_REGION_LOCKS is parsed at 5 sites with .strip() and no .lower() (contention_gate.py:166,184; inference.py:288; concurrency_aware.py:1145; dashboard.py:2242). The 5 sites agree with each other; the inconsistency is only against env_bool.
verification: tests/unit/test_combined_ops.py, test_features.py, test_runtime_flag_spec.py (spec coverage will require the new rows).

## A4-18 [sev 2] [modularity/refactor] [effort M] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=-20
**kv_pool_admission.acquire (220 lines, cc 68, 13 params) on the per-inference path spreads ticket state across 8 dicts**
files: O:src/scheduling/kv_pool_admission.py:654, O:src/scheduling/kv_pool_admission.py:762, O:src/scheduling/kv_pool_admission.py:830, O:src/llm_primitives/inference.py:1244
evidence: acquire() runs on every inference call (inference.py:1244, pool_admission.acquire inside _call_caching_backend). After `admitted = True` (762-817), a ~55-line block mixes stats counters, fork and busy-fork attribution and admission_info under the condition lock. Per-ticket state lives in 8 dicts (_waiting, _lpm_score, _lpm_skips, _trunk_held, _trunk_since, _fork_source, _busy_fork, _admission_info). Cleanup is split between acquire's finally (5 of them) and release, which makes leaks hard to reason about. cancel_check() exceptions are swallowed with a bare `pass` on every poll (830-831), so a broken cancel callback silently disables cancellation for the whole wait.
proposal: Extract `_record_admission(url, ticket, verdict, credit, waited, ...)` and `_leave_queue(url, ticket, queue, admitted, pidx)`. Introduce `@dataclass _TicketState` stored in one `self._tickets[ticket]`, so one pop releases everything. Log the first cancel_check failure at WARNING.

One small correction: acquire runs per inference call only for backends whose context limit reports shared_pool (inference.py:1231-1244, `if pool_limit is not None and pool_limit.shared_pool`), not on every call.
verification: Run the existing kv-pool admission unit tests (tests/unit/test_kv_pool_admission*.py) unchanged. Add a test that every per-ticket structure is empty after acquire+release and after an abandoned acquire.

## A4-19 [sev 2] [modularity/refactor] [effort M] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=-40
**_fence_install (cc 157, 307 lines) inlines data tables and a 3rd argv parser that already diverges from run_shell's**
files: O:src/repl_environment/_fence_audit_hook.py:55, O:src/repl_environment/_fence_audit_hook.py:229, O:src/repl_environment/knowledge_fence.py:556, O:src/repl_environment/task_root.py:285, O:src/repl_environment/_fence_rules.py:1
evidence: The closure design is deliberate (tamper resistance) and must stay. Its cc-157 bulk, though, is ~60 lines of constant tables (wrappers, shells, ctypes_symbols, events, interpreters at 95-143), a 13-way if/elif event dispatch in hook() (287-359, cc 62), and check_command (229-285, cc 50). check_command re-implements argv analysis (command words, --flag=value, `ls -R` detection, path-like positionals) that also exists in knowledge_fence._check_shell (556-600) and task_root.check_shell_scope (285, cc 73; it imports knowledge_fence's private _AWK_IO_RE/_script_program/_sed_does_io). The three already differ: the hook treats every `grep` as recursive (recursive_commands | {'grep',...} at 80-81), while _check_shell requires -r. Only 24 subprocess-spawning tests in the 1,260-line test_ap54_eval_knowledge_fence.py exercise this code.
proposal: Move the constant tables and a pure `parse_argv(argv, cwd) -> (commands, path_words, recursive)` into _fence_rules.py. That module is already stdlib-only and embedded verbatim into the child, so the hook captures them at install time from `ns`. Build hook dispatch as a dict of inner handlers captured in the closure (wrapped in MappingProxyType). knowledge_fence._check_shell and task_root.check_shell_scope reuse parse_argv. Add fast pure unit tests for parse_argv.
CORRECTED: Extract a pure parse_argv into _fence_rules.py (already embedded into the child via `ns`). Keep the per-layer recursion policy explicit: the hook may stay stricter, documented as such in one place. Do not let a shared parser silently loosen the hook.
verify-note: Metrics confirmed: _fence_install 307 lines, cc 157; hook cc 62; check_command cc 50. The hook adds 'grep' (and rg, ag, ack, ...) to recursive_commands unconditionally (_fence_audit_hook.py:81-82). knowledge_fence._check_shell treats grep as recursive only with a recursive flag (via _is_recursive_grep_flag). task_root imports knowledge_fence privates. Caveat: the hook's stricter grep rule may be deliberate defense-in-depth, so unification must share argv parsing, not policy.
verification: tests/unit/test_ap54_eval_knowledge_fence.py (64 tests incl. hook-only/kernel), tests/unit/test_oab1_task_scope.py, plus new table-driven parse_argv tests. Document the intended grep-recursion rule in one place.

## A4-20 [sev 2] [debuggability/refactor] [effort S] [behav possible] [confirmed] loc_delta=-110
**Seven copies of project-root/allowlist helpers silently fall back to cwd and freeze at import; sibling-prefix match**
files: O:src/repl_environment/external_access.py:19, O:src/repl_environment/file_mutation.py:14, O:src/repl_environment/procedure_tools.py:12, O:src/tools/web/fetch.py:35, O:src/tools/code/lint.py:47, O:src/tools/code/run_tests.py:46, O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:103, O:src/canvas_export.py:30
evidence: _get_project_root is defined 7 times (the 4 repl/tools files above plus tools/code/lint.py:28, run_tests.py:27, pipeline_monitor/claude_debugger.py:48). The llm_root+/tmp allowlist builder exists 3 times (environment.py:103, tools/file/read.py:22, list.py:21) and _get_canvas_dir twice (canvas_export.py:30, tools/canvas_tools/__init__.py:23). Each wraps get_config() in `except Exception: pass` and falls back to os.getcwd() or a hardcoded /mnt/raid0/llm without logging. Several are evaluated at import into constants: REPLEnvironment.ALLOWED_FILE_PATHS class attribute (environment.py:151), lint.py:47, run_tests.py:46, read.py:41, list.py:40. Under fact (a) (get_config raises off-host), allowlists silently become cwd and env changes after import never apply. lint/run_tests use `ALLOWED_PATHS=[project_root]` with no trailing separator plus str.startswith, so '/mnt/raid0/llm/epyc-orchestrator-old/x' passes. proposal: Add one cached module, src/config/path_fallbacks.py, with project_root(), file_allowlist() and canvas_dir(). Each logs its fallback once at WARNING. Validate with is_under(realpath(p), root). Replace the copies, and turn import-time constants into calls.
verify-note: I found 6 _get_project_root definitions: external_access.py:20, file_mutation.py:14, procedure_tools.py:12, tools/web/fetch.py:35, tools/code/lint.py:28, run_tests.py:27, plus claude_debugger.py:48. Other copies: _get_allowed_paths at tools/file/read.py:22 and list.py:21, _get_allowed_file_paths at environment.py:103, and _get_canvas_dir twice. Each swallows get_config failures silently. lint.py:46-48 builds ALLOWED_PATHS=[_get_project_root()] at import with no trailing separator and uses prefix matching, so the sibling-prefix bypass is real but production-dead (A4-09). One variance: environment.py's fallback reads ORCHESTRATOR_PATHS_LLM_ROOT rather than cwd, so the copies do not even share a fallback.
verification: Unit test with get_config patched to raise: expect a single warning and the fallback value. Test that a sibling-prefix path is rejected. Run tests/unit/test_repl_file_mutation.py, test_repl_environment.py, test_canvas_export.py.

## A4-21 [sev 2] [best_practice/refactor] [effort S] [behav none] [partially] loc_delta=0
**Import-time config reads in unit modules extend fact (a): importing src.vision raises off-host**
files: O:src/vision/config.py:19, O:src/repl_environment/environment.py:151, O:src/gate_runner.py:37, O:src/mcp_server.py:40, O:src/tools/file/read.py:41
evidence: vision/config.py:19-20 calls `get_config().vision` and `get_config().timeouts` at module import and derives ~30 module constants from them. Per known fact (a), VisionConfig's default factory calls kernel_paths.backend_dir(), which raises without /mnt/raid0/llm/kernels/production. So importing any src.vision module (vision/__init__, search, batch, analyzers/*, api/routes/vision.py:12) fails off-host. api/routes/__init__.py:48-53 swallows that as 'Vision router unavailable', which hides the cause. Other import-time side effects in the unit: REPLEnvironment.ALLOWED_FILE_PATHS class attribute (environment.py:151), the ALLOWED_PATHS constants in tools/file/*.py and tools/code/*.py, gate_runner's exec_module of a scripts file (37-43), and mcp_server's sys.path.insert (40), which duplicates the PYTHONPATH already set in .mcp.json.
proposal: Turn vision/config into lazy accessors: a module-level __getattr__ that reads a functools.cache'd get_config(), so `from src.vision.config import VISION_CACHE_DIR` keeps working. Make ALLOWED_FILE_PATHS a cached classmethod or property. Remove mcp_server's sys.path.insert. Coordinate with the separate hermetic-conftest fix.
CORRECTED: Make vision/config lazy (module __getattr__ over a cached accessor), and make ALLOWED_FILE_PATHS a cached accessor, as part of the hermetic-conftest work. Keep mcp_server's sys.path.insert, or replace the documented launch with `python -m src.mcp_server` and update the docstring first. Also note gate_runner's five module-level _registry_timeout(...) calls (gate_runner.py:52-58) as another import-time config read.
verify-note: Confirmed: vision/config.py:19-20 calls get_config().vision and .timeouts at import. VisionConfig and PathsConfig call kernel_paths.backend_dir (config/models.py:963,1230), which raises KernelPathError off-host. This largely restates known fact (a) for vision. Two sub-claims are wrong. (1) api/routes/__init__.py:48-55 catches only ImportError. KernelPathError subclasses RuntimeError (kernel_paths.py:64), so the failure propagates instead of being swallowed as 'Vision router unavailable'; the cause is not hidden. (2) mcp_server's sys.path.insert (line 40) is not redundant. The module docstring documents `python src/mcp_server.py` launches with no PYTHONPATH, so removing it breaks that launch mode. Both .mcp.json files happen to set PYTHONPATH.
verification: In a clean checkout without /mnt/raid0: `python -c 'import src.vision, src.repl_environment.environment'` succeeds. Then run tests/unit/test_vision*.py and test_repl_environment.py.

## A4-M3 [sev 2] [performance/bug] [effort S] [behav none] [verifier-added] loc_delta=2
**YAML read_file's max_bytes 'cap' reads the entire file into memory before truncating**
files: O:orchestration/tools/system.py:38, O:orchestration/tools/system.py:16
evidence: system.py:36-40 reads `if stat.st_size > max_bytes: content = p.read_bytes()[:max_bytes].decode(...)`. read_bytes() loads the whole file and the slice happens afterwards. ALLOWED_PATHS includes '/mnt/raid0/llm/' (line 16), the model-weights tree, so TOOL('read_file', path=<any .gguf>) pulls tens to hundreds of GB into the API process before discarding all but 1 MB. A4-01 proposes making this handler the single read_file implementation, so this must be fixed in the same change.
proposal: Replace with `with open(p, 'rb') as fh: raw = fh.read(max_bytes)` and decode with errors='replace'. Switch _is_path_allowed to realpath + separator-aware containment (_fence_rules.is_under), since abspath lets a symlink inside /tmp/ escape.
verify-note: 
verification: Unit test: create a sparse 2 GB file in tmp_path with ALLOWED_PATHS monkeypatched, call read_file with max_bytes=1000, and assert tracemalloc peak < 10 MB and len(content) is about 1000 plus the marker. Add a symlink-escape refusal test.

# A5 — orchestrator scripts/ (excluding autopilot)  (findings kept: 28, refuted: 0, bloat_est: 45000)

O:scripts outside autopilot is about 170k LOC. Its main problem is size and layout, not the logic itself. The biggest bloat is completed or retired work that was never removed. The E8 quality-baseline chain is 19 scripts (20,966 LOC) plus 15 test files (11,565 LOC). An operator ruling retired it on 2026-08-27, and it can no longer run because it pins the v8 llama tree. Smaller items in the same class: scripts/lib (3,974 LOC) is a forked copy of R:scripts/lib that only tests and a 100%-coverage Makefile gate use; seed_specialist_routing_v2.py (1,458) is an older copy of the main seeding runner; and deprecated/ (3,550) keeps an orchestrator copy of question_pool.py alive. The second problem is structure. src/ has 71 imports from scripts.* (stack_manifest, stack_numa, stack_paths and others), and the src.config <-> scripts.server import cycle has already forced duplicated code: a stdlib-only runtime-facts reader in src/config/models.py and a copy of the PathsConfig defaults in stack_paths. There are also 196 sys.path mutations, plus bare-name imports that load the same file under two module names (the scorer-race and question_pool shadowing incidents came from this). The launcher has a two-way cycle between orchestrator_stack and stack_commands, held together by 17 (*a, **kw) proxy functions. I also found concrete defects. The canonical seeding runner drops tool_chains from its retry diagnostics, a direct result of keeping three copies of the runner. reembed_episodic_store still launches the v9 in-tree binary on the start --repair-embeddings path. The GPU shadow-lane preflight requires v8 version strings, so after the v10 promotion it can never report a match. The launch sidecar writer swallows errors, so serving_calls can attribute requests to an old launch. Bloat estimate: about 45k LOC removable or consolidated (about 31k in scripts/, about 14k in tests used only by that dead code). Most of it is mechanical deletion backed by a ruling or grep evidence.

## A5-01 [sev 4] [bloat/deletion] [effort M] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=-32600
**Retire the E8 quality-baseline script chain (21k script LOC + 11.6k test LOC) that an operator ruling already retired and that cannot run on the current kernel**

ROOT ruling_op19_e8_chain_20260827.json says to 'retire the E8 chain'. It also records that the source evidence was destroyed and 'the instrument breaks on the current tree'. Coordinate with the ROOT unit to retire its host-only E8 tests in the same change.

Removing 'run_e8_quality_baseline_reseed.py' from bench_core_claim._BENCH_PROCESS_MARKERS therefore needs to cite that clause: the interlock becomes vacuous once the script is gone. ## A5-03 [sev 3] [modularity/refactor] [effort L] [behav none] [partially] loc_delta=-150
**src imports scripts.server.* 71 times, and the src.config <-> scripts.server cycle has already produced duplicated readers; move the stack library into a src package**
files: O:src/cli_orch.py:26, O:src/api/routes/dashboard_topology.py:19, O:src/config/models.py:529, O:src/config/models.py:538, O:scripts/server/stack_paths.py:17, O:scripts/server/stack_paths.py:98, O:scripts/server/stack_manifest.py:1742, O:scripts/server/runtime_facts_manifest.py:18
evidence: `grep -rnE '^\s*(from|import) scripts(\.|\s)' src` finds 71 import statements against 18 scripts modules: stack_manifest 21, stack_numa 19, stack_numa_mode 5, bench_core_claim 4, plus runtime_facts_manifest, realized_fleet, fleet_markers, stack_paths, stack_processes, gpu_shadow_lane, orchestrator_stack, and scripts.autopilot.*. src/cli_orch.py:26 and dashboard_topology.py:19-25 import at module level. The cycle is src.config -> runtime_facts_manifest -> stack_manifest -> stack_paths -> src.config (stack_paths.py:98 `from src.config import _registry_timeout  # noqa: E402`, placed mid-module to dodge it). Working around the cycle has already created duplicates. src/config/models.py:529-619 adds `_bootstrap_port_listening` and `_bootstrap_runtime_selected_servers`, a stdlib-only second reader of orchestrator_runtime_facts.json ('Read fresh realized facts without importing scripts.server'). stack_paths._get_paths (:17-66) restates PathsConfig defaults that must be kept 'byte-for-byte aligned with src.config.models.PathsConfig'. pyproject.toml:203 packages only ['src','orchestration'], so a built wheel of src would fail on `import scripts`; it only works because the editable .pth puts the repo root on sys.path. The import-time validators at stack_manifest.py:1742-1744 are fact (a): they make every src importer depend on the host.
proposal: Create src/stack/ (or src/launch/) and git-mv the pure-data/library modules into it: stack_manifest, stack_numa, stack_numa_mode, stack_paths, realized_fleet, runtime_facts_manifest, fleet_markers, stack_processes, stack_state, bench_core_claim. Split their import-time validation into an explicit `validate()` that orchestrator_stack.main() and the stack-change guard call, and make module globals lazy (functools.cache accessors). Leave one-line re-export shims at the old scripts/server/*.py paths for one release so R's string-embedded imports keep working. Then delete the bootstrap duplicates in src/config/models.py:529-619 and make stack_paths use PathsConfig directly, since the cycle is gone. After that, scripts/ depends on src/ and never the reverse; enforce it with an import-linter contract (`src` must not import `scripts`).
CORRECTED: First break the edge, not the directory. (1) stack_paths must stop importing src.config: pass _registry_timeout lazily through a function, or move it into a leaf module. (2) Have src.config read runtime facts through a leaf, dependency-free reader module that both src.config and runtime_facts_manifest import. That deletes the models.py:529-619 duplicate. (3) Then optionally relocate the library modules to src/stack/. Shims must alias the module object (`sys.modules[__name__] = importlib.import_module('src.stack.x')`) so monkeypatching and the bare-name R snippets keep binding the same object. (4) Add an import-linter contract that src does not import scripts. Verify with `python -X importtime -c 'import src.config'` showing no scripts.*, plus the existing test files once fact (a) is fixed.
verify-note: The facts check out. I count 71 `from/import scripts.` statements in src against 18 modules: stack_manifest 21, stack_numa 19, stack_numa_mode 5, bench_core_claim 4, and so on. stack_paths.py:98 has the mid-module `from src.config import _registry_timeout  # noqa: E402`. models.py:529-619 holds the stdlib-only `_bootstrap_runtime_selected_servers` duplicate reader. stack_paths._get_paths' docstring requires byte-for-byte alignment with PathsConfig. pyproject [tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] packages=['src','orchestration']. The editable .pth contains the repo root. The proposal's central claim is wrong, though. git-mv'ing the modules into src/stack/ does NOT remove the cycle. The cycle is an edge set: src.config's ServerURLsConfig lazily imports runtime_facts_manifest/stack_manifest (models.py:663, :689, :726, :795), which reach stack_paths, which imports src.config. Relocating the files keeps every edge, so 'delete the bootstrap duplicates since the cycle is gone' does not follow. The shim plan has a second problem. One-line `from new import *` shims would create second module objects and break the ~150 monkeypatch.setattr sites across 20 test files, plus R's string-embedded `sys.path.insert(0,'scripts/server'); import orchestrator_stack; from stack_numa import _numa_prefix` (R:prb_t4_tale_gpu.py:65-68). Finally, `orchestrator_stack.py --validate-only` is really `start --validate-only`, and it only validates a stack template.
verification: `grep -rnE '(from|import) scripts\.' src | wc -l` reaches 0. `python -X importtime -c 'import src.config'` shows no scripts.* modules. After fact (a): `pytest tests/unit/test_stack_manifest_imports.py tests/unit/test_build_server_command_helpers.py tests/unit/test_runtime_facts_manifest*.py -p no:cacheprovider` passes. `orchestrator_stack.py --validate-only` gives the same output before and after.

## A5-06 [sev 3] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=5
**Bug: reembed_episodic_store launches the v9 in-tree llama-server binary on the live `start --repair-embeddings` path**
files: O:scripts/graph_router/reembed_episodic_store.py:44, O:scripts/graph_router/reembed_episodic_store.py:167, O:scripts/maintenance/repair_episodic_embeddings.py:85, O:scripts/server/stack_commands.py:1593, O:src/registry/kernel_paths.py:91
evidence: reembed_episodic_store.py:44 has `LLAMA_SERVER = Path("/mnt/raid0/llm/llama.cpp/build/bin/llama-server")`. Per CLAUDE.md, v10 serves from kernels/production/{cpu,gpu}, while the frozen tree's build/ still holds the v9 binaries, so 'anything that hardcodes llama.cpp/build/bin is reading a kernel that serves nothing'. start_embedding_server (:167-186) launches it with no LD_LIBRARY_PATH (the three-ggml-generations hazard) and with stdout/stderr sent to DEVNULL, so a wrong-ggml or startup failure leaves no trace. The path is live: stack_commands.py:1593 `--repair-embeddings` -> repair_episodic_embeddings.py:85/392 subprocesses this script. The kernel-freeze runbook's offender list (ROOT docs/reference/kernel-freeze-runbook.md:357) does not mention this file. It only takes effect when no stack embedder is healthy on the probed port (probe_existing_server reuses running ones).
proposal: Replace the literal with `src.registry.kernel_paths.server_binary('cpu')`, set env['LD_LIBRARY_PATH'] from `backend_ld_library_path('cpu')` composed with ambient via stack_env.compose_ld_library_path, and log stderr to <log_dir>/reembed_<port>.log instead of DEVNULL.
verify-note: reembed_episodic_store.py:44 has the literal `/mnt/raid0/llm/llama.cpp/build/bin/llama-server`. It is the only such literal left in O scripts outside lib/autopilot (grep 'build(-hip)?/bin/llama|llama.cpp/build'). start_embedding_server (:168-190) launches it with stdout/stderr DEVNULL and no LD_LIBRARY_PATH. It is reached from stack_commands.py:1593 --repair-embeddings, through repair_episodic_embeddings.py:85/:376-389 REEMBED_SCRIPT. Ports already serving are reused (probe_existing_server, :317), so the stale binary launches only for unserved ports, as the finder said. kernel_paths exposes server_binary (:123) and backend_ld_library_path (:91), and stack_env.compose_ld_library_path exists (:170). The ROOT runbook offender list (:356-362) omits this file.
verification: Unit test: monkeypatch subprocess.Popen to capture argv/env and assert argv[0] == str(kernel_paths.server_binary('cpu')) and that env LD_LIBRARY_PATH starts with the backend dir (works off-host with ORCHESTRATOR_PATHS_KERNEL_ROOT pointed at a tmp store). `git grep -n 'llama.cpp/build/bin' scripts/graph_router` returns nothing.

## A5-07 [sev 3] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=-5
**Bug: the GPU shadow-lane preflight and stage0 pin binary version 10107/67a433bf4 (v8), so the v10 store binary always fails the version check**
files: O:scripts/server/gpu_shadow_lane.py:56, O:scripts/server/gpu_shadow_lane_preflight.py:360, O:scripts/server/gpu_shadow_lane_stage0.py:333, O:orchestration/gpu_shadow_lane_tenancy.yaml:54
evidence: gpu_shadow_lane.py:54 resolves the binary through the store (`LANE_BINARY_DIR = backend_dir("gpu")`, now v10 = binary 10303 / ffc1bac82 per CLAUDE.md), but :56-57 still pin `LANE_BINARY_VERSION = "10107"` and `LANE_BINARY_COMMIT = "67a433bf4"`. Those are the v8 values (same as FROZEN_V8_LLAMA_VERSION/HEAD in run_e8_quality_baseline_reseed.py:80-83). gpu_shadow_lane_preflight.py:360-365 appends the blocker 'HIP binary version mismatch' whenever either string is missing from `llama-server --version`, and stage0.py:333-334 requires tenancy.slot.binary_version/commit to equal the same constants, which gpu_shadow_lane_tenancy.yaml:54-55 duplicates. So the lane cannot pass preflight on v9 or v10. Tests (test_gpu_shadow_lane_preflight.py:85) feed the constant back to itself and stay green. The runbook fixed the path literals at these sites but not the version pins, because the expected identity is restated in two files instead of read from the store.
proposal: Derive the expected identity from the kernel store, e.g. a `kernel_paths.describe()['gpu']` field or the store's README/manifest that verify_kernel_store.sh already checks. Remove LANE_BINARY_VERSION/COMMIT and the yaml duplicate, or have the yaml say `version: from_store`. If a pin is wanted on purpose, add it to the kernel-promotion checklist and to stack_change_guard's hardcoded-surface rules.
CORRECTED: Same fix, with a scoped verification grep. `git grep '10107\|67a433bf' scripts/server orchestration` would fail on legitimate history: orchestration/instrument_eras.yaml:148-153 (the E-era record) and stack_numa.py:26/:523 (measurement provenance prose). Restrict the guard to scripts/server/gpu_shadow_lane*.py and orchestration/gpu_shadow_lane_*.yaml.
verify-note: gpu_shadow_lane.py:54 resolves through backend_dir('gpu'), but :56-57 pin '10107'/'67a433bf4'. preflight.py:360-365 blocks on a mismatch. stage0.py:333-334 checks tenancy.slot equality. tenancy yaml :54-56 even says `freeze: production-consolidated-v8`. The test feeds the constants back in (test_gpu_shadow_lane_preflight.py:84). gpu_shadow_lane_preflight.py:15 and np_ceiling.yaml:13 restate the v8 identity too.
verification: Unit test: build a fake store whose `--version` output says 10303 (ffc1bac82) and assert check_lane_preflight reports no version blocker. Add a test asserting that `git grep -n '10107\|67a433bf' scripts/server orchestration` is empty.

## A5-08 [sev 3] [best_practice/refactor] [effort L] [behav none] [partially] loc_delta=-200
**196 sys.path mutations and bare-name imports load the same file under two module names; this is already the root cause of two incidents**
files: O:scripts/benchmark/seeding_scoring.py:34, O:scripts/benchmark/dataset_adapter_modules/general.py:18, O:scripts/benchmark/seed_specialist_routing.py:62, O:scripts/benchmark/analyze_skill_transfer.py:29, O:scripts/server/contention_matrix.py:58, O:pyproject.toml:203
evidence: `grep -rnE 'sys\.path\.(insert|append)' scripts | grep -v scripts/autopilot` gives 196 lines. About 133 insert the repo root (PROJECT_ROOT 58, REPO_ROOT 19, _REPO 9, ...). That is redundant under the project venv: .venv/.../_editable_impl_epyc_orchestrator.pth already contains /workspace/repos/epyc-orchestrator. About 45 insert scripts/, scripts/benchmark, scripts/server or scripts/autopilot so that bare imports like `from seeding_types import` work. The same module then exists under two names: seeding_types is imported bare 18 times and as scripts.benchmark.seeding_types 4 times (analyze_skill_transfer.py:29, skill_transfer_regression.py:27, plan_offline_reward_pairwise_holdout_expansion.py:45, test_inf78...:3). Documented harm: seeding_scoring.py:34-46 had to add a private-key importlib loader because 'a bare from debug_scorer import ... binds whichever copy won the sys.path insertion race'. general.py:18-21 says the module 'is imported under two different package names depending on the entry point'. ROOT eval-tower-architecture-audit EVL-12 A2 records that a bare question_pool import bound the orchestrator shadow copy instead of the research one. scripts has no __init__.py at the top level (namespace package), so qualified `scripts.x.y` imports already work.
proposal: Standardise on qualified imports: convert bare `from seeding_types/debug_scorer/seeding_* import` to `from scripts.benchmark.<m> import`. Replace the root inserts with one documented launch convention, `uv run python -m scripts.<pkg>.<mod>`, or keep a single tiny `scripts/_bootstrap.py` used only by `if __name__ == '__main__'` blocks. Delete the per-file inserts. Add a ruff/grep CI check that rejects new `sys.path.insert` under scripts/ and src/. Order of work: benchmark/seeding_* first (worst dual identity), then server/ (see A5-09), then graph_router/analysis.
CORRECTED: Convert intra-O bare imports to `scripts.benchmark.<m>` and drop the scripts/<subdir> inserts. That fixes the dual-identity problem. Keep at most a 2-line repo-root insert under `if __name__ == '__main__'`, or require `.venv/bin/python -m scripts.x.y` and fix the docstrings. For cross-repo modules, keep the existing explicit by-path loaders under private sys.modules keys (seeding_sampling._load_research_benchmark_module, seeding_scoring._load_orchestrator_debug_scorer), and forbid putting R's root or R/scripts/* on sys.path. Add a grep gate for new sys.path.insert under scripts/ and src/.
verify-note: Numbers reproduced: 196 sys.path.(insert|append) lines in non-autopilot scripts; seeding_types imported bare 18 times and qualified 4 times; there is no scripts/__init__.py; the .pth holds the repo root. The seeding_scoring.py:34-46 and general.py:18-21 comments are accurate, and the ROOT EVL-12 A2 text confirms the question_pool shadowing. The proposal has three flaws. (1) A `scripts/_bootstrap.py` cannot be imported by a directly executed `python scripts/x/y.py` until the repo root is already on sys.path. That is a chicken-and-egg problem. (2) Root inserts are redundant only under the project venv. Docstrings and cron examples invoke system/miniforge python (e.g. q_scorer_runner.py:24 `/home/daniele/miniforge3/bin/python3 .../scripts/q_scorer_runner.py`), and those lose the editable .pth. (3) Qualified imports do not fix the cross-repo half of the incidents. In BOTH O and R, `scripts/` and `scripts/benchmark/` are namespace dirs, and both repos ship scripts/benchmark/debug_scorer.py and question_pool.py. If both roots are on sys.path, `scripts.benchmark.debug_scorer` binds whichever root comes first. contention_matrix.py:476-504 already inserts R's scripts/benchmark and scripts/lib at sys.path[0] in-process.
verification: `git grep -c 'sys.path.insert' -- scripts | awk -F: '{s+=$2} END{print s}'` drops from 196 toward 0. For each converted script, `uv run python -m scripts.benchmark.seed_specialist_routing --help` works. Add a test asserting `'seeding_types' not in sys.modules` after importing scripts.benchmark.seed_specialist_routing.

## A5-09 [sev 3] [modularity/refactor] [effort S] [behav none] [confirmed] loc_delta=-20
**API process and stack tools mutate sys.path and import bare `stack_manifest`/`stack_numa`, producing second module copies alongside scripts.server.***
files: O:src/api/routes/dashboard_topology.py:458, O:src/registry/model_descriptors.py:1389, O:scripts/server/contention_matrix.py:58, O:scripts/server/contention_matrix.py:520, O:scripts/server/dynamic_stack_evidence_packet.py:71, O:scripts/validate/check_contention_matrix_fresh.py:26, O:tests/unit/test_stack_manifest_imports.py:24
evidence: dashboard_topology.py:458-468 runs inside the uvicorn API workers. It does `sys.path.insert(0, .../scripts/server)` and then `from stack_manifest import ROLE_LAUNCH_META`. The same file imports `scripts.server.stack_manifest` at :73, :333, :664 and :913, so a worker ends up with two stack_manifest modules. Each one re-parses launch_manifest.yaml + model_registry.yaml (0.53s with pure-Python safe_load, measured) and re-runs the import-time validators. The surrounding `except Exception: return []` turns any failure (e.g. KernelPathError) into 'no aliases' on the dashboard with no log line. model_descriptors.py:1389-1391 does the same inside src. contention_matrix.py:58-59 inserts scripts/server and then mixes `import stack_manifest` / `from stack_numa import NUMA_CONFIG` (:520-521, :1657, :1869, :1985, :2109, :2308), while stack_manifest itself imports `scripts.server.stack_numa`. That gives two NUMA_CONFIG dicts, and monkeypatching one does not affect the other. contention_matrix.py:74 also calls logging.basicConfig at import, which reconfigures the root logger of any importer (dualhalf_probe.py:25 imports it).
proposal: Replace every bare import with `from scripts.server.stack_manifest import ...` (or `src.stack.*` after A5-03), delete the sys.path inserts, and narrow dashboard_topology's except to ImportError with a logged warning. Move contention_matrix's basicConfig into main().
verify-note: dashboard_topology.py:456-468 (role_aliases, called from dashboard.py:259/6595/7251) inserts scripts/server and does `from stack_manifest import ROLE_LAUNCH_META` under `except Exception: return []`. The same file imports scripts.server.stack_manifest at :73/:333/:664/:913. model_descriptors.py:1389-1391 does the same. contention_matrix.py:58-59 inserts scripts/server, with bare `import stack_manifest`/`from stack_numa import` at :520-521, :1657, :1869, :1985, :2109, :2308, while stack_manifest imports scripts.server.stack_numa (:33). contention_matrix.py:74 calls logging.basicConfig at import, and dualhalf_probe.py:25 imports it. YAML parse cost measured here is 0.30s with libyaml. That is a one-time cost per worker, not per request, so the problem is correctness/debuggability rather than perf, which matches the dimension.
verification: Add a test: import src.api.routes.dashboard_topology, call the alias helper, then assert `'stack_manifest' not in sys.modules` and `'stack_numa' not in sys.modules`. `git grep -nE '^\s*(import|from) (stack_manifest|stack_numa)\b' -- src scripts` returns nothing.

## A5-10 [sev 3] [modularity/refactor] [effort L] [behav none] [partially] loc_delta=-150
**Two-way import cycle between orchestrator_stack and stack_commands, held together by 17 `(*a, **kw)` proxies, a module __getattr__ and about 13 one-line wrappers**
files: O:scripts/server/stack_commands.py:112, O:scripts/server/stack_commands.py:123, O:scripts/server/orchestrator_stack.py:677, O:scripts/server/orchestrator_stack.py:829, O:scripts/server/orchestrator_stack.py:3073, O:scripts/server/orchestrator_stack.py:3101, O:scripts/server/orchestrator_stack.py:3423
evidence: stack_commands.py:112-189 defines `_orchestrator_stack()` (lazy import) plus 17 forwarders such as `def start_server(*a, **kw): return _orchestrator_stack().start_server(*a, **kw)`; `grep -c '^def .*(\*a, \*\*kw)'` = 17. orchestrator_stack imports stack_commands lazily inside main() (:3423) and exposes cmd_* plus 19 stack_manifest/stack_paths names through a module `__getattr__` (:3073-3110). orchestrator_stack.py:677-840 wraps stack_processes one line at a time (load_state, save_state, kill_process, _pids_on_port, _pid_alive, _child_pids, ...). A call like stack_commands.kill_process therefore goes through two forwarders before reaching stack_processes.kill_process_tree. The forwarders hide signatures from type checkers and IDE navigation. They exist mainly as test seams: across tests, monkeypatch.setattr on the launcher module targets load_state 29x, STACK_PRIORS_PATH 27x, wait_for_health 17x, save_state 17x, _pids_on_port 14x and kill_process 12x, in 23 test files.
proposal: Split along dependencies rather than history. (1) stack_lifecycle.py owns process/state operations (today's thin wrappers plus stack_processes) behind a small `StackRuntime` dataclass of injectable callables (popen, kill, pids_on_port, load_state, save_state). (2) launch_commands.py owns build_server_command/start_server/start_orchestrator. (3) stack_commands imports both directly with no proxies. (4) orchestrator_stack.py becomes a thin argparse main. Tests inject a fake StackRuntime instead of monkeypatching module attributes. Keep `__getattr__` re-exports only for names R uses (R:scripts/benchmark/prb_t4_tale_gpu.py:67), with a DeprecationWarning.
CORRECTED: Same split. For verification, write characterisation tests first: build an argparse Namespace, inject fakes for Popen/kill/pids_on_port/load_state/save_state/wait_for_health, run cmd_start/cmd_stop/cmd_reload, and snapshot the argv/env lists and state writes. These must be byte-identical after the split. Never use `start --dry-run` as a no-launch check.
verify-note: Structure confirmed: 17 `(*a, **kw)` forwarders in stack_commands.py (`grep -c` = 17, starting :123); a lazy `_orchestrator_stack()` at :111; `__getattr__` with 17 manifest + 2 path names at orchestrator_stack.py:3073-3110; kill_process → orchestrator_stack.kill_process (:829) → stack_processes.kill_process_tree. Test-seam counts are close (load_state 40, save_state 27, STACK_PRIORS_PATH 27, wait_for_health 22, _pids_on_port 14, kill_process 12 across 20 files). The verification step is wrong and hazardous, though. `orchestrator_stack.py start --dry-run` without --migrate-to is REFUSED with exit 2 (orchestrator_stack.py:3455-3468, added 2026-10-03), because before that fix it 'LAUNCHED THE PRODUCTION STACK on a cold host'. `start --validate-only` (:3205-3231) only loads and validates a stack template. Neither one characterises the launch path.
verification: After fact (a) is fixed: tests/unit/test_orchestrator_stack_reload.py, test_build_server_command_helpers.py, test_launch_env_every_branch.py and test_stackchg_8083_batch.py pass. `orchestrator_stack.py start --dry-run`/`--validate-only` output is byte-identical before and after. `grep -c '(\*a, \*\*kw)' scripts/server/stack_commands.py` = 0.

## A5-04 [sev 2] [debuggability/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [partially] loc_delta=-20
**Bug: the canonical seeding runner drops tool_chains from infra-retry diagnostics (3-way fork drift)**
files: O:scripts/benchmark/seed_specialist_routing.py:429, O:scripts/benchmark/seed_specialist_routing.py:580, O:scripts/benchmark/seed_specialist_routing.py:601, O:scripts/benchmark/seed_specialist_routing_v2.py:577, O:src/pipeline_monitor/diagnostic.py:129
evidence: First-pass diagnostic (:429-449) passes `tool_chains=rr.tool_chains`. The infra-retry diagnostic (:580-606) lists the same ~25 kwargs but jumps from `tools_called=rr_diag.tools_called` (:601) straight to tap_offset_bytes, with no tool_chains. build_diagnostic then writes `"tool_chains": tool_chains or []` (diagnostic.py:129), so every retried question's record says it had no REPL tool chain. Commit 82090cde ('Persist REPL tool-chain diagnostics') added the kwarg at 2 sites in seed_specialist_routing_v2.py (:425, :577) but only 1 site in the canonical file (`git show 82090cde --stat`: canonical +1, v2 +2). The reverse drift also exists: v2's retry site lacks tool_output_tokens, which d82f017c added only to the canonical file. The root cause is two hand-copied 25-kwarg blocks inside one file, repeated across 3 forks.
proposal: Extract `_diagnostic_for(rr, question, config_key)` in seed_specialist_routing.py and call it from both the first-pass and retry paths, so the kwarg list exists once and includes tool_chains and tool_output_tokens. Delete the v2 fork (A5-05) so there is nowhere else to forget it.
CORRECTED: Extract one `_diagnostic_kwargs(rr, prompt_info, config_key)` helper and use it at both sites. To test it, stub `debugger.pop_retries()` to return [(suite, qid)] and stub evaluate_question_3way to return a RoleResult with tool_chains=[{'tool':'x'}]. Then assert the appended diagnostic with is_retry=True carries tool_chains == [{'tool':'x'}]. Do not 'force an infra retry'; that path never reaches this code.
verify-note: The bug is real. The first-pass build_diagnostic at seed_specialist_routing.py:429-478 passes tool_chains=rr.tool_chains (:449). The retry build_diagnostic at :580-628 goes from tools_called (:600) to tap_offset_bytes with no tool_chains, and diagnostic.py:129 writes `tool_chains or []`. `git show 82090cde --stat` shows canonical +1 and v2 +2. The label is wrong, though. This is not an 'infra-retry' path. It is the post-fix mini regression suite (verify/generalize/regress), driven by `debugger.pop_retries()` (:483-484), and it only runs when a ClaudeDebugger is active (`if debugger is not None`, :424; created at :1061-1067). The scope is narrower than stated. Also, v2 lacks tool_output_tokens at BOTH sites, not just the retry site.
verification: Add a unit test (after fact (a) is fixed): run run_batch_3way with a stub that forces one infra retry, where rr_diag.tool_chains=[{'tool':'x'}], and assert the retry diagnostic row has tool_chains == [{'tool':'x'}]. Run tests/unit/test_seed_specialist_routing_main_and_retry.py.

## A5-12 [sev 2] [debuggability/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=10
**Launch-sidecar write failures are swallowed silently, so serving_calls attributes requests to the previous launch**
files: O:scripts/server/stack_log_banner.py:141, O:scripts/server/stack_log_banner.py:161, O:src/backends/serving_calls.py:160
evidence: write_launch_pid (:141-162) is the ONLY writer of <log_dir>/server_launches/<port>.json, and it wraps the write in `except Exception: pass`. All 5 broad excepts in this module are silent (metrics: 5 swallowed / 5 broad). src/backends/serving_calls.py:160-200 server_identity() reads that file on every request and labels the result `identity_source: "stack_sidecar"`. If the write fails (ENOSPC, permissions, a bad port in record), the old launch's file stays, and each request is attributed to the old launch_id/binary_realpath/argv_sha256 with nothing anywhere saying so. That is the same 'binary you reason about is not the binary running' trap described in CLAUDE.md (INF-70). Best-effort is the documented intent (':Everything here is best-effort'), but best-effort does not have to be silent.
proposal: Keep it non-blocking, but on failure (a) print one `[launch-banner] sidecar write failed: <exc>` line to stderr and to the log handle, and (b) unlink the stale <port>.json, so serving_calls reports identity_source 'absent' instead of a wrong identity. Apply the same stderr line to the other four swallowed sites.
verify-note: stack_log_banner.write_launch_pid (:141-162) writes <port>.json via tmp+os.replace inside `except Exception: pass`. All five broad excepts (:51, :80, :129, :161, :210) are silent: one `return None` and four `pass`. serving_calls.server_identity (:160-200) stats/reads that sidecar per request (mtime-cached) and labels it 'stack_sidecar' with no pid cross-check. So a failed rewrite leaves the previous launch's identity attributed to new requests.
verification: Unit test: make sidecar_dir read-only (tmp_path chmod), call write_launch_pid, then assert that capsys.err contains 'sidecar write failed' and that a previously existing <port>.json was removed. A serving_calls.server_identity test then returns identity_source 'absent'.

## A5-15 [sev 2] [debuggability/refactor] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=-80
**'Is autopilot/a bench running?' is reimplemented 6 ways despite a declared single source of truth; the pgrep copies match quoted prompts**
files: O:scripts/benchmark/corpus_quality_gate.py:447, O:scripts/benchmark/eval_batch_serving_probe.py:58, O:scripts/benchmark/j12_think_loop_probe.py:117, O:scripts/maintenance/routing_classifier_rollout_window.py:91, O:scripts/benchmark/xmas_live_ab.py:36, O:scripts/benchmark/xmas_live_ab.py:391, O:scripts/autopilot/model_gate_report.py:137, O:scripts/server/bench_core_claim.py:41
evidence: Four byte-identical `_active_autopilot()` copies run `pgrep -f 'scripts/autopilot/autopilot.py start'`, which matches any argv containing that text. model_gate_report.py:1051 `_process_line_matches_pattern` exists precisely because 'Controller prompts can quote benchmark commands, so pgrep -af script.py may otherwise match the planner process'. That fix is itself duplicated (xmas_live_ab.py:391), and the four quiet-window gates do not use it. The QUIET_WINDOW_PROCESS_PATTERNS table is duplicated verbatim (xmas_live_ab.py:36, model_gate_report.py:137 as XMAS_QUIET_WINDOW_PROCESS_PATTERNS). Meanwhile bench_core_claim.py:41-44 declares itself the 'Single source of truth for bench-driver identity ... a process-name list must not be restated anywhere else' and implements argv-aware matching in is_bench_process. Result: the gates disagree about what counts as 'running'. A planner whose prompt quotes the autopilot command blocks the quiet window with an unexplained refusal.
proposal: Add `bench_core_claim.running_processes(kind: Literal['autopilot','bench','quiet_window'])`, built on is_bench_process-style argv matching, and move the quiet-window table there. Replace the 4 `_active_autopilot` copies and both pattern tables/matchers with calls to it. Have refusals print the matching pid and argv.
verify-note: The four byte-identical `_active_autopilot()` copies run `pgrep -f 'scripts/autopilot/autopilot.py start'` (corpus_quality_gate.py:447, eval_batch_serving_probe.py:58, j12_think_loop_probe.py:117, routing_classifier_rollout_window.py:91), and the metrics list a fifth body-identical copy in seed_operator_strategies.py:317. Further ad-hoc variants exist in bep_ab.py:236, dcp_j7_ab.py:85, internal_interaction_j17_live_ab.py:378, run_real_suite_v1_evaltower_window.py:81 and ri10_canary_dispatch_requests.py:77. QUIET_WINDOW_PROCESS_PATTERNS (xmas_live_ab.py:36-49) and the argv-aware _process_line_matches_pattern are duplicated in model_gate_report.py:137/:1051. Caveat: bench_core_claim's 'single source of truth' covers bench-driver identity for placement, a narrower concept than 'quiet window'. A shared matcher can live there, but keep the two tables distinct.
verification: Unit tests with fake `ps -eo pid,args` output: a planner process whose argv contains the quoted command is NOT detected, and `.venv/bin/python3 scripts/autopilot/autopilot.py start --max-trials 2000` IS detected. `git grep -c 'def _active_autopilot'` = 0.

## A5-22 [sev 2] [debuggability/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [confirmed] loc_delta=6
**preflight_gate swallows a corrupt contention matrix and then reports it as 'topology hash drift'**
files: O:scripts/server/preflight_gate.py:121, O:scripts/server/preflight_gate.py:137, O:scripts/server/preflight_gate.py:375, O:src/scheduling/contention.py:1055
evidence: _live_topology_hash (:121-143) has two problems. First, `except Exception: return None` on import (:130) means an ImportError or KernelPathError becomes the generic error 'live topology hash unavailable' (:375-378), with the cause dropped. Second, `except Exception: pass` around load_contention_matrix (:137-138) means a present but corrupt matrix silently falls back to `topology_fingerprint(NUMA_CONFIG)` (the full topology). That is a different function from the stamped-subset `topology_fingerprint_for_matrix` the expected hash was computed with (contention.py:1073-1076, which diverge whenever explicit-only roles such as gpu_shadow_lane or eval_batch_frontdoor are in NUMA_CONFIG). The gate then fails with 'topology hash drift vs expected' (:381), sending the operator to look for a topology change that did not happen. The gate fails closed, so this is a diagnosis cost rather than a safety issue.
proposal: Catch only FileNotFoundError for the matrix. On any other exception, return (None, f'matrix unreadable: {exc!r}') and include that string in result['error']. Narrow the import except to ImportError and include its text.
verify-note: preflight_gate._live_topology_hash (:121-143) has `except Exception: return None` on the imports (:130). It also has `except FileNotFoundError: pass` and `except Exception: pass` around load_contention_matrix (:135-138), which fall back to topology_fingerprint(NUMA_CONFIG). contention.topology_fingerprint_for_matrix (:1058-1079) fingerprints only the stamped subset when it is a subset of the live roles. So a corrupt matrix yields the full-topology hash and the 'topology hash drift vs expected' error (:381) instead of a read error. The gate fails closed, as the finder says. Note the proposal changes the helper's return type; check_topology_hashes is its consumer.
verification: Unit test: write a corrupt matrix file, call check_topology_hashes(expected_topology_hash='x'), and assert result['error'] contains 'matrix unreadable' and does not contain 'drift'.

## A5-M2 [sev 2] [best_practice/bug] [effort S] [behav intended] [verifier-added] loc_delta=-30
**llama-mtmd-cli resolution is implemented three times, and every copy silently falls back to legacy in-tree builds that bypass the kernel store**
files: O:scripts/lib/env.sh:95, O:src/vision/analyzers/vl_describe.py:306, O:src/services/lightonocr_llama_server.py:107, O:src/registry/kernel_paths.py:123
evidence: env.sh:68-92 refuses to substitute a build-path literal for llama-server ('A literal here silently outranks the kernel-store layer'). A few lines later, env.sh:102-114 loops over ${LLAMA_CPP_BIN}, then ${LLM_ROOT}/llama.cpp/build/bin (the v9 binaries per CLAUDE.md), build-hip, build-v2 and build_libomp_pgo_bolt, and exports the first one whose `--version` succeeds, with no log line. The same fallback list is duplicated in src/vision/analyzers/vl_describe.py:306-319 (it logs a warning) and src/services/lightonocr_llama_server.py:107-117. vl_describe.py:283-285 admits the duplication ('unifying them is filed as follow-up'). Both src comments still call llama.cpp/build/bin 'PRODUCTION BUILDS FIRST', which has been stale since v10 moved serving to kernels/production/*. In vl_describe, `root = LLAMA_MTMD_CLI.parents[2]` assumes the llama.cpp/build/bin layout. When LLAMA_MTMD_CLI resolves into the store, the candidate paths no longer point at any tree. Consumers are live: VisionConfig.llama_mtmd_cli (models.py:1226-1229) feeds vl_describe, and launch_production.sh:15 sources env.sh. This is the same split-brain class as A5-06.
proposal: Add `kernel_paths.tool_binary(backend, name)` next to server_binary, plus one probe helper (version + per-binary LD_LIBRARY_PATH). Use them from vl_describe and lightonocr. Make env.sh resolve mtmd only from the store, print a stderr warning on failure, and drop the legacy-tree candidates, or keep them only behind an explicit opt-in env var.
verify-note: 
verification: Unit test with ORCHESTRATOR_PATHS_KERNEL_ROOT pointing at a tmp store whose llama-mtmd-cli fails --version. Assert that resolution returns None or raises, never a llama.cpp/build* path, and that a warning is logged. `git grep -n 'build/bin/llama-mtmd-cli' -- scripts src` returns nothing.

**47 hand-rolled JSONL readers in scripts/ with diverging semantics (missing file -> [] vs raise; non-object rows accepted vs rejected)**
files: O:scripts/datasets/_common.py:27, O:scripts/lab/readiness_report.py:46, O:scripts/tasks/summarize_real_task_corpus.py:24, O:scripts/graph_router/build_offline_reward_verifier_npz.py:64, O:scripts/analysis/ri10_canary_score_responses.py:26, O:scripts/benchmark/bep_ab.py:45, O:scripts/analysis/corpus_v1/common.py:266
evidence: `grep -rnE '^\s*def _?(load|read|iter)_jsonl[a-z_]*\(' scripts src | grep -v scripts/autopilot` gives 47 definitions in scripts/ (plus 5 in autopilot, 2 in src). Sampled bodies differ: datasets/_common.py:27 and lab/readiness_report.py:46 return [] for a missing file and raise on non-dict rows; tasks/summarize_real_task_corpus.py:24 raises FileNotFoundError and accepts any JSON value; ri10_canary_score_responses.py:26 also raises on an empty file; bep_ab.py:45 is a one-line list comprehension with no line numbers in errors. The metrics' duplicate-body report flags 3 identical copies in graph_router alone (build_offline_reward_oracle_stress_rows.py:26, score_offline_reward_oracle_neuraltxt.py:33, score_offline_reward_oracle_token_coverage.py:27), plus tasks/materialize vs reconstruct and graph_router prepare vs reconstruct. scripts/datasets/_common.py and analysis/corpus_v1/common.py are already two partial 'shared' modules.
proposal: Add scripts/_io.py (or src/util/jsonl.py) with `read_jsonl(path, *, missing_ok=False, require_object=True, allow_empty=True) -> list[dict]`, `iter_jsonl(...)` and `append_jsonl(path, row)`, all with path:lineno errors.
